# Knowledge Base Log

Chronological record of wiki maintenance sessions. Each entry records what was done.

## [2026-07-27] maintenance | Harvest 17 observations — three defect classes found by string scans, not by the reviewer (887 no-pipe wrappers, 18,272 nakaguro bullets, a four-months-corrupt conjugation table)

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2 — `wiki` mode (scheduler: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 21 unharvested-observation-lines signal)

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` found **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep via MCP. Branch level with `origin/main`. Lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-1gmnrx`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 spend this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown + one JSON index only, `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:
- **[F] Harvest** — all **17** loose observations from the 2026-07-27 polish (00908–07441 priority lane / 06650–06655 frontier), systemic-fix (22000–23499) and accuracy-review (19701–19950) runs, filed and cleared. New **Cleanup P28** (mixed `・` vs `- ` bullets in `notes`, measured this session at **18,272 line-initial instances across 2,524 entries**); new **Tooling items 38–40** (`review_accuracy.py --ids` destroying the §A review record; a cross-reference-pair tag-consistency check; the conjugation-vs-headword invariant behind 22070 走り続ける). Updates to Cleanup P17/P20/P21 and Tooling 6/8/11/19/20/23/24/34. Two **Entry Follow-ups** (03794/12672 悪口 variant-reading consolidation; 08169/17946 中火, where one entry carries the reading of a different word).
- **[H] Metrics trend — twenty-sixth refresh** of `topics/quality-metrics.md` (16 new metrics lines, 353→369 runs / ~9,923 adjudicated flags). The 26-run escalation-free window ends at **2** flags — both single judgment calls, not a tail — while `tags` apply-among-decided climbs to **71.1%** (413/168) on the 19701–19950 off-vocab cohort (**49.6% of entries** carrying an off-list tag), furigana screening holds at **2.1%** (3/143, with one 102-entry screen returning **0 applicable**), the review queue sets a new floor at **12,352**, and OpenRouter's daily peak nearly doubles to **$0.86** (still 17% of cap).
- **[A] Sync** — `project/overview.md` counts refreshed (29,955 entries / 27,152 general).
- **[E] Lint** — re-verified the recurring `formality_variant` claim: it occurs in exactly one file (`prompts/comprehensive_polish.md:107`), **not** in `CLAUDE.md`; the misattribution has now propagated through the observation stream for two cycles and is stated flatly in Tooling item 34.
- **Queue sync** — `ideas/backlog-queue.json` gained `furigana-no-pipe-span` (needs-detector, scope 887) and `notes-bullet-nakaguro` (needs-decision, scope 18,272); `updated` → 2026-07-27.

**Editorial theme**: the second consecutive refresh whose most consequential finding sits outside both ledgers. 22070 走り続ける shipped with all 33 conjugated forms doubled (`走り続けるる`, `走り続けるます`) and **validated cleanly for nearly four months**; 887 no-pipe brace spans render as literal curly braces on the live site and are invisible to the furigana detector; the 19701–19950 off-vocab tags that produced this window's high `tags` precision are decidable by set membership without any model call. Recorded on the metrics page as a maintenance principle — *the external reviewer is a good instrument for judgments and a poor, expensive one for facts* — with the corresponding deterministic checks filed as Tooling 6, 8, 11, 24, 39, 40.

**Routed to the curator**: the `comprehensive_polish.md:107` schema-invalid cross-reference types (fourth cycle); the exact-headings addition to the `vocabulary-notes` skill (Tooling 20); and both Entry Follow-ups, each turning on a reading/headword decision that changes an entry's identity.

## [2026-07-26] maintenance | Harvest 29 observations — two long-standing diagnoses turned into measurements (292 dead inline links; the notes scorer mis-scoring 6,351 entries)

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2 — `wiki` mode (scheduler: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 33 unharvested-observation-lines signal)

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` found **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep via MCP. Branch level with `origin/main`. Lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-4wpwyh`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 spend this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown-only changes).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvest** — all **29** loose observations from three 2026-07-26 routine polish runs, the 2026-07-25 new-entries run, two accuracy-review sweeps (18346–18652, 18653–19200), and a 21000–21999 systemic-fix sweep. The distinguishing feature of this harvest is that two items were **promoted from repeated anecdote to measurement** by running the scans they implied, rather than filed as further reinforcements:

  - **Dead inline links — the scan [Tooling 11](ideas/tooling-backlog.md#11-inline-link-target-id-resolution-gate-in-validatepy-or-pre-commitci) has been requesting since 2026-06-10.** Two runs had just reported writing bad target IDs that `validate.py` passed clean (10 and 2). Dictionary-wide: **292 dead links / 144 distinct bad IDs / 160 entries** out of 262,189 links (0.11%). **217 (74%) resolve to exactly one candidate** in `word_id_lookup.json` — unambiguous 1:1 repairs; 74 are ambiguous homographs; 1 (逆転する) has no target. Two things only a whole-corpus view could show: every occurrence is in **00000–07999**, the inline-link-polished band, so the population is **iatrogenic — created by polishing, not by entry creation**; and the failure mode is **number invention, not word confusion** (`00433_miru`→`00283_miru` — the romaji is right, the digits are guessed), which is precisely why no semantic check catches it. Filed as **new Cleanup P27**, queued `blocked` on the gate, because repairing before the gate ships would simply refill.
  - **Notes-scorer bug #1, quantified.** `score_note_quality.py`'s `has_bare_kanji` counts inline-link *base forms* as bare kanji: **6,351 of the 6,555 entries (96.9%)** whose notes contain links lose the 5-point furigana credit — ~21% of the dictionary — and the loss falls hardest on the *most-polished* entries, making `priority/notes.txt` **inverted with respect to link coverage**. That is the mechanical explanation for the ~68-run priority-lane no-op streak recorded in [Tooling 20](ideas/tooling-backlog.md#20-notes-priority-ranking-excludes-recently-polished--structurally-passing-entries). With the lane now **5/5 productive** for a second consecutive run past the closed-tier basics, the standing retirement escalation resolves the other way: **fix the one-line scorer bug, keep the lane.**

- **[D] New items** — Cleanup **P27** (dead inline-link target IDs); Tooling **36** (`verify_furigana.py` can't see same-session entries — it resolves through `entries_index.json` instead of the filesystem convention `get_next_id.py` already follows) and **37** (detector + skill rule for copula て-form `で` mislinked to the particle `で`).
- **[C] Deepened existing items with counts** — Tooling **17** (the `general`-too-broad family counted for the first time: **95 instances** in one 548-entry sweep, ~⅔ of all tag-dimension noise), **24** (two more 100%-FP screens, 49/49 and 9/9, and both noise families shown **decidable without a model**), **26** (**9/9** formality FPs where the entry's own REGISTER text already stated the register — prompt fix, else retire the dimension), **21** (first side-by-side throughput numbers, plus a concrete **cursor-desync**: screening killed at 437/548 while accuracy finished, the shared cursor recorded the accuracy endpoint), **23** (candidate purge specified as three judgment-free rules; the seen-in-entry lane measured **self-feeding at ~10 in / 9 out per run** — it holds the pool, it cannot grow it), **8**, **34**; Cleanup **P20** (cohort unbroken to 19200 — **and the model shown to be the wrong detector**: 32 caught of 51 a free set-membership scan found instantly), **P21** (band unbroken ~06150→06649, `・` bullets as a co-occurring template defect, batch-sweep case now strong because the block's vocabulary repeats), **P9** (density **~2× higher above ID 21000**), **P17**, **P18**.
- **[E] Lint and maintain** — marked the **duplicate 気持ち filing** in `entry-followups.md` (two sections for the same pair) and recorded its third report; synced `ideas/backlog-queue.json` (23 items; the new `inline-link-dead-target-id` entry carries the detect snippet, the 217/74/1 split as its `filter`, and `status: blocked` so the selector correctly skips it — verified `routine_next.py --explain` still reports 7 open batch-ready items); refreshed four "Last updated" headers.
- **[A] Sync** — `project/overview.md` counts refreshed (29,935 entries / 27,132 general / ~116,900 examples / ~16,600 cross-references) and the June-2026 dateline advanced.
- **[H] Metrics trend — not due.** 362 lines in `metrics-history.jsonl` against the 353 runs covered by `topics/quality-metrics.md` = **9 new**, below the ≥10 threshold. Deferred to the next `wiki` run.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not touch prompts, skills, entries, or build scripts): the one-line `score_note_quality.py` fix (now the highest-leverage known bug after the link gate); the `validate.py` link-resolution gate that P27 is blocked on; `prompts/comprehensive_polish.md:107`'s two schema-invalid cross-reference types — **third consecutive cycle**, and the 2026-07-25 misattribution to `CLAUDE.md` has now propagated into the observation stream (re-verified false this run: the string appears in that prompt and nowhere else); and two `inline-word-links` skill rules (copula `で`, bound morphemes).

**Index**: no new *pages* — all new content lives in existing pages, so `index.md` needed no change.

  - The **39** (not 44) entries with braced inline-link base forms — re-measured dictionary-wide.

- **[C] Deepened existing items** — Tooling **11** (the nine silent dead links, and the finding that the *documented* link check is the one that verifies nothing), **24** (the screener's noise diagnosed to prompt-builder context truncation via the stray-`)` tell — ~37 of 48 flags), **21** (first side-by-side throughput measurement: ~8 vs ~29 entries/min), **23** (the seen-in-entry logger's missing reading check), **7** (a worked sense-vs-entry-split example); Cleanup **P8**, **P20**, **P21**.

- **[A] Sync** — `project/overview.md` General-tier and total counts refreshed (27,092 / 29,895).
- **[H] Metrics trend — twenty-fifth refresh** of `topics/quality-metrics.md` (12 new lines, 341→353 runs, ~9,133 adjudicated flags): escalation-free window extends to a second consecutive refresh (**26 straight runs, 328–353**), `tags` apply-among-decided **62.0%**, furigana screening **1.1%**, review queue **new floor 12,496**, OpenRouter ~$0.44/day (<9% of cap). The refresh's headline is that **the one metric moving the wrong way is invisible to both ledgers**: dead inline links are not a reviewer flag, so no dimension can see them — filed to Tooling item 11 rather than logged as a `[pattern]` observation that would only round-trip back to the next harvest.

**Two things routed explicitly to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not touch prompts, skills, or entries): the `comprehensive_polish.md:107` invalid-cross-ref-type fix (open since 2026-05-09), and the `ている` scope decision.

## [2026-06-11] curator session | Tag-policy decision recorded; corrected schema-tag-reliability.md; item 14 resolved; Cleanup P20 added

**Session type**: Curator-directed review of the first Routine v2 runs (not a scheduled wiki run)

**Context**: The curator reviewed the first 17 Routine v2 runs and resolved the
semantic-tag source-of-truth contradiction (runs were adjudicating identical
"invalid tag" flags in opposite directions). Decision: **expand, then enforce**
— 30 established-by-usage tags blessed into `VALID_SEMANTIC`; near-duplicates
get a 1:1 migration map; the ~9,000-instance long tail migrates gradually.

**Activities**:
- **Corrected** `topics/schema-tag-reliability.md`: the 2026-06-11 "reviewer
  false positives" analysis had the facts backwards (the reviewer was enforcing
  the documented taxonomy, which its prompt embeds; `schema.json` has no tag
  enum). Replaced with "The tag-vocabulary contradiction and its resolution",
  recording the policy decision and the standing adjudication rule.
- **Resolved** Tooling Backlog item 14 (with premise correction); the shipped
  fix is `review_accuracy.py` prompt v3 + the policy, not adding a list the
  prompt already had.
- **Added** Cleanup Backlog **Priority 20** (out-of-taxonomy tag migration,
  9,036 instances / 7,292 entries) and queued `unknown-semantic-tags` in
  `backlog-queue.json` (priority 9; `tag-sole-general` moved to 10) backed by
  the new `check_tag_drift.py --check unknown-semantic` detector.
- Updated Routine references after `prompts/routine.md` was deleted
  (routine2.md is now the only Routine prompt).

**Note for future runs**: the `tag_drift` detector queue-depth metric jumps
(~4.9k → ~13.9k) because the new unknown-semantic check is included — new
instrument, not a regression.

Added: the GDEX framework (Kilgarriff et al. 2008) formalizing the four Atkins–Rundell criteria into a scorable system with five weighted features (sentence length, word frequency, main-clause position, anaphor avoidance, topic safety); the **encoding vs. decoding example** distinction (Frankenberg-Garcia 2012, 2015) — decoding examples sorted by sense, encoding examples sorted by lexico-grammatical pattern, with the experimental finding that multiple pattern-sorted examples significantly improve production accuracy; the LLM-generated examples research (Patel et al. 2024 NAACL — OxfordEval metric, 80% win rate over Oxford examples; Haider et al. 2024 — quality degradation for lower-resource languages, informativeness as the hardest criterion; Takahashi et al. 2025 — diversity as a key dimension for Japanese L2 examples); Japanese-specific considerations (script-choice register signal, sentence-final expressions as pragmatic encoding, particle-pattern coverage as a critical encoding function, topic–comment structure requiring longer examples); a proper References section with 15 cited works; and six actionable implications for je-dict-1 (encoding-aware polishing, BCCWJ collocation comparison, automated vocabulary control, progressive-complexity enforcement, LLM example risk mitigation, particle-pattern coverage in verb polishing). Added reciprocal links from `collocations.md`, `dictionary-microstructure.md`, and `dictionary-and-machine-translation.md`.
- Updated "Last updated" dates on all modified pages plus `index.md`.

- Added an exact-enum verb-POS guard to `build/add_conjugations.py`, replacing the substring test `if not any('verb' in p for p in ([pos] + pos_tags))`. **Session type**: Manual session (curator-directed; theme = multilingual expansion plan, research-and-exploration phase). Continues the same-day multilingual sessions below. This session picked the threads those sessions flagged as next: the **per-language static-vs-client-side rendering trade-off** the hub repeatedly named as "the one major design question still only sketched" (§6), plus two un-swept pages directly adjacent to it.

**Pre-flight**: Ran `pipeline/sweep-stranded-prs.py` — 0 open PRs, nothing stranded (main progress `next: 05390`).

**Activities**:
- [F/Harvest] Checked `polishing/observations.md` — still fully harvested through 2026-06-05 session 025 (the top-of-file note records this); no new observation blocks appended since. No change to the file, no `[skill]` recommendations arose.
- [D/B] Created **`topics/multilingual-rendering-architecture.md`** — the worked design for the hub's §6 delivery layer, which [Translation Sidecar Design §6](ideas/translation-sidecar-design.md) had explicitly scoped *out* ("the data layer the renderer consumes, not the delivery layer"). Web-researched. Develops: (1) the three delivery options (per-language static / client-side swap / hybrid) on a static host; (2) the **SEO force** — Google Search Central's direct guidance that separate URLs beat cookie/JS swapping, that Googlebot is mostly US-located and sends no `Accept-Language`, that auto-redirection by language is to be avoided, and that subdirectories + generated `hreflang`/`x-default` are the recommended structure (so pure client-side swap is the option Google recommends *against*); (3) the **size force** — grounded in *measured* live numbers (31,454 HTML files, **492 MB**, 17.5 MB `search-index.js`) against the GitHub Pages **1 GB hard published-site limit**, showing full per-language static rendering reaches the ceiling at the *first* additional language and is impossible at the second without mitigation; the same invariant-spine-duplication insight that chose the sidecar over per-language copies; and the search index as its own sub-problem (must split into shared-spine + per-language gloss overlay); (4) the **resolution** — a size-controlled hybrid (per-language static *entry* pages under `/zh-Hans/…` for SEO + a client-side toggle that *navigates* between them and remembers the preference in `localStorage`, never cookie/`Accept-Language` swapping, never auto-redirect — reconciling the curator's "in-browser toggle that persists" intent with Google's separate-URL rule), with three size-control measures and an explicit **hosting decision** the curator must make before language #3 (GitHub Pages caps at ~two static languages; file-count limits on some hosts like Cloudflare Pages' 20k are already exceeded by the English-only site); (5) build-pipeline consequences and a decision record. Sourced (Google Search Central international docs; GitHub Pages limits docs; SEJ/Weglot hreflang; CloudCannon/better-i18n SSG i18n). Design-only.
- [C] **Swept `project/architecture.md`** (un-swept until now) — added an "Implications for multilingual versions" subsection (source layer stays put; build pipeline gains a `--lang` axis; the static-host 1 GB constraint is the binding one with the measured numbers; the staleness obligation joins the maintenance loop) and Related-pages links to the hub, the sidecar design, and the new rendering page. Refreshed its "Last updated" (was 2026-04-13).
- [C] **Swept `research/keigo-honorifics.md`** — added an "Implications for multilingual versions" subsection building on its existing "L1 background effects" content: keigo is the clearest case where note adaptation is **reframing** (same invariant forms, different L1-relative stance — English "foreign concept" framing is *wrong* for a Korean-L1 reader whose 존댓말/반말 is grammaticalized; the kanji bridge is an *advantage* for a Chinese-L1 reader reading 拝見/拝借) rather than the false-friend **addition** species the Chinese brief targets. Named this a **second species of note adaptation** (reframing vs. addition) and noted recommendation #7 inverts from optional aside to first-class per-language content. Linked to the hub and the Chinese brief.
- [A/E] Propagated the rendering design through the hub `ideas/multilingual-dictionary.md`: rewrote the §6 "hybrid is likely best" line to point at the new page; rewrote the §10 "per-language page explosion" risk from "measure before committing" to "*now measured* — binds at the first language"; promoted rendering/hosting into §9 step 1's *early* design lock-in (alongside storage + staleness); rewrote the "Implications" line that called static-vs-client-side "the one major design question still only sketched" (now a worked design), refreshing the still-open future-work list (Korean/Vietnamese briefs, i18n label map, advisor review UI, calibration sample); added the new page to Related pages and refreshed the hub's "Last updated". Records the curator's decision, then develops: (1) **why simplified↔traditional is not a 1:1 transcode** — the one-to-many character merges that make simplified→traditional the *lossy* direction (干→干/乾/幹, 后→后/後, 发→發/髮, 里→里/裡, 面→面/麵), and the mainland/Taiwan **vocabulary** divergence script conversion can't touch (软件/軟體, 信息/資訊, 网络/網路, 视频/影片, 营销/行銷), concluding `zh-Hans`/`zh-Hant` are two *data-layer* languages sharing ~100% of adaptation *content*; (2) the **BCP-47 code space** (`zh-Hans`/`zh-Hant`/`zh-Hant-TW`/`zh-Hant-HK`) and why naming the first tree `zh-Hans` (not bare `zh`) is the entire — and free — forward-compatibility cost; (3) the **assisted-conversion + human-review seeding path** for adding traditional later (OpenCC `s2twp` phrase/region-aware draft → tier-ordered reviewer queue), with the shared-vs-variant-specific table showing the script-neutral brief lets `zh-Hant` inherit all costly editorial content; (4) the **embedded-fragment preservation hazard** (a converter must mask `{…|…}`/`⟦…⟧` spans or it corrupts the Japanese spine; re-run the sidecar preservation check); (5) font/search/UI consequences; (6) a decision record separating what's settled from what stays open (whether `zh-Hant` ever ships; generic vs. `zh-Hant-TW`/`-HK`; staffing). Web-researched and sourced (Wikipedia *Ambiguities in Chinese character simplification*; BYVoid OpenCC configs; toolkk; Laoret). Design-only — modifies no schema/script/entry.

Turns the plan's Option B (sidecar files, §3) + per-field source hashing (§2) into a concrete, critique-able proposal grounded in a fresh re-read of the live `build/schema.json`: (1) an exact invariant-vs-translatable partition keyed to real JSON paths; (2) a concrete sidecar file (flat field-keyed map with per-field provenance, keyed on invariant `examples[].id`/`sense_number` rather than array position, with `examples[].english`→`translation` renaming) using `05000_manjuu`/`zh-Hans`; (3) the six referential-integrity rules a `translation_schema.json` + `validate.py --lang` mode must enforce, including the **"superset-compatible, not equal"** embedded-fragment rule (Chinese D-class notes legitimately *add* Japanese fragments the English never had); (4) the staleness mechanism in full — what is hashed, the NFC+whitespace normalization that prevents cosmetic-edit false positives, the four-state machine (fresh/stale/missing/orphan), the per-language `queue.txt` format, and a `report.py` TRANSLATION COVERAGE block; (5) the furigana/⟦…⟧ preservation contract with extraction regexes (noting the Priority-9 nested-wrapper edge case the extractor must tolerate); (6) the build-time join + **field-level (never entry-level) English fallback** contract; and (7) the open questions it surfaces (particle structured fields lack stable per-element keys; whole-field vs. sub-field `notes` hashing; `src` storage cost; whether English itself migrates to a sidecar). Explicitly design-only — modifies no schema/script/entry.
- [B] Created **`research/llm-translation-quality-japanese-pairs.md`** — the *feasibility* half of the plan's §7 demand × feasibility gate, which the plan deferred to "a calibration sample." Web-researched this session. Establishes that **ja/zh/ko are all high-resource** for current LLMs/NMT (FLORES-200/WMT evidence: Google Translate ≥ GPT-4-turbo ≥ best open LLM Gemma2-9B ≥ NLLB for these pairs; low-resource cliff is far below), that frontier LLMs produce roughly **junior-translator-quality** first drafts on high-resource pairs (degrading resource-rich→resource-poor; the GPT-4-vs-human-translator studies), and — the key caveat for *this* project — that LLMs are documented to be **weakest exactly on cognates/homographs/false friends** (the orthography-semantics bilingual-word-processing study; language-proximity information-loss work; multilingual-MT hallucination work). So aggregate quality is high but per-item risk concentrates on the 同形異義語 content the Chinese brief targets, justifying the brief's deep/advisor routing and the sidecar's fragment post-validation. Includes a per-pair feasibility table (ja↔en/zh/ko/vi/id-th), the reasons published benchmarks still don't substitute for the calibration sample (pedagogical-prose register, fragment preservation, false-friend *addition*, human-edit rate), and a real-source reference list (Lu et al. 2025 arXiv:2502.02481; the GPT-4-vs-human studies 2411.13775/2407.03658; arXiv:2501.09127; arXiv:2506.23340; Guerreiro et al. 2023 TACL; Korean MQM LREC-COLING 2024; JP-TL-Bench 2601.00223). Numbers stated qualitatively where the fetched figures were not reliably precise, per the no-fabrication standard.
- [C] **Swept `research/kanji-learning-dictionaries.md` for multilingual relevance** (Activity 1 from the session brief) — the clearest case in the wiki where the research *inverts* by L1. Added an "Implications for multilingual versions" subsection: for a hanzi-background (Chinese) learner the lookup problem largely dissolves, furigana changes *function* (the reading, not the glyph, is the difficulty), Halpern's English core-meaning gloss becomes redundant-to-harmful and is replaced by a 同形異義語 divergence-flag layer, Chikamatsu's (1996) "false-friend characters" aside becomes the headline content, phonetic-radical awareness transfers but to different sounds, and the kanji-index enrichment roadmap itself re-prioritizes by L1 (reading/false-friend data up, radical/stroke-count aids down). Framed as: the kanji index and furigana are invariant spine, but their pedagogical justification and the translated gloss layer are per-L1 adaptation.
- [E] Kept `ideas/multilingual-dictionary.md` as the hub: added pointers to the new sidecar-design page from §2 (staleness), §3 (storage), §8 (build inventory), and "Implications"; pointers to the new LLM-feasibility page from §4 (model choice) and §7 (feasibility), and updated the "Implications" future-work list (both demand and feasibility halves and the sidecar draft now exist; the static-vs-client-side rendering trade-off is now the main still-only-sketched design question). Added both new pages to `index.md` (Ideas + Research) and refreshed its header note. Added reciprocal back-links from the Chinese brief and the demand page (the two now point at the feasibility page and sidecar design). Refreshed "Last updated" on every modified page.

It expands the seed 同形異義語 table in `ideas/multilingual-dictionary.md` into a sourced, operational reference for the translation pipeline and the human advisor: (1) the structural framing (kanji/hanzi overlap as the whole story; the "homograph barrier"); (2) the 文化庁 (1978) **S/O/D/N** classification (同形同義/同形類義/同形異義/欠落) with the textbook-survey proportions (~67% S, ~4% O) and the operational reading that S is the silent majority needing no warning, D is the headline false-friend class, and O is the under-noticed trap for an automated pipeline; (3) an expanded ~20-row **同形異義語 (D) false-friend table** (手紙/勉強/大丈夫/丈夫/新聞/経理/愛人/汽車/人参/階段/天井/文句/怪我/約束/結構/検討/娘/湯/先生/私) that the Chinese notes should *lead* with; (4) the **O / calque / part-of-speech production-error layer** (scope/register/connotation drift; Sino-Chinese calque coinages; POS mismatch); (5) **L1-specific `common_mistakes` to substitute** (の-overgeneralization from 的 per Chan 2014; transitivity-pair confusion; reading interference; kango over-formality); (6) a **"what to drop from the English notes" table** (gairaigo false-friend warnings, per-character morphemic glosses, "like the English word X" framings — all English-specific); (7) the zh-Hans/zh-Hant script-neutral note; and (8) how the pipeline consumes the brief as a routing signal (D/O headwords → deep/advisor pass). Sourced to 文化庁 1978, 熊・玉岡 2014, Chan 2014, Koda 2005, Mori 1998, Li et al. 2023, plus advisor-verifiable reference lists (Wiktionary/Wikipedia/Fordham 2017). Web-researched this session.

Built from the **Japan Foundation Survey on Japanese-Language Education Abroad 2021** (~3,794,714 learners; confirmed top-10 country counts: China 1,057,318 / Indonesia 711,732 / Korea 470,334 / Australia 415,348 / Thailand 183,957 / Vietnam 169,582 / USA 161,402 / Taiwan 143,632 / Philippines 44,457 / Malaysia 38,129). Key analytic move: re-read country counts by **L1/target language** and drop the already-served English populations (Australia, USA), yielding Chinese ~1.2M (China+Taiwan) ≫ Indonesian ~712K > Korean ~470K. Crossed demand with LLM feasibility to reproduce the plan's ordering with numbers behind it (Chinese first, Korean strong second, Indonesian the high-demand/unknown-feasibility wildcard). Caveats documented (country ≠ L1; cohort-type fit; triennial lag). Sourced to JF 2021 and Nippon.com 2022.

## [2026-06-06] maintenance | Multilingual dictionary plan, observation harvest

**Session type**: Manual session (curator-directed)

**Activities**:
- [D] Created `ideas/multilingual-dictionary.md` — a comprehensive standing plan for extending je-dict-1 from Japanese→English into a Japanese→multilingual learner's dictionary (Chinese first; Korean/Vietnamese/others later, demand- and feasibility-driven). The curator directed this work and supplied the anchor constraints: headwords and example sentences stay identical across all language versions; notes are *largely* the same but with targeted adaptation for false friends, calques, and cultural differences; glosses/definitions/explanations/notes render in the target language; a top-of-page toggle picks the language and the choice persists in the browser. The plan covers: (1) an exact invariant-vs-translated field inventory audited from `build/schema.json` (the schema cleanly isolates target-language fields — `gloss`, `definitions[].gloss/explanation`, `examples[].english`, `notes`, `*_references[].label`, `common_mistakes/particle_contrasts[].explanation` — from the Japanese spine, with `conjugation.forms[].label` and tag display names belonging in shared per-language label maps rather than per-entry); (2) the source-of-truth + staleness problem (English is the pivot; every English polish silently invalidates translations; recommend per-field source hashing + a re-translation queue modeled on `reviews/queue.txt`); (3) three storage options with a recommendation for **sidecar translation files** (`translations/<lang>/…`) over nested per-language fields or full per-language entry copies, to keep the active English workflow and the parallel-agent pipeline decoupled from translation churn; (4) an AI translation pipeline that pivots from the polished English (with the Japanese supplied as context), reusing the `review_runner.py`/queue/two-pass-screening shape and enforcing byte-level preservation of embedded furigana wrappers and `⟦…⟧` inline links; (5) note adaptation by content type, with the Chinese 同形異義語 false-friend table (勉強/手紙/大丈夫/新聞/丈夫/経理) as the seed for a per-language adaptation brief, drawing on `research/l1-transfer-japanese-vocabulary.md`; (6) UI/storage/SEO (localStorage toggle, English fallback, per-language static pages vs. client-side swap, per-language search index, `hreflang`); (7) the simplified-vs-traditional Chinese decision (treat `zh-Hans`/`zh-Hant` as separate codes; resolve with the advisor before building); (8) a build-script change inventory; (9) a de-risking rollout (design lock-in → ~50-entry advisor-reviewed calibration sample → tier-first → UI pilot → scale → second language); and (10) open questions/risks. Added to `index.md` and cross-linked from `ideas/dictionary-growth.md` (the other major expansion axis).

- [B] Researched **input processing, noticing, and depth of processing in L2 vocabulary acquisition** and created `research/input-processing-noticing-vocabulary.md`. Covers Schmidt's (1990, 1994, 2001, 2010) Noticing Hypothesis (input → intake requires conscious registration; strong version: noticing is necessary; weak revision: facilitative); Robinson's (1995) extension connecting attention, memory, and task demands to noticing (understanding > noticing > no awareness for learning outcomes); Leow's (2000, 2015) empirical support via aware-vs-unaware learner studies; Tomlin & Villa's (1994) fine-grained attention decomposition (alertness/orientation/detection — detection as minimal attentional event, productive tension with Schmidt's awareness requirement); Craik & Lockhart's (1972) Levels of Processing framework (shallow orthographic → deep semantic; depth predicts durability of memory trace); Laufer & Hulstijn's (2001) Involvement Load Hypothesis operationalizing depth for vocabulary tasks via three factors (need/search/evaluation — composition > reading+fill-in > reading alone in two-country EFL experiment); Barcroft's (2002, 2004, 2015) TOPRA model showing semantic elaboration can *inhibit* form encoding (+structural outperformed +semantic for L2 form recall — counterintuitive but empirically robust); Sharwood Smith's (1991, 1993) input enhancement framework (external techniques increase salience but don't guarantee intake); VanPatten's (1993, 2004) Input Processing theory (Primacy of Meaning, Lexical Preference predicting content words processed before function words); Lee & Huang's (2008) meta-analysis on textual enhancement; Godfroid, Boers & Housen's (2013) eye-tracking evidence on fixation and vocabulary learning. Seven implications for je-dict-1: entry structure maps to staged processing model (gloss = Stage 1, examples = Stage 2, notes = Stage 2–3); cross-references as distributed evaluation encounters; furigana serving dual attention functions (reducing orientation cost + supporting form-meaning mapping per TOPRA); progressive examples implementing involvement load staging; similar-words sections as high-involvement evaluation tools; dictionary as attention-directing tool not just information repository; particle processing gap predicted by VanPatten's Lexical Preference. Added page to `index.md` and as a related page on `vocabulary-acquisition.md`, `depth-of-vocabulary-knowledge.md`, `incidental-vocabulary-reading.md`, `dictionary-lookup-behavior.md`, `vocabulary-learning-strategies.md`, `example-sentences.md`, `dictionary-skills-training.md`, and `near-synonym-discrimination.md`.
- [A] Updated stats across wiki pages to reflect 2026-06-05 `report.py` output (28,603 entries, 25,820 general, 16,289 cross-references, 0.57 per entry, ~112,771 total examples, 41.1% symmetry with 7,838 asymmetric refs, 1,513 candidates, 5,329 entries with inline links at 18.6%). Updated: `project/overview.md`, `project/vocabulary-tiers.md`, `topics/enhancement-plan-retrospective.md`, `topics/cross-references.md`, `research/sense-relations-semantic-networks.md`. Updated "Last updated" dates on all modified pages plus `index.md`.

the receptive–productive gap (Webb 2005/2008/2009 finding productive vocabulary consistently smaller, gap widening for lower-frequency words, productive learning producing gains across more knowledge aspects; Laufer 1998 estimating 80% ratio for high-frequency dropping to 50% for lower-frequency); Levelt's (1989, 1999) speech production model (conceptualization → formulation → articulation, with lemma selection and lexeme retrieval as the L2 bottleneck); Jiang's (2000) three-stage L2 lexical development showing L1 lemma mediation; six information categories for encoding dictionaries (collocations, syntactic patterns, register constraints, contrastive information, encoding examples, morphological/derivational information; Atkins & Rundell 2008); specialized production dictionaries (BBI Combinatory Dictionary, Oxford/Macmillan Collocations Dictionaries, Laufer & Levitzky-Aviad 2006 "Bilingual Dictionary Plus", Shoji 2012 Common Japanese Collocations); Frankenberg-Garcia's (2012, 2015) distinction between decoding and encoding examples (multiple encoding examples more effective for production, learners prefer examples over explicit rules, examples must exhibit the specific patterns needed); Nesi & Haill's (2002) five-type dictionary-induced error taxonomy in production (wrong entry, misinterpreted information, wrong sense, correct sense rejected, dictionary gap); Japanese-specific encoding challenges (particle selection, transitivity pairs, register selection across wago/kango/gairaigo, aspect/ている production, keigo production); and a detailed mapping of je-dict-1 features to encoding needs with six priorities (particle patterns as highest-impact gap at inconsistent coverage, transitivity at 32%, encoding-oriented example evaluation, register systematization, similar-words expansion, aspect documentation at 17%).

Covers the traditional case for monolingual dictionaries (CLT immersion orthodoxy, the Big Four as quality confound, controlled defining vocabularies); empirical evidence favoring bilingual dictionaries (Laufer & Hadar 1997: bilingual and bilingualized outperformed monolingual at all levels below advanced, N=123; Bogaards 1996: bilingual produced highest retention for translation recall, N=297; Lew 2004: bilingual outperformed monolingual across all task types including productive); the bilingualized dictionary as compromise (Laufer & Melamed 1994: better retention than pure bilingual or monolingual; Laufer & Kimmel 1997: the format accommodates diverse individual learning strategies; Nakamura 2000: Japanese-specific findings); cognitive and psycholinguistic perspectives (Kroll & Stewart 1994 Revised Hierarchical Model showing L2→L1 lexical links stronger than L2→concept links especially at lower proficiency; De Groot 1992/1993 distributed feature model predicting concrete/abstract word differences in translatability; Dijkstra & Van Heuven 2002 and Thierry & Wu 2007 on persistent L1 activation during L2 processing); lookup direction research (L2→L1 decoding vs. L1→L2 encoding, with bilingual dictionaries strongest for decoding); Japanese-specific considerations (linguistic distance amplifying monolingual dictionary inaccessibility, Tono 2001 on strong bilingual preference among Japanese learners, kanji as a reading-access barrier solved by furigana in bilingual dictionaries); the modern consensus (Adamska-Sałaciak 2010 on the teacher orthodoxy being unsupported, Lew 2016 on digital dictionaries blurring boundaries); and detailed implications for je-dict-1's hybrid position (bilingual J→E direction with monolingual-style depth through examples, collocations, and notes). ## [2026-05-12] maintenance | Furigana wrapper anomalies, expanded conjugation-mismatch finding, three backlog updates

**Session type**: Manual session (curator-directed: continue exploration of entry issues from the previous session)

**Activities**:
- [F] Continued the 2026-05-11 entry-exploration thread, this time running stratified audits across the full 27,221-entry set to surface two new actionable patterns:
  - **Conjugation/POS mismatches widened from 12 to 130 entries.** The previous session's "12 onomatopoeia with bad godan conjugations" was a 9.2% subset. The full audit shows 91 adverbs (mostly く-ending forms like 著しく, すごく, ますます, おそらく, あいにく), 31 expressions (反応を見る, 場を和ませる, 頼りにする, …), 5 noun-adverbs (真っ二つ, 多く, 遠く), 2 auxiliaries, and 1 na-adj+adverb — all carrying a conjugation field while their POS tag contains no `verb-*` value. Every one of the 130 carries a stray `verb_class` tag that triggered `add_conjugations.py`. Adverbs ending in く are the cleanest case: the script generates forms like `著しきます` and `すごかない` that are not Japanese.
  - **859 malformed furigana wrappers across 624 unique entries.** Four sub-patterns: 211 with the お-prefix inside the wrapper (`{お酒|おさけ}`), 13 with ご-prefix, 172 pure-kana wrappers (some reversed like `{ところ|所}`), and 463 with okurigana inside the wrapper (`{若い|わかい}`). Of the okurigana subset, **68 are visibly wrong on the live site** because the reading is truncated (`{やり方|かた}` paints `かた` over the entire `やり方`). Confirmed downstream impact: 01525_wakai (basic-tier 若い) is missing its conjugation table on the live site because `add_adjective_conjugations.py` couldn't parse the malformed headword.
- [D] Created `topics/furigana-wrapper-anomalies.md` — substantive new analysis page documenting the four sub-patterns, distribution across headword/examples/notes, three downstream effects (rendering bugs, search/lookup misses, inline-link surface extraction failures), three causal hypotheses (copy-paste from external sources, LLM autocomplete drift, no validator), and a detection sketch for a `check_furigana_format.py` validator. Frames the pattern as the string-level analogue of the tag-drift pattern documented in `schema-tag-reliability.md`: slightly off-spec data parses successfully and accumulates silently because no validator checks it.
- [C] Extended `topics/schema-tag-reliability.md` with the widened conjugation-mismatch finding. Updated the case study from "12 onomatopoeia" to "130 entries across 5 primary-POS categories" with a breakdown table and explicit examples of generated nonsense forms (`著しきます`, `反応を見らない`). Updated the detection-sketch count and cross-referenced the new wrapper-anomalies page. Last-updated date bumped to 2026-05-12.
- [F/A] Updated `ideas/cleanup-backlog.md`: rewrote Priority 6 to cover all 130 conjugation-mismatch entries (was just 12), added the breakdown by primary POS, added a one-liner shell command to extract the full list. Added new Priority 9 for malformed furigana wrappers with the four-sub-pattern breakdown and severity ranking (68 truncated-reading cases highest-priority because they display visibly wrong furigana).
- [F/A] Updated `ideas/tooling-backlog.md`: rewrote item 5 to cover the 130-entry scope (was 12), with explicit guidance on the expression-case ambiguity. Added new item 8 (`check_furigana_format.py` validator with severity-ranked output) and new item 9 (targeted headword fix script for the 22 malformed-headword entries that the format-validator would flag).
- [F/A] Updated `ideas/entry-followups.md`: added 01525_wakai with the concrete repair recipe (fix headword format → re-run conjugation script) and 08261_totonoenaosu (POS mis-tagged as `verb-ichidan` but should be `verb-godan`).   - **Runaway automation**: 12 adverbial onomatopoeia entries (ぐつぐつ, こつこつ, ぱくぱく, etc.) carry full godan conjugation blocks with nonsense forms because a stale `verb_class` tag triggered `add_conjugations.py`. Lists all 12 IDs.
  - **Categorical compression**: the four-bucket `politeness` field (plain/polite/humble/honorific) cannot represent uchi/soto referent orientation (母/お母さん), bikago vs. true honorifics (ご飯, お釣り), or familiar suffixes (〜ちゃん, 〜くん).   - Proposes detection heuristics (mismatched POS/conjugation; politeness tag with no politeness keyword in notes; semantic-tag/gloss keyword mismatch) and frames the broader implication that *tags are write-rarely, read-often* and therefore quietly accumulating debt.

## [2026-05-10] maintenance | JE dictionary history research, stats sync, cross-reference lint

**Session type**: Nightly maintenance

**Activities**:
- [B] Researched **the history of Japanese-English dictionaries** and created `research/je-dictionary-history.md`. Covers the full arc from the 1603 Nippo Jisho (日葡辞書, Jesuit missionaries in Nagasaki, 32,293 entries, first dictionary of Japanese in a European language) through the Dutch interlude (Medhurst's 1830 word list, Hori's 1862 pocket dictionary); the Hepburn era (1867 first edition, 1872 second, 1886 third with "traditional Hepburn" romanization); Brinkley's 1896 Unabridged JE Dictionary (Sanseido, 1,687 pages); the Kenkyusha era (1918 first edition by Takenobu Yoshitarō, 1931 second with George Sansom, wartime pirated editions for Harvard/British military use, 1974 fourth edition, 2003 fifth edition "Green Goddess" with ~480,000 entries); the parallel New English-Japanese Dictionary line (1927 first edition by Okakura, 2002 sixth); character dictionaries (Nelson 1962, Halpern SKIP/core meaning); and the digital revolution (Breen's EDICT 1991, JMdict 1999 XML migration, WWWJDIC mid-1990s, 214,000+ entries by 2023, Creative Commons licensing; Casio/Sharp handheld denshi jisho; Jisho.org, Weblio, Wisdom 2003). Identifies five themes: glossary-to-learner-dictionary evolution, the compiler-type shift (missionary → publisher → scholar → community → LLM), the persistent kanji lookup problem, the quality-accessibility gap between Kenkyusha and JMdict, and je-dict-1's position in the tradition. Cited Hepburn 1867, Brinkley 1896, Kenkyusha 2003, Nelson 1962, Breen 2004.

## [2026-05-05] maintenance | Vocabulary learning strategies research, stats sync, cross-reference lint

**Session type**: Nightly maintenance

**Activities**:
- [B] Researched **vocabulary learning strategies and their implications for dictionary design** and created `research/vocabulary-learning-strategies.md`. Covers the three major VLS taxonomies: Oxford's (1990) SILL (direct/indirect strategies), Schmitt's (1997) 58-strategy taxonomy organized by discovery/consolidation phases (determination, social, memory, cognitive, metacognitive), and Gu & Johnson's (1996) 108-item questionnaire study of 850 Chinese EFL learners showing metacognitive strategies (self-initiation, selective attention) as the strongest positive predictors while visual repetition was the strongest negative predictor. Key strategies analyzed with evidence: guessing from context (effective for receptive knowledge but insufficient alone; requires 95–98% text coverage), dictionary consultation (Luppescu & Day 1993, Nesi 1999, Tono 2001 on common failures), word part analysis/morphological strategy (especially powerful for Japanese kanji compounds per Mori & Nagy 1999), the keyword method (strong short-term but debated long-term effectiveness, Hulstijn 1997), word cards and spaced repetition (Nakata & Webb 2022 synthesis), note-taking (Schmitt & Schmitt 1995), and semantic mapping (the clustering debate: Tinkham 1993/1997 and Waring 1997 showing interference vs. Nation 2000 resolving as introduction-vs-consolidation). Self-regulation dimension via Tseng, Dörnyei & Schmitt's (2006) SRCvoc framework (commitment control, metacognitive control, satiation control, emotion control, environmental control) and Tseng & Schmitt (2008) on motivated vocabulary learning. Mizumoto & Takeuchi's (2009) explicit VLS instruction study with 146 Japanese EFL students. The involvement load hypothesis (Laufer & Hulstijn 2001) connecting strategy choice to retention through need, search, and evaluation components. Japanese-specific considerations (script complexity, L1-specific strategy patterns, dictionary as strategy enabler). Detailed implications for je-dict-1 organized by discovery strategies, consolidation strategies, and self-regulation support, with specific feature-to-strategy mapping tables and five recommendations. Cited Atkinson 1975, Craik & Lockhart 1972, Ebbinghaus 1885, Gu & Johnson 1996, Hoshino 2010, Hulstijn 1997, Ishii 2015, Laufer & Hulstijn 2001, Luppescu & Day 1993, Mizumoto & Takeuchi 2009, Mori & Nagy 1999, Nakata & Webb 2022, Nation 2000/2001, Nesi 1999, Oxford 1990, Schmitt 1997, Schmitt & Schmitt 1995, Tinkham 1993/1997, Tono 2001, Tseng et al. 2006, Tseng & Schmitt 2008, Waring 1997.

Cited Atkins & Rundell 2008, Biber et al. 1999, Cowie 1998, Erman & Warren 2000, Granger & Meunier 2008, Moon 1998, Nesselhauf 2005, Pawley & Syder 1983, Siepmann 2005, Wray 2002, Yamashita & Jiang 2010.

## [2026-04-15] maintenance | Controlled defining vocabulary research, stats sync, tier-system cross-link

**Session type**: Nightly maintenance

**Activities**:
- [B] Researched **controlled defining vocabulary** (CDV) and created `research/controlled-defining-vocabulary.md`. Covers the historical arc from Ogden's Basic English (1930) through West's General Service List (1953) to LDOCE's explicit ~2,000-word defining vocabulary (Procter 1978) and COBUILD's full-sentence definition style (Sinclair 1987); what a CDV accomplishes (non-circularity, reading-level control, stylistic consistency, computational tractability); its criticisms (naturalness trade-offs, intra-CDV circularity, polysemy within defining words, L2-specific pragmatics); treatment in bilingual and Japanese-English dictionaries; and a detailed analysis of how je-dict-1's closed basic+core tiers plus the inline-word-link system function as an analogue of the CDV tradition. Concrete recommendations include keeping the closed-tier policy as a hard constraint, adding an automated self-containment linter, and adopting soft-CDV discipline for the English notes field. Cited Adamska-Sałaciak 2016, Hanks, Herbst 1996, Nation 2001/2013, Ogden 1930, Procter 1978, Rundell 2008, Sinclair 1987, West 1953.
- [C] Expanded `project/vocabulary-tiers.md` self-containment section to explicitly frame the tier system as a CDV analogue and link to the new research page, and added the new page to the vocabulary-tiers related-pages list.
- [A] Refreshed entry counts with end-of-day `report.py` output (23,841 entries / 21,058 general / 7,427 cross-references / 96,585 examples / 2,513 candidates / 45.5% symmetry / 3,463 asymmetric refs) across `project/overview.md`, `project/vocabulary-tiers.md`, `topics/cross-references.md`, `topics/entry-consistency.md`, and the retrospective page. **Session type**: Nightly maintenance

**Activities**:
- [B] Researched **semantic prosody** — the evaluative colouring that attaches to words through habitual collocation — and created `research/semantic-prosody.md`. Covers the Sinclair/Louw/Stubbs/Partington theoretical tradition, distinctions between connotation / semantic preference / semantic prosody, the Hunston/Whitsitt critiques, Japanese examples organised by adverbs (ろくに, なかなか, いちいち, いかにも, わざわざ, せっかく, まさか), verbs and auxiliaries (〜てしまう, benefactives, 〜がる, 〜ぶる), and noun-modifiers (とんでもない, 〜くさい, 〜っぽい). ## [2026-07-28] maintenance | Routine v2 `wiki` — harvest 16 observations; three long-open diagnoses closed

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector reason: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes")

**Activities**:
- **[F] Harvested all 16 loose observations** from the 2026-07-27 routine polish run (00908–07441 priority lane / 06656–06668 frontier), the 2026-07-28 accuracy-review (19951–20450), and the 2026-07-28 polish run. Unusual in that three observations *closed* long-open diagnoses rather than reinforcing them:
  - **Tooling 20 — scorer bug #2's root cause, a substring match.** `normalize_pos()` tests `'verb' in pos.split(',')[0]`, and `"adverb"` contains `"verb"`, so every `adverb, …` entry is scored against the verb template and required to have sections it cannot have (00266_maido stuck at 54 after a full rewrite). With bug #1 this completes the diagnosis of the priority lane's no-op streak — it was fed entries that were already good *and* entries that could never be improved.
  - **Tooling 24 — the screener's FP mechanism located in the source** (the `followed by:` context snippet clipped mid-`{kanji|reading}`; 47/48 flags on 19951–20388, and 7/18 on *brand-new* entries 30165–30175), **and the proposed lint beat the screener 7:1**: a hand-run `[A-Za-z]`-in-reading scan found 8 defects dictionary-wide where the paid screener found 1.
  - **Tooling 11 — the filed gate shown insufficient.** Both new dead links point at *real entries for the wrong word* (何→`00294_motomoto`, でも→`09528_tanaka`), so existence-checking the target ID would not catch them; the complete check is base-form agreement.

## [2026-07-30] maintenance | Routine v2 `wiki` — 21 observations harvested; the four-cycle screener FP family closed; a new synthesis page on instrument vs. corpus defects

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector reason: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 25 unharvested observations, 1,024 candidates, max entry 30,240)

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 21 loose observations** from four runs: the 2026-07-29 wiki harvest's own raised observations, the 2026-07-29 polish and systemic-fix runs, the 2026-07-30 polish run (05xxx onomatopoeia block + 06688–06693 frontier), and the 2026-07-30 accuracy-review (21901–22500). Four observations *closed* items rather than reinforcing them:
  - **Tooling 24's context-snippet half — RESOLVED after four cycles.** `extract_furigana_pairs()` captured only 10 characters of following context and `build_screening_prompt()` rendered it as `(followed by: {協議会|きょうぎか)`. The window almost always cuts inside the next `{kanji|reading}` wrapper, and **the annotation's closing paren then reads as the wrapper's closing brace** — so the model saw a syntactically complete wrapper holding a truncated reading and correctly reported what it was shown. That last step is why three prior descriptions of the bug all missed it: the artifact is a *well-formed* string that means something other than what the entry says. Fixed in both prompts (24-char capture, `trim_context()` cutting to a wrapper boundary, 「」 delimiters, explicit no-infer-truncation instruction). Final pre-fix precision: **0/21** on 21301–21900 and ~0 on the ~16% of 21901–22500 flagged. Recorded consequence: **every screener precision figure in the series predates the fix**, so the standing "retire the screener" argument is suspended, not resolved, until one clean range is screened against the repaired prompt.
  - **Tooling 19 — the implementation site found.** `manage_candidates.py sync` already computes, on every run, the exact set of `(surface, reading)` pairs that just crossed from "no entry" to "has an entry". That set *is* the stale-`noentry` work list, free as a side effect of work the pipeline already does. Splits the item into an incremental hook (closes the source; ~20 words per sync) and the historical entry-major sweep, with the first not waiting on the second.
  - **Tooling 23 — the writable candidate pool reached zero.** The 2026-07-29 new-entries run drained the `seen in entry` pool completely (20 available, 19 created). The `candidates_low` selector signal is now not merely inaccurate but **anti-correlated**: it reads ~1,024 and stays silent exactly when the pool a run can write from is empty. A `source` field would let the selector count the writable pool and bias toward `polish`, which is what replenishes it — closing a feedback loop it currently cannot see.
  - **Tooling 6 — the map's value re-scoped by measurement.** 275 off-vocab tags in 21301–21900 spanned **~100 distinct tag strings**, so most appear once or twice. The head is a lookup table and should be one; the tail is genuinely judgment-dependent and no table written today contains the next range's inventions. Map the head, review the tail, gate the inflow — none substitutes for the others.
- Also filed: **new Tooling 45** (extend the ledger's `n` aggregation from `reject` to `flag` — the recording-side fix for the distortion that made 161 instances of one missing lookup table read as the largest escalation event on record, and the reason `needs_curator.txt` is 102 unreadable lines), **new Tooling 46** (the accuracy reviewer flagged 245 off-vocab tags where a free deterministic scan found 275 — **~11% missed on a class decidable by set membership**; pre-scan and pass the reviewer only the residue), **new Tooling 47** (below), **Tooling 43 update** (okurigana variants enter the candidate queue as new words — 売り上げ vs the existing 04102 売上 — a third failure mode on the same (surface, reading) identity key shared with items 41/43), **Cleanup P13/P21/P9 updates**, **new Cleanup P33**, and an **Entry Follow-up for ば**.
- **Two new defect classes measured by the harvest itself**, both from checking an observation's premise rather than accepting it:
  - **Cleanup P33 — 77 entries** whose notes announce `ONOMATOPOEIA`/`オノマトペ` while `tags.semantic` omits `onomatopoeia`. The observing run supposed this was confined to the 05xxx block; it reaches from 01047 upward, but the run's *cause* held — 19924 ぱらぱら (much later) does carry the tag, so it is a bounded create-era population, not an ongoing leak. Batch-ready systemic-fix: the evidence is inside the entry being changed, so precision should be near 1.0.
  - **Tooling 47 — cross-reference `headword` fields are scanned by no furigana instrument.** Every one (`check_furigana_format.py`, both OpenRouter passes, `find_missing_furigana.py`, `verify_furigana.py`) covers `examples` and `notes` only. Those reference objects carry a redundant `reading`, so each **checks itself**: 869 raw disagreements → 116 fully-annotated → **11 after normalizing `〜`/`～`, `／` and katakana** → **7 genuine defects** (over-wrapped okurigana `{見積|みつも}もり` ×3, `{肩透|かたすか}かし`, `{届|とどけ}け`; missing okurigana `{弾|は}む` for はずむ; unmarked rendaku ぎっくり{腰|こし} for ぎっくりごし). The four misses are one enumerable family (`{面倒|めんどう}くさい` declared めんどくさい, both readings current). Filed with the finding that `check_artifacts.py`'s `missing-target-id` **does** cover the target-less shape a polish run asked about, but a `ref_is_resolvable` filter in front of it suppresses exactly the malformed subclass that matters.
- **[D] Created `topics/instrument-defects.md`** — the run's main content contribution, and the synthesis the 2026-07-30 accuracy-review observation asked for ("second consecutive cycle in which a *the dictionary has systemic errors* signal turned out to be an instrument defect"). Six documented cases (`score_note_quality.py`'s `"adverb"`-contains-`"verb"`; `validate.py`'s check that existed but was wired into one of four code paths; `review_runner.py`'s context snippet; the two found this run; and this run's own link lint), what they share, and the central argument: **as corpus quality rises, P(instrument bug) overtakes P(16% of a swept range is defective), so a flag-rate jump on a polished range is now a tooling hypothesis first** — inverting the reading that was correct a year ago. Six detection heuristics, the cheapest being "check whether the flag's quoted evidence is even well-formed by the project's own rules" (`{分別|ふんべ)` cannot exist in a validating entry, so it did not come from one).
- **[E] Lint and sync.** `backlog-queue.json` resynced against fresh detector runs — **`unknown-semantic` 5,939 → 5,519**, the largest single-refresh drop recorded in that class (the 2026-07-30 accuracy-review migrated 93 off-vocab tags); `semantic-mismatch` 834→845, `sole-general` 3,791→3,790, wrappers 763→762, `concrete-noun-domain-mismatch` 8→9. Two new items added (`tag-onomatopoeia-missing` scope 77, `crossref-headword-reading-disagreement` scope 11). Wiki-wide cross-reference lint: **0 broken links**.
- **[H] Metrics trend NOT due**: 390 metrics lines vs the 383 covered by `topics/quality-metrics.md` — 7 new, below the ≥10 threshold.

**Verification note, and why case 6 is in the new page**: the link lint written for Activity E reported **119**, then **24**, then **7** broken anchors before the true count turned out to be **0** — three independent bugs in its own slug function (path keys resolved inconsistently; runs of spaces collapsed where GitHub maps each separately; en/em dashes converted where GitHub drops them). Acting on iteration 1 would have "fixed" 119 working links into broken ones. What settled it was an anchor already in the repo — `#priority-23-20-entries-2918129200-missing-metadatavocabulary_tier`, whose `2918129200` records a silently-dropped en-dash — i.e. **artifacts already known to work are the calibration set when an instrument disagrees with the corpus.** Written up as case 6 rather than quietly fixed, because it is the page's own thesis reproducing live.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not modify prompts, skills, entries, or build scripts): the §C one-sentence change extending `n` aggregation to `flag` decisions (Tooling 45) — the smallest item on the list and the one that unblocks honest escalation statistics; the off-vocab pre-scan sequencing (46); the `ref-reading-disagreement` sub-check and the `ref_is_resolvable` filter question (47); the `manage_candidates.py sync` stale-`noentry` hook (19); the `source` field on candidates, now with a second justification — the selector's `candidates_low` signal (23); the `pure-kana` subpattern split that would make 258 katakana wrappers mechanically fixable (P9); a `--workers N` and the container image's missing `jsonschema`/`pytest` (21, 42 — still standing, and named in the new page as the common ancestor of several instrument defects); and **ば**, which needs a headword-form decision before it can be written.

**Process note**: three of the four items closed this week were closed by a run *checking an observation's premise* rather than acting on it — the katakana check already existed, the `missing-target-id` check already covered the reported shape, and the link lint's 119 findings were its own. The 2026-07-29 log recorded that filings naming file, line, and replacement text are what move items; this week adds the complement — **verifying the premise before filing is what stops items from being opened in the first place.**

**Activities**:
- **[F] Harvest** — all **19** loose observations from the day's two polish runs (frontier 06694–06697 and 06698–06704), the new-entries run, the accuracy-review run, and this Routine's own §7 CI-gate note, filed and cleared. New **Tooling 48–52**, new **Cleanup P34**, one **Entry Follow-up** (06703 振り切る), updates to Tooling 6/24/44 and Cleanup P11/P13/P21/P24/P31, two field-level traps added to `topics/cross-references.md`, and case **7** added to `topics/instrument-defects.md`.
- **[H] Metrics trend — twenty-eighth refresh** of `topics/quality-metrics.md` (12 new metrics lines, 383→**395 runs** / ~11,642 adjudicated flags). Two `[pattern]` observations logged for the next harvest, the first adverse pair this page has produced in a while.
- **[E] Queue sync** — `ideas/backlog-queue.json`: four new items (`tag-sole-action-verb` 2,085, `crossref-missing-target-id-unlabeled` 5, `notes-duplicate-conjugation-block`, `tag-formality-contradicts-register-note` 6), one duplicate resolved (`link-baseform-braces` → `inline-link-braced-base-form`), one **promotion** (that item, priority 24 → 5).

**Editorial theme — measure the premise, then file.** Four observations arrived carrying a proposed fix. Measuring each against the corpus first (30,049 entries) changed three of them:

| Observation as filed | What the corpus said | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| "A cross-reference with no `target_id` validates cleanly — add `required: [target_id]`" | 64 such refs exist; **59 carry a `label` and are intentional** pointers to entry-less words | The rule is "`target_id` **or** `label`". The proposed one-liner would have broken 59 working refs |
| "Sole-`action` tags are dense in the frontier band" | **2,085 entries** dictionary-wide, ~7% | Not a band observation — the largest tag family after sole-`general`, and a standing lane rather than a sweep |
| "A tag can contradict the entry's own notes — detect it" | **6** entries when anchored to the word's position in the REGISTER line; **1,238** when not | The anchor *is* the check; the loose form is a model's job, not a checker's |
| "Braced base forms in inline links — 37 entries, provably safe" | **36**, and already filed twice (P24, 07-25 and 07-29) | Confirmed — but the finding is about the *backlog*, not the defect |

That last row is the run's most useful non-metric result. A batch-ready, provably-safe, user-visible, 36-entry item has now been discovered by three separate runs and worked by none, because its queue priority (24) sits below anything the `systemic-fix` selector reaches — while each polish run that trips over it pays the discovery cost again. Promoted to priority 5 on the grounds that small safe items should be scheduled *because* they clear in one run, not deprioritised because they are small.

**The metrics refresh's two adverse signals** (both logged as `[pattern]` for the next harvest, because neither has an owner yet): the **review queue is rising for the second consecutive refresh** (12,352 floor → 12,919 → **13,375**, +1,023), the first sustained rise in the series — the Routine's own fixes now dominate the queue's inflow, so "changed since last review" may no longer be the right definition; and **curator escalations are a trend, not an event** — 337 in four days against 2 in the preceding two windows, all off-vocabulary `tags` with no in-list destination, against a human loop that closed three items in the same period. The mitigation is already filed and measured: the reviewer's `tags` dimension named in-list destinations for 34 tags the deterministic map could not resolve, cutting one block's escalation 87→46, so a `--dimensions tags` sweep over the *existing* `needs_curator.txt` backlog should precede any request for hand adjudication (Tooling 6).

**One instrument should probably be retired.** Tooling 24 asked for a post-fix precision measurement of the furigana screener, since every figure on record predated the 2026-07-30 context-snippet fix. It arrived: **0 applied of 29 flags across three consecutive post-fix runs**, including one flag whose body refutes itself (22097 — expected and provided readings identical). And it is no longer cheap: at ~1.9 entries/min against the accuracy pass's ~3.4, it pins the `accuracy-review` cursor to the slower dimension. Zero precision, halved coverage — the item's recommendation is now retire or heavily downsample, with the deterministic replacements it already specifies (non-hiragana-reading lint, grouping/orphan-kana detector, item 47's self-checking cross-reference readings) covering every true-positive class the screener has produced in two months.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not modify prompts, skills, entries, or build scripts): the `vocabulary-notes` skill line — kanji named as objects of discussion in English etymology prose ("the 仮 element") still need furigana wrappers, missed in 4 of 18 new entries; the `cross-reference-entry` skill line — a katakana headword's `reading` must be *transliterated* to hiragana, not copied, since the schema pattern is hiragana-only; the `routine2.md` §7 log-wording change (Tooling 48) — a timed-out run must log "checks not visible within the cap", never "no run was queued"; the schema clause "`target_id` or `label` required" (51); the screener retirement decision (24); and the taxonomy question behind Cleanup P13 — does `VALID_SEMANTIC` want a slot for spatial position, or should `location`/`place`/`position`/`space` keep resolving to `general`?

**Closed with no action**: the variant-okurigana finding (00463 分かる listing its variants as `{解|わ}かる`/`{判|わ}かる`, which render as 解かる/判かる). The reporting run grepped the corpus itself and found **0 remaining** — a one-off, not a family. Recorded because a run that checks its own finding's scope before filing is the process this log has been arguing for.

**Activities**:
- **[F] Harvest** — all **31** loose observations from the 2026-07-31 accuracy-review runs (22213–23100 and 23101–23500/22276–22333), the two 2026-07-31 polish runs, the 2026-07-31 systemic-fix run, the 2026-08-01 polish run, and the previous harvest's own raised notes. Filed and cleared.

- **[E] Queue sync** — `ideas/backlog-queue.json`: four new items, scopes resynced against fresh detector runs (**`unknown-semantic` 5,519 → 5,009**, a second consecutive large drop; `semantic-mismatch` 845→865; `sole-general` 3,790→3,791; wrappers held at 762).
- **[H] Metrics trend NOT due**: 404 metrics lines vs the 399 covered by `topics/quality-metrics.md` — 5 new, below the ≥10 threshold.

**Editorial theme — the same method, and this time it pointed both ways.** The last two harvests established measuring an observation's premise before filing it. Two observations this week each arrived with a proposed backlog item and a scope guess. Measuring them changed both, in opposite directions:

| Observation as filed | What the corpus said | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| "Stale `noentry` markers … estimated scope is large: 5 hits in ~14 entries suggests hundreds dictionary-wide" | **3,797 of 7,320 (52%)** now resolve — but only **2,887** safely; 883 match on a single character or a reading alone | Filed as **Cleanup P35** at ~10× the estimate, *with* the exclusion that makes it safe. The naive version of this sweep would have written 883 wrong links. |
| "The 067xx block was created with zero inline links … a targeted backfill would be cheaper" + "there is no detector for this — suggest `check_link_coverage.py`" | Zero-link is 0–1.2% below ID 06000, 62.7% in 06000–07999 (frontier = 06723), **~100% above 08000** | **Closed with no action.** There is no batch; there is a cliff at the polish frontier. The proposed detector would report "77% of the dictionary," which is the frontier's position restated. |

The second row is the more useful result, because the observation was *reported twice by two runs* and read as a defect both times. `CLAUDE.md` states that inline links are never added at creation time — so an unpolished block having no links is the documented design, and "this looks like a bad creation batch" had inverted the causality. **A band-shaped observation from a six-entry sample is more likely to be a property of the lane than of the entries.** That is the corpus-side twin of `topics/instrument-defects.md`'s argument, and it is now written on the new page as its own heuristic.

**The strategic fact underneath it** is worth more than the item that was avoided: 23,294 entries are unlinked, the frontier lane advances 4–8 entries per run when it meets them, and at that rate it reaches ID 30,000 in roughly two years — during which the dictionary grows by more entries than the lane links. The lane cannot close the gap. The only filed item that attacks the real cost is **Tooling 49**, the read-only link *suggester*, since the measured split on a linking slot is ~90% dictionary lookup and ~10% editorial judgment. Until then, the honest description is that inline links are a feature of the polished below-frontier corpus rather than of the dictionary, and that is a curator decision better made deliberately than by accumulation.

**A defect class that no semantic instrument can see.** The 2026-07-31 systemic-fix run repaired 87 inline links whose targets were outright homophones of the intended word (機能→昨日, 状況→上京, 電気→伝記, 性格→正確, 福祉→副詞, 会社→外車) across 64 entries — every one a Sino-Japanese compound, all 1:1 with no ambiguity. The §4 self-check over those same 64 entries returned **zero** findings on the dimension that was broken, and 25 unrelated tag opinions instead. Every one of those links rendered, resolved, and worked; the reader simply landed on a different word. Deterministic base-form resolution is the only instrument that can see the class, which is the argument for putting `check_link_baseform.py --count` on the same CI ratchet as the dead-target gate once its 318 remaining cases are worked down.

**Homograph substitution, now documented as a family.** Three instruments hit it in one week and the shape is identical in all three — the tool substitutes a neighbour sharing the surface or the sound, then reasons correctly about the wrong word. The paid reviewer did it twice by *reading* (23075 講読 → 購読) and twice by *writing* (23166 激高/げきたか → げきこう, 4 error flags; 23182 悪感/あくかん → おかん, 5 flags); the corpus did it 87 times in links. All the reviewer flags were rejected, and the prediction that falls out is sharp: **an entry documenting the minority reading of a homograph will reliably draw a full sweep of error-severity flags across every dimension.** One corollary is worth keeping — a rejected flag can still be a coverage signal, and checking what the model had in mind found 激高/げきこう genuinely missing (candidate C22661) while 悪寒/おかん already existed at 12670.

**One instrument's retirement case is now five-for-five.** Tooling 24: the furigana screener ran 63 entries raising **zero** flags, then 58 entries raising 3 of which **zero** applied — all three in documented false-positive families, one of them flagging a reading that appears in the entry's *own* similar-words contrast. Cumulative post-fix: 0 applied of ~35 flags across five consecutive runs, no counter-evidence since the context-snippet repair. And because `polishing/tasks/cross-model-review/progress.txt` is shared, the screener's ~2 entries/min pins the cursor while the accuracy pass runs at ~13 — **the slower instrument sets the pace of the faster one**. A fixed 50-entry sample per run would unpin the cursor without discarding the instrument; retiring it outright is the other option. Curator decision either way.

**And one ordering is now settled practice.** Tooling 46 asked whether the free `VALID_SEMANTIC` diff should run before the paid reviewer. Two more blocks answered it: **110 of the 118** entries the reviewer flagged on `tags` in 22767–23100 had already been found by the free scan, and in 23101–23500 **38 of 54** flags either re-suggested a tag the free scan had applied or proposed `general` — only 4 were novel and correct. Five consecutive blocks agree. The free scan is the detector; the paid pass is a destination oracle plus in-list check, and should be documented as the default ordering rather than left to each run.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not modify prompts, skills, entries, or build scripts): the schema question behind the **datedness gap** — 外套 is both formal and dated, and `formality` cannot say so, which is why the reviewer proposed overwriting a correct value; the **screener retirement-or-downsample** decision (24), now with five data points; the **P31 adjective FORMS-block** decision (is the generated `conjugation` table authoritative? — eight entries were deliberately left inconsistent rather than drifted); the `manage_candidates.py sync` **stale-`noentry` hook** (19), which the P35 measurement re-prioritises since 85% of the population is months old at most; the **candidate-list filter** (54); the `review_accuracy.py` **empty `description`** bug (53); the reviewer-prompt line telling the model that a headword's kanji *and stated reading* are jointly authoritative; and the two standing adverse metrics from the last refresh — the rising review queue and the 337-item escalation backlog — which still have no owner.

**Process note**: this is the third consecutive harvest in which measuring first changed a filing, and the first in which it produced a *closure*. Two of the four items filed this week were filed narrower than proposed (P35 with its 883-link exclusion; Tooling 55 with its below-frontier caveat), and the one that was not filed at all had been reported twice. The rule that keeps earning: **file the measurement, not the observation.**

## [2026-08-02] maintenance | Harvest ×25, the bare-headword measurement, the unlinkable residue, twenty-ninth metrics refresh

**Session type**: Routine v2 `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 29 unharvested observations, 15 open backlog items)

**Activities**:
- **[F] Harvest** — all **25** loose observations from the 2026-08-01 wiki run's own §7 CI gate, the two 2026-08-01 polish runs, the systemic-fix run, the new-entries run, and the accuracy-review run. Filed and cleared.
- **[D/C] Created `Cleanup P36`, `P37`, `Tooling 56–62`** and deepened **`topics/inline-link-integrity.md`** with a new section, *The unlinkable residue*.
- **[E] Queue sync** — `ideas/backlog-queue.json`: three new items (`headword-missing-furigana`, `tag-politeness-polite-on-plain-vocab`, `crossref-closed-paradigm-symmetry`), `unknown-semantic` rescoped 5,009 → **4,900** against a fresh measurement.
- **[H] Twenty-ninth metrics refresh** of `topics/quality-metrics.md` (411 runs; 16 new lines since the 395-run refresh).

**The measurement that changed the filing most.** A `systemic-fix` run reported one entry — `27889_ageru`'s headword written `挙げる` rather than `{挙|あ}げる` — and noted that "headword-position furigana appears to escape at least one of the checks." Measuring it turned a single-entry note into the run's headline: **248 headwords carry bare kanji against 25,773 braced ones, so the corpus is 99.05% committed to a convention the schema does not state and no instrument checks.** `headword` is the only Japanese-bearing field with no format constraint at all, and it renders as the entry page's `<h1>` — the one line a learner reads first, shipping without ruby in a dictionary whose stated rule is that all kanji carry furigana.

Two properties decided how it was filed. It is **not legacy residue**: 126 of the 248 were created in 2026-07, 13 in 2026-08, only 7 predate 2026, and the newest affected block is 30298–30317. And it is **stratifiable** — 197 are all-kanji headwords where the sibling `reading` field makes `{headword|reading}` correct by construction, leaving 51 okurigana/katakana mixes that need alignment. So the sweep is easy and the sweep is *not the point*: without the ratchet in Tooling 56 it refills within weeks. Same root cause as Tooling 47 (cross-reference `headword` fields), and the two should be fixed by one shared list of "every field that can hold Japanese" rather than two hard-coded field names.

**The instrument that has been sitting unrun.** Cleanup P20 has debated *which* tag mappings to add to `TAG_MIGRATION` since 2026-06-21. This harvest measured what the **nine already there** are worth: **660 live instances, 13.5% of the 4,900-instance population, never swept dictionary-wide.** `time`→`time-general` alone has 204. Meanwhile successive accuracy-review runs have migrated 35–104 tags apiece by paid LLM review, re-deriving decisions the repo already encodes. Two further numbers reorder the plan: the distribution is much flatter than any single band suggested (**818 distinct labels, 345 singletons**, a curated top-50 map capping near 48%), and the residue is **not** concentrated ahead of the review frontier — 46.7% sits inside 6739–23607, which accuracy-review has already swept, against 51.7% above 23608. So the deterministic sweep has to cover the whole corpus, the map strategy has a natural ceiling near half, and what remains is one curator taxonomy decision rather than 768 individual ones.

**Two observations were narrowed by measurement, in the same direction.** A polish run proposed a targeted sweep of the remaining `politeness: "polite"` entries after fixing 78 in one creation block; 174 remain, but 99 carry an overt honorific marker and a hand sample of the other 75 is largely *correct* (こちら/そちら/あちら/どちら are polite demonstratives; すみません, よろしく are set polite expressions) — a per-entry review of a few dozen, not a batch. And a run described the missing copula-inflection link convention as making "the full-coverage tier-1 rule technically fail on any sentence containing them"; the true figure is **225 polished examples, 0.6%**. Both were filed at their measured size.

**But the politeness item's instrument finding outgrew its cleanup finding.** `check_tag_drift.py --check politeness-unsupported` reports **0 flags across 0 entries** on this corpus — because its predicate is `politeness in ("humble","honorific")` and never examines `polite` at all. The detector was green through an entire batch-creation defect that a human found by reading twenty entries. That is a clean instance of what `topics/instrument-defects.md` argues in general: **a check reporting zero is evidence about the check's predicate before it is evidence about the corpus.**
