**A new page section, and the reason it is a convention question rather than a backlog item.** Four independent runs hit Japanese text that both project rules apply to and neither rule can satisfy: bound morphemes (今-, 毎-, 来-, 義-), copula and auxiliary inflections (`で、`, `ではありません`), morphemes whose surface form is already occupied (prohibitive な, blocked by `09497_na` at the candidate door), and metalinguistic mentions ("the kanji 今"). The unifying property is that **the link rule was written for words, and the corpus contains Japanese that is not a word** — an affix, an inflection of a function word, a homograph blocked by a (surface, reading) key, or a glyph mentioned rather than used. Every measured scope here is small; what is unbounded is the *rediscovery*, since three of the four have now been re-derived at least twice by different runs. The treatments the runs converged on independently are consistent with each other, which is the best evidence they are right, and they cost four lines in a skill file. Routed to the curator as one decision rather than four.

**One observation was reversed outright.** A new-entries run asked that the reviewer prompt be told "`onomatopoeia` lives in `pos` and mimetics take `descriptive` in `semantic`." That convention does not exist: `onomatopoeia` is in **both** `VALID_POS` and `VALID_SEMANTIC`, and the corpus is split **172 / 116 / 71 overlapping**. The reviewer is not misreading a rule; it is reporting the absence of one. This matters because two filed items now pull in opposite directions on the same undecided question — Cleanup P33 is a 77-entry batch that would *add* `onomatopoeia` to `semantic`, and the proposed prompt rule would stop it being suggested there. Running either before the curator decides would make the corpus less consistent, not more.

**Metrics: both adverse trends reversed, and a third appeared underneath them.** Curator escalations went 175 → **0** and `needs_curator.txt` drained for the first time since it opened (337 → 299); the review queue fell 13,375 → **13,172** after two consecutive rises, having peaked at 13,549 intra-window. Neither reversal needed an intervention — both look like the cohort passing rather than a fix landing, so neither is closed. `tags` fell 93.3% → 69.8% on a mixed rather than pure off-vocabulary cohort, which is a cohort change and not a regression: set-membership flags stay near-perfect, taste flags do not. Furigana screening reached **six consecutive windows at zero precision** (0/29; 0 of ~64 cumulative post-fix), now with a cost measurement attached — it is serial at ~7–8 entries/min while `review_accuracy.py` is concurrent at ~44.

**The new adverse metric is the one worth the curator's attention.** The polish frontier advanced **34 entry IDs** this window while the dictionary grew **59** — the unpolished remainder widened by 25 entries in two days, across 16 runs of which 5 were polish runs. At ~2.1 frontier IDs against ~3.7 new entries per run, **no schedule of polish runs at the current per-entry cost closes the gap**, and adding polish runs at the expense of new-entries runs only trades one lane's progress for the other's. The 2026-08-01 harvest reached the same conclusion from the inline-link side. Logged as a `[pattern]` observation, because the response is not more polish — it is cheaper per-entry polishing (Tooling 49's read-only link suggester) or an explicit decision that the frontier marks a moving quality boundary rather than a target.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not modify prompts, skills, entries, or build scripts): the four unlinkable-residue conventions as one skill decision; the `onomatopoeia` pos-vs-semantic question, which gates Cleanup P33, the reviewer prompt, and the entry-creation skill together; the §A cursor rule (Tooling 58 — the cursor regressed 23608→22900 and a monotonic cursor can never see the holes at 6926–7179, 7262–7640, 8238–8528, 9850–10449, 13975–14186, 16167–16373); the furigana-screener retirement-or-downsample decision, now at six data points and three refreshes waiting; the frontier-versus-growth scheduling question above; and the standing P31 adjective FORMS-block and formality-detector questions carried forward from previous harvests.

**Process note**: the last four harvests have each been organised around measuring an observation's premise before filing it, and this one produced the full range of outcomes in a single session — one filing enlarged 250-fold (bare headwords), two narrowed to their true size (politeness, the copula gap), one reversed (`onomatopoeia`), and one closed outright with zero remaining occurrences (the 義-family address forms). The rule that keeps earning is unchanged: **file the measurement, not the observation.** What this run adds is its corollary for tooling — *check the instrument before believing its zero*, which is how a detector reporting no politeness defects and a corpus containing 174 of them came to be filed as one item.

## [2026-08-02] maintenance | Harvest ×18 (run 2) — the `noentry` provenance split, the `Xする` label convention, and lexical-family tag incoherence

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2 — `wiki` mode (scheduler: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 23 unharvested-observation-lines signal)

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep via MCP. Branch level with `origin/main`. Lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-34kgoo`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 spend this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvest** — all 18 loose observations from the day's metrics-refresh note, the two polish runs, the accuracy-review run and the new-entries run. Two of them proposed new backlog items; both were measured against the corpus before filing, and both came back **smaller and better-shaped** than proposed.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — `noentry` markers that were never correct.** A polish run found `01004_tsu` marking 一二三四五六八九 `noentry` when all eight are basic-tier entries created months *before* the marker, and proposed a "false positive" detector distinct from the stale-marker detector. The distinction reduces to one date comparison: if a marker's resolved target was created before the entry the marker sits in, the target existed at every moment the link could have been written. Result: **447 markers in 317 entries wrong when written** against **3,362 that genuinely went stale** (447 is a floor — a marker written during a later polish pass against a newer target counts as "stale" here). The two subclasses behave in opposite directions: the stale class grows with every `new-entries` run (85% pointing at bands 26000+), while the wrong-when-written class is **closed** — 12/13/35/78/97/45/21 by ID band up to 06999 and **zero above 07000**, because above the polish frontier there are no links at all. 301 of the 447 meet the existing A1/A2 mechanical criterion. **No second detector filed**: same scan, same queue, same one-token fix. The useful output is a `wrong_when_written` column, which *removes* a check — a marker whose target predates it needs no sense-drift judgment. The failure mode the observing run guessed is visible in the samples (surface form looked up instead of the lemma: 形→`02193_katachi`, 間→`00914_aida`, 家→`00612_ie`, 本→`00111_hon`).

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — the `Xする` link-label convention.** Filed as "worth a decision on which convention wins before a sweep". Of links whose declared base ends in する: **822 target a real `Xする` entry, 441 label the base `Xする` while targeting the bare noun** — but **174 of those 441 have no `Xする` entry to point at**. A "label must match the target's headword" rule would therefore delete a する that 174 sentences actually contain. Only 267 are decidable, and only the "prefer the する entry where one exists" reading is a sweep. Filed as a **Cleanup Informational** with the three coherent options, since the decision is the curator's.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — lexical-family tag incoherence.** A polish run standardised the four 皿 entries to `["tool"]` and noted 茶碗/箸/コップ still drifting. Measured across the family: **32 vessel/utensil entries carry 12 distinct tag-sets with no plurality** — 7 `["general"]` (also P13), 7 `["tool"]`, 6 `["food"]` (which describes the contents, not the object), 3 `["culture","food"]`, and nine one-off sets of which five use off-vocabulary labels (`tableware`, `objects`, `tools` against the valid singular `tool`, `gardening`, `household` — also P20). Filed as **new Cleanup P38** + queue item `tag-lexical-family-incoherence`. The generalisation is Tooling 57's closed-paradigm argument applied to lexical families: where the family is enumerable, disagreement among its members is mechanically detectable and cannot be a false positive. Recommendation is to size 2–3 more families before building an instrument.

- **[C] `topics/deterministic-vs-semantic-tasks.md` deepened** — first update since 2026-04-06. New section sorting the four paid review dimensions by whether *detection* is mechanical, which is the half being paid for: `tags` (a free `VALID_SEMANTIC` diff finds 110/118 and 38/54 of what the reviewer flags — the model is being paid for detection it cannot improve on, and only the destination needs judgment), `translation` (the 23893 romaji-left-in-an-English-translation and 23894 translation-contradicts-its-Japanese defects are truth-conditional cross-language comparisons — irreducibly semantic, and what the money is for), `furigana` (six consecutive zero-precision windows — the counterexample showing that *semantic* does not imply *worth a model*, because a model with no reliable evidence produces confident noise). Stated as a refinement to the page's own design principle 1: "automate detection, not decision" assumes detection is the automatable half, which holds only where the defect has a mechanical signature.

- **[C] `topics/inline-link-integrity.md`** gained both link measurements (new subsections and a seventh table row) plus the partial-versus-zero coverage distinction one polish run contributed: below the frontier an entry shows the linker its own conventions and the remaining decisions are marginal; above it every token is an open question. That is why the 4–8 entries/run figure is the right one to plan against and why Tooling 49's suggester changes the economics more above the frontier than below.

- **[E] Lint and sync** — wiki relative-link lint **0 broken**; three anchor typos I had introduced fixed against the real heading text (`#priority-11-…`, `#priority-13-…`, `check_semantic_clusterspy`); `backlog-queue.json` +1 item, `inline-link-stale-noentry` re-scoped with the provenance note, `updated` bumped.

**Also filed**: **Cleanup P20 update** — the migration map splits into *forced* renames (destination follows from the tag name alone: `medicine`→`health`, `linguistics`→`language`) and *context-dependent* labels with no entry-independent destination, where a first-draft map produced `education` for 立証 "proof", `nature` for 焼却炉 "incinerator", `time-season` for ゲレンデ "ski slope" and `business` for 溶鉱炉 "blast furnace". Resolved by the asymmetry that **dropping an off-vocabulary tag never adds a false claim while migrating one can**, so `drop` is the correct default for the context-dependent class and step 2 of the sequencing should extend the map with forced renames only, not the top 50 by frequency. Named residue: `interrogative` on exactly three entries (00534, 00543, 23898). **New Tooling 63** (`validate_tags.py` collapses 13,037 warnings into one number with no per-category breakdown — the second harvest running to write the same throwaway script to recover the off-vocab population), **64** (`manage_candidates.py add` prints only "Use --force to bypass this check", naming neither the word nor whether it matched an entry or a candidate — in a batch of 17 adds the caller cannot see what was dropped), **65** (`validate.py` accepts an inline link whose base form contradicts its target: これ→`00959_kiiroi` and 同じ→`00591_isogashii` both validated cleanly; the comparison exists in `check_link_baseform.py`, so what is missing is the validation-time ratchet — the same guard-beats-sweep argument as the headword-furigana item). **Tooling 24 update** — a fourth post-fix run at zero precision with the itemised disposition (4 documented false-positive families, 1 毎年 variant, 1 flag whose own concern text refutes it, 1 escalated), and the rate gap now ~1.4 vs ~7 entries/min against a shared cursor, so the cost of keeping the screener is paid in accuracy-pass coverage. **Tooling 54 update** — the ~1-in-10 usable-candidate ratio measured a second time the same way (15 good "seen in entry", 7 hand-picked from ~990), with the sequencing point that teaching the selector to score changes *reporting*, not supply. **Entry Follow-ups** — 06742 告白 (告白記 removed as a non-word; 告白文/告白録 are the plausible intents, worth one second opinion) and 04477 大皿 (`→得：07739_otoku`: label and target are different words). **The 06000–07999 zero-link block was filed a fourth and fifth time** and is marked as such on the existing Informational so it is not filed a sixth.

**Routed to the curator** (a `wiki` run may not modify prompts, skills, entries or build scripts): the `Xする` base-label convention (441 links, 267 decidable); the P20 step-1 sweep of the nine `TAG_MIGRATION` mappings already shipped, still unswept after three harvests recommending it; the `container`/`tableware` taxonomy question that partly blocks P38; and the standing items carried forward — the furigana-screener retirement decision (now at seven data points), the `onomatopoeia` pos-versus-semantic question, and the frontier-versus-growth scheduling gap.

**Process note**: this is the fifth consecutive harvest organised around measuring an observation's premise before filing it, and the first where all three measurements *narrowed* their filing without contradicting it. The pattern worth naming is what each measurement bought: not a smaller number, but a **boundary**. The `noentry` split found a subclass that cannot grow; the `Xする` count found the 174 cases no rule can reach; the tableware scan found that the incoherence is in the *set*, not in any member. In each case the boundary, not the count, is what tells a future run where to stop — which is a better argument for the measure-first discipline than "the estimates were wrong" was.

## [2026-08-03] maintenance | Harvest of 22 observations; three premises measured; thirtieth metrics refresh

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 26 unharvested observation lines).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 22 loose observations** from the 2026-08-02 accuracy-review (23908–24500), the 2026-08-02 polish run, and the 2026-08-03 accuracy-review (24501–25100). Three proposed detectors were measured against the corpus before filing.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — `explanation` that duplicates `gloss`.** Reported on two entries (24542 突出, 24544 可憐) with a one-line detector proposed. Measured: **201 senses in 179 entries**, out of 36,153 senses carrying an `explanation`; normalised comparison adds **zero** further hits, so it is exact duplication, not near-duplication. The distribution is the finding — **zero occurrences outside six contiguous creation blocks** (04470–04563 from 2026-01; 24159–24188, 24539–24558, 24786–24815, 25222–25245, 25301–25330 from 2026-04), the same batch-creation signature as P11/P20/P21. Filed as **new Cleanup P39**, queue item `definition-explanation-duplicates-gloss` (batch_ready, scope 201), and **Tooling 68**. The mechanical action is a *drop*, safe by construction: `schema.json` requires only `sense_number` and `gloss`, so removing a verbatim duplicate removes no information — the drop-vs-migrate asymmetry, transplanted from tags to fields.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — inline links whose reading disagrees with their target.** Proposed from a 20-entry number/date repair. Measured across all **266,899** links, and the naive rule is unusable: **28,906** hits, dominated by inflection (した→する ×1,016, なった→なる ×467). Restricting to targets with only non-conjugating POS gives 2,790; stripping particle/copula tails gives **2,113**; requiring that **an entry with exactly the surface reading already exists** gives **998** — the shippable slice, because it names an alternative instead of merely calling a reading odd. It reproduces the reported family exactly (何/なん→`03100_nan`, 会社/がいしゃ→`19013_gaisha`, 通り/どおり→`09884_doori`, では→`02945_deha`). Filed as **Tooling 66** with the ±3 adjacent-ID slip variant, two cautions (rendaku/on-kun alternation is not necessarily a defect; the 1,115 with no alternate entry are candidates for suffix entries, not link repairs), and a **P35 update** showing the number/date cluster is one family seen from three angles.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — `〜の前で` for `〜の前に`.** Proposed as a detector after a real fix at 06757. Measured: **100 occurrences in 90 entries and not one** has an event/temporal noun in front of it; every remaining instance is the correct locative (上司の前で, 仏壇の前で, 子供たちの前で). Filed as a Cleanup **Informational** recording that the scan returns nothing, so no later session re-proposes it. The defect was real and, as far as the corpus can show, unique.

- **[H] Thirtieth metrics refresh** of `topics/quality-metrics.md` (12 new lines, 411 → 423 runs, ~12,640 adjudicated flags). Headline: **last refresh's cohort hypothesis was testable and passed** — `tags` apply-among-decided returned from 69.8% to **91.5%** [634/59] on the two high-ID off-vocabulary blocks, so the dimension should be read as a thermometer for the *cohort*, not for the reviewer. New review-queue floor **12,625** intra-window; escalations still effectively closed (2); OpenRouter peak day $0.545 of $5. Furigana screening's **seventh consecutive zero window**, now with a throughput argument attached (~1.3–1.4 entries/min vs ~3 for the accuracy pass). The one adverse metric — **the frontier covered half of new growth for the second window running** (+40 entries vs +19 frontier IDs, after +59 vs +34) — is logged as a `[pattern]` observation for the next harvest, since two windows with different mode mixes and the same ratio is a trend.

**Also filed**: **Cleanup P20 update** — the drop-vs-migrate rule promoted from principle to procedure after its first deliberate test: the 24501–25100 run split 138 off-vocabulary labels into forced renames vs context-dependent, inspected the second class per entry, then re-audited the finished diff and caught **8 wrong destinations — all context-dependent, zero forced renames** (`place`→`building` turning パリ into a building; `environment`→`nature` on 焼却場). Recorded with the density datum (three consecutive blocks at 40–53%, but 106 of 138 labels now occur once or twice, so frequency-ranked maps hit diminishing returns) and the observation that a `--dimensions tags` pass over freshly-migrated entries is an *independent* check, not a redundant one (~$0.0004/entry). **Cleanup P38 update** — the counter/day-of-month retagging is the **second lexical family**, and unlike tableware it is not blocked on a taxonomy decision, so closed enumerable paradigms are where a family-aware detector should start; the mechanism is now stated as the item's thesis (a family drifts apart when its members are polished at different times, so per-entry review cannot see it). **Cleanup P37 update** — register drift and tag-vocabulary drift **dissociate** (1 politeness and 1 sole-`general` defect in 600 entries against 41% off-vocabulary tags), so register sweeps should work up from low IDs and tag sweeps down from high ones. **Tooling 24 update** — the two new zero windows with their false-positive families reduced to two regex-shaped rules. **Tooling 54 update** — the candidate variant-orthography check measured (reading alone: 28 hits, mostly homophones; shared reading **and** ≥1 shared character: 13 hits, ~11 actionable). **Tooling 59 update** — the reading test as a triage axis (56 items → 44 fast + 12 careful) and the `noentry`-plus-candidate outcome when no correct target exists. **New Tooling 67** — a per-range off-vocabulary tag *density* report, since the CI ratchet is a gate and cannot target. **Tooling 20 update** — the first post-scorer-fix priority lane found real work, and it was **inventory-shaped notes** (SIMILAR WORDS lists with no contrasts) rather than missing ones; noted that the scorer rewards the fix for an adjacent reason (length), so a rising note score is a ranking signal, not a quality one. **Entry Follow-ups** — 24314 等身 (bound morpheme; the headword move is a URL decision for the curator) and 07994 陳謝 (off-vocab `apology`/`official`, sweep with its block).

**Routed to the curator**: the furigana-screener retire-or-gate decision (now at eight data points and a throughput argument); the frontier-versus-growth scheduling gap (measured twice, ownerless); the `Xする` base-label convention; the P20 step-1 sweep of the nine shipped `TAG_MIGRATION` mappings; and the `container`/`tableware` taxonomy question blocking part of P38.

**Process note**: sixth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where one measurement returned **zero**. That is the more valuable outcome to be able to record: three of the four detectors proposed this week were worth building at a different shape than proposed, and the fourth was worth not building at all — which is only knowable by running the count, and the count cost less than writing the item would have.

## [2026-08-04] maintenance | Harvest of 27 observations; four premises measured; a backlog item's own premise refuted

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 31 unharvested observation lines).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. Branch level with `origin/main`; lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-smc81b`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index; `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 27 loose observations** from the 2026-08-03 metrics refresh, the 2026-08-03 polish and new-entries runs, the 2026-08-04 systemic-fix run (link-target-baseform batch 4), the 2026-08-04 polish run (06769–06774), and the 2026-08-04 accuracy-review (25101–25600). Four proposed detectors were measured before filing.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — the free-text `part_of_speech` field, and the premise it refutes.** Proposed as "a mechanical normalization pass driven by `metadata.tags.pos`", which is what [Cleanup P22](ideas/cleanup-backlog.md) and Tooling 29 have described since June as *display-only* and *deterministic*. Measured: **401 distinct values across 30,187 entries**; 6,573 (21.8%) deviate from their own `pos`-tag-set's plurality spelling; `noun`+`verb-suru` alone carries **39 spellings** because separator, affix order, and naming convention vary independently. The result that matters is the counterexample: the field is the **sole** record of transitivity for **50** verbs that have no `tags.transitivity`, of `proverb` for 8 entries and `idiom` for 13, and of a "verb phrase" qualifier for 19 with no schema home at all. A tag-driven regeneration would delete all of it. Filed as a **P22 update + Tooling 29 update** that invert the sequencing: the batch-ready half is a **backfill** (71 entries, queue item `pos-freetext-transitivity-backfill`), worth doing whether or not the normalizer ever runs because it converts English prose into queryable tags — 50 of them being transitivity values `find_missing_transitivity.py` reports as missing today.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — `body-part` on words that are not body parts.** Proposed after six corrections in one 531-entry accuracy-review range. Since the project's semantic tags are **denotational** (the reviewer judges each tag against the headword), the provable slice is `body-part` on an entry whose `pos` is a verb class: **41 entries**. Splitting by whether the headword contains a body part separates the two cases — **30 with none anywhere** (調査 "investigation", 舞う, 描く, 溢れる, なびく, ときめく, 火傷, 日焼け, 閉経, 大便 — pure drift) and **11 that name one** (目を伏せる, 首を傾げる, 手術, 発汗, 整髪, 洗髪 — a topical-vs-denotational convention question, to decide once rather than 11 times). In-list destinations exist (`health`, `appearance`, `action`, `body-internal`), so unlike P20 this is not a vocabulary gap. Filed as **new Cleanup P40** + queue item `tag-bodypart-non-denotational`, generalised to: *any concrete-thing tag (`tool`, `furniture`, `clothing`, `animal-*`, `plant-*`) on a verb-only entry is a type error.*

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — conjugation tables that generate the potential of a potential.** Proposed as a suppression flag after `30367 待ちきれる` → 待ちきれられる / 待ちきれろ. Measured, and it reaches **basic tier**: `00557 できる` publishes **できられる** and **できろ**, `01165 見える` publishes 見えられる, `01229 聞こえる` publishes 聞こえられる — live on the site, because conjugation tables are hard-coded in the entry JSON. Of two mechanical tests only one survives: the headword-shape test (`-きれる`) returns 9 but mixes lexicalized potentials with ordinary intransitive pair members (途切れる, 千切れる) whose potential is merely rare, while the **entry-internal self-declaration test** (prose calls it a potential form, table still has a Potential row) returns **6 with no false positives**. Filed as **new Cleanup P41**, queue item `conjugation-potential-of-potential`, and **Tooling 70** for the generator side.

- **[A/F] Measurement 4 — a literal `\n` in a notes field.** Proposed as a cheap `check_consistency.py` addition after a programmatic note rewrite risked it. Measured: **exactly 1 entry in 30,187** (`17662_kakuyasushimu`). Filed as **Tooling 74** explicitly as a permanent guard rather than a sweep — the defect is invisible to `validate.py` by construction — with the single instance on Entry Follow-ups.

- **[C] `topics/entry-consistency.md` deepened** — first update since 2026-05-17, and the synthesis this harvest earned. The page framed consistency entirely as a *between-entry* property, which is what makes it expensive: a cluster must be enumerated before any defect is visible. Added the **entry-internal contradiction** as a distinct class — two fields of the *same* entry disagreeing — with the six measured instances now on file (potential-of-a-potential, notes-vs-`formality`, `explanation`==`gloss`, `pos`-vs-`verb_class`, free-text-vs-`tags.transitivity`, link-label-vs-target). Three properties make it worth naming: detection is nearly free, there is no false-positive family because a self-contradicting entry is wrong either way, and every instance was **generator-created** — which is why the fix is a ratchet rather than a sweep.

- **[E] Lint** — wiki relative-link and anchor check across all pages: **0 broken** (the check itself needed fixing first — an em-dash-naive slug function reported 13 phantom failures against GitHub's actual anchor rules).

**Also filed**: **new Tooling 69** (`add_conjugations.py` branches on the *reading* ending in する — the family is exactly 4 entries and **2 are native verbs**, こする and すする, so a "skip katakana loanwords" guard would miss half of it; the reliable tests are `verb_class` and the headword, and after the hand fixes this is a regeneration guard, not a queue), **71** (`pos` vs `verb_class` disagreement: 0 live after the observing run fixed 5, so a ratchet with `00392_suru` allow-listed), **72** (a "naked Japanese" detector for link coverage — written ad hoc twice — with the requirement that particle/copula mentions inside notes prose must *not* be suppressed as noise, since that is exactly where fully-linked entries still leak), **73** (screening writes `"Parse failure"` as a flag: 3 of 217, inflating the flag rate that item 24's precision argument rests on). **Tooling 24 update** — **eighth consecutive zero-precision window** (11/217 flagged, 3 of them not flags at all), with the disposition list adding nothing new. **Tooling 20 update** — 00755, 06481 and 00118 top the priority list and are skipped every run; the test that would turn this into a concrete scorer bug is stated. **Tooling 54 update** — the candidate pool's usable supply sits *inside* its rejects (換気扇掃除 → 換気扇, 人影もない → 人影), so the filter should **split**, not only reject, which is the cheapest candidate source available. **Tooling 59 update** — the benign "imprecise base label, correct target" family stated as a rule, plus the noun↔suru-verb cross-linked pairs the reading test cannot catch. **Cleanup P13 update** — 350 pure-katakana entries carry sole-`general`. **P20 update** — a fourth consecutive high-ID block at **41%** off-vocabulary density (25101–25600), where the standing ">20% flagged means reviewer noise" heuristic misfires. **P37 update** — 24 katakana entries carry `formality: "formal"`, several contradicted by their own notes (06773 バスケ). **New Informational** — the 105 entries with no semantic tag at all are two populations (53 closed-class function words, where `grammatical` is the convention; 52 open-class omissions), small and bounded, needing a convention rather than an instrument. **Entry Follow-ups** — 17662 (the literal `\n`) and 03654 豊か (a 採れる link with no correct target; the honest fix is `noentry` plus a candidate). **The 06000–07999 zero-link block was filed an eighth and ninth time**; unchanged answer, but its cost estimate now agrees across three independent takes (~25–40 links/entry, ~3× a normal polish entry).

**Activity H not run**: `pipeline/metrics-history.jsonl` had 9 new lines since the thirtieth refresh (2026-08-03), below the ≥10 threshold. Next `wiki` run should expect to do it.

**Routed to the curator**: the `part_of_speech` house-style choice that blocks the normalizer (the backfill does not need it); the topical-vs-denotational tag convention that blocks 11 of the 41 P40 entries; the furigana-screener retire-or-gate decision (eight zero windows plus a throughput argument); the frontier-versus-growth scheduling gap (third refresh running, still ownerless); and the standing P20 step-1 sweep of the nine shipped `TAG_MIGRATION` mappings.

**Process note**: seventh consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where a measurement **refuted an item already in the backlog** rather than reshaping a new proposal. P22 and Tooling 29 had asserted for six weeks that the `part_of_speech` rewrite was display-only and lossless; it is lossless for 99.8% of entries and lossy for 71, and nobody would have found that by reading the item — only by running the count *against* it. The generalisable rule is narrow and worth keeping: **"regenerate a derived field from its source" is safe only once you have checked that the field really is derived.** The measure-first discipline has so far been applied to new proposals; this run is the argument for pointing it at settled items too.

## [2026-08-05] maintenance | Harvest of 16 observations; three premises measured; a six-times-filed detector finally sized

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 16 unharvested observation lines).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. Branch level with `origin/main`; lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-ou0w7n`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index; `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 16 loose observations** from the 2026-08-04 polish session 007 (priority lines 194–199, frontier 06775–06780), the 2026-08-04 accuracy-review (25601–26200), the 2026-08-05 new-entries run, and the 2026-08-05 polish run (frontier 06781–06786). Three premises were measured before filing.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — neighbours named in notes prose but missing from `cross_references`.** This shape has now been filed by six polish runs since 2026-07-31, most recently by a priority lane that hit it **6 times out of 6** (00486 年, 00507 部屋, 00631 一月: rich notes naming obvious neighbours, `cross_references` empty). Rather than file a seventh confirmation, this harvest **built and ran the detector [Tooling 55](ideas/tooling-backlog.md#55-detector-contrast-words-named-in-notes-prose-but-absent-from-cross_references) specifies**, across all 30,205 entries. It works, and the population is **2,795 entries / 5,391 refs** — roughly a quarter again as many relationships as the ~17,500 already in the structured field, every one of them with the target's entry ID already resolved and inline-linked in the prose. Three results shape the item: (1) **bullet-leading position is the filter** — requiring the link to lead its bullet removes 1,364 raw hits, and they are the right ones, because a mid-bullet link is a word the bullet *uses*, not the word it is *about* (00053 学科's `学科試験 vs 実技試験` otherwise proposes a `contrast` ref to 試験); (2) **the remainder is two populations of near-equal size**, discrimination sections (`SIMILAR WORDS`/`CONTRAST`/`OPPOSITE`, **1,402 entries / 2,395 refs**, batch-ready, with the header supplying the `type`) and thematic `RELATED*` rosters (1,470 / 2,999, a convention question — promoting 噴火's four `RELATED VOLCANIC TERMS` links would turn `cross_references` into a topic index); (3) **the queue grows as the frontier advances**, since 3,043 of 3,050 affected entries sit below ID 07000 — the check can only see inline-linked entries by construction. Filed as **new Cleanup P42** + the queue item moved `needs-detector` → `open`/`batch_ready`/scope 1,402.

- **[C] `topics/cross-references.md` deepened** — first update since 2026-06-09, with the design finding the measurement produced: this is a **field-choice failure, not a coverage failure**. The lexicographic work is already done in all 2,795 entries — the distinction was decided, the target's entry was found, the sentence was written — and only the transfer into the structured field is missing, which is why 676 of them need exactly one ref and none needs more than five. Two further points now on the page: two note sections that look alike (discrimination vs. thematic roster) are different asks and only one is a defect, and **prose position carries meaning**, so reading the notes field as a bag of links loses information that is actually there.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — `formality: formal` contradicted by the entry's own notes — which refuted the observing run's own example.** Proposed as a sweep after 06776/06779/06780. The naive predicate ("tagged `formal`, notes mention casual/colloquial/neutral") returns **1,560 of 5,068 `formal` entries and is unusable**: a formal word listing its CASUAL EQUIVALENTS is doing exactly what a good entry does. 06779 いずれにせよ is one of those — its own REGISTER note reads "written/formal expression … sounds stiff in casual conversation" — so the tag is right and the CASUAL EQUIVALENTS section names *other words*. The run's genuine finds (06780, and 06776's sole-`general`) it had already fixed. The sound predicate reads only the REGISTER/FORMALITY section's description **of the headword itself** and returns **exactly 5**, all in one contiguous 07352–07411 creation cohort. And it cannot be this priority's instrument in any case: only **555 of 5,068** `formal` entries have a REGISTER section at all, so the test is unarguable where it fires and silent for 89% of the population — a ratchet for new entries, not a sweep. Filed as a **P37 update**; queue scope 6 → **5**.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — `word_id_lookup.json` and katakana.** Reported as "keys katakana words under their hiragana reading, so a katakana lookup returns nothing and invites a spurious `noentry`". Half right: `by_reading` holds **0** pure-katakana keys, but `by_headword` holds **2,159**, ピンク among them. The lookup is **asymmetric, not missing**, so the real failure mode is a caller consulting `by_reading` first — the natural choice when working from an example sentence's furigana — and silently getting nothing for the entire loanword vocabulary. Filed as **new Tooling 76** with three fixes in cost order, the cheapest (a two-line note in the `inline-word-links` skill) being the one that would have prevented this instance. A stale `noentry` written today is [Cleanup P35](ideas/cleanup-backlog.md) tomorrow.

**Also filed**: **new Tooling 75** — the accuracy reviewer assigns `error` to some "this tag is not in the valid list" findings and `warn` to others *within one range*, on a class that is binary and machine-checkable, so there is no entry-level fact it could be responding to. This is not cosmetic: routine2.md §A triages **by severity**, working error-severity issues individually and bulk-rejecting warn-severity families — so flags from the dimension currently running at **83.0%** apply-among-decided are partly routed into the reject bucket. Recommended standing rule: severity is not a triage axis for `dim: tags`. **Tooling 24 update** — the screener's zero streak **ends at one**: 0 flags on 78 entries in 25601–25792 (first post-context-fix sample) but a genuine hit at 24842 (katakana プロ sealed inside a kanji group), closing the window at 1/27. One true positive is not a rate, and the class it belongs to is the one item 24 proposes to catch with a regex for free — so the evidence now separates cleanly: **retire on cost, not on precision**. **Cleanup P20 update** — a fifth consecutive high-ID block (25601–26200 at 197/~600 = **33%** off-vocabulary, 45 migrated in range), the first reading below the 40–53% band that held for four blocks; the standing step-1 sweep of the nine already-shipped `TAG_MIGRATION` mappings remains unrun after four harvests recommending it, and remains the only action here needing no detector, no budget and no curator decision. **Tooling 54 update** — the ~1-in-10 usable-candidate ratio measured a **third** time by a third method (~200 sampled across the non-"seen in entry" pool, almost nothing entry-worthy, against 16 "seen in entry" candidates that were all high-value); three agreeing measurements make ~980 candidates safe to plan against as **~100 usable words**, which is the number that should drive restock cadence. **Entry Follow-ups** — 30380 使いやすい (a `cross_references` object with no `target_id`, self-resolving once 使いにくい exists) and 00486 年 (a basic-tier entry whose sense-1 examples all illustrate 今年/去年/来年, so the headword never appears standalone). **The 06000–07999 zero-link block was filed a tenth and eleventh time** (06775–06780; 06781–06786, all created 2026-01-18); the band is now traced continuously from ~06150 to 06786 with no exception found by any run, and it is the most-refiled observation in the project.

- **[H] Thirty-first metrics refresh** (14 new lines, 423 → **437 runs**, ~13,147 adjudicated flags). **New review-queue floor at 12,018** — 607 below the previous floor and the largest single-refresh drop in the series, though it is a *coverage* event (two large accuracy-review runs) rather than a change in fix rate. Escalations closed for a third consecutive refresh (**0**); `needs_curator.txt` steady at 301. `tags` holds the top of its range at **83.0%** [337/69] while the cohort's off-vocabulary density *fell* 41% → 33% — precision tracking the defect's existence rather than its density, which is what a calibrated instrument should do, and a further argument for Tooling 75. Furigana screening 1/27. **The frontier-versus-growth gap reproduced a third time and its ratio is now the most stable number on the page**: +57 entries against +29 frontier IDs, the frontier again covering almost exactly half of new growth.

**Routed to the curator**: the frontier-versus-growth rate, now a three-refresh trend at ~50% and a mode-mix decision rather than an observation; the furigana-screener retire-on-cost call (the precision argument is now settled in the screener's *favour* and the throughput argument carries the whole case); the thematic-`RELATED*` convention that gates half of P42; and the standing P20 step-1 sweep, unrun after four harvests.

**Process note**: eighth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where the measurement's *product* was a shippable queue item rather than a correction. Six runs had filed the notes-prose/`cross_references` gap and each filing added one more anecdote; the item sat at `needs-detector` with `scope_estimate: null` for five days because nobody had spent the twenty minutes to run the scan its own description specified. The measurement did three things no further confirmation could have: it sized the work (2,795, not "several"), it found the false-positive family that makes a naive implementation unusable (mid-bullet collocational links), and it split the population into a batch-ready half and a curator question. The rule worth keeping: **when an item has been filed more than twice, the next filing should be a measurement, not a confirmation** — a third anecdote adds nothing a count would not, and the count is usually cheaper than the anecdote.

## [2026-08-06] maintenance | Harvest of 25 observations; three premises measured, three instruments closed

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 25 unharvested observation lines, 29 by the selector's count).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. Branch level with `origin/main`; lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-927wzs`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index; `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 25 loose observations** from the 2026-08-05 accuracy-review (25872–26440), the 2026-08-05 polish and new-entries runs, the 2026-08-05 systemic-fix batch, and the 2026-08-06 accuracy-review (26189–26700). Three premises were measured against the whole corpus before filing, and each measurement closed an instrument rather than refining it.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — the off-vocabulary tag queue, counted whole, and the map-extension premise sized against it.** The observing run proposed "~55 more 1:1 mappings" in `TAG_MIGRATION` to make "the remaining ~2,500 baselined entries batch-fixable". The repo scan that proposal implies had never been run. It returns **2,530 entries / 3,208 instances / 687 distinct off-list names** (down from 2,808 entries — the lane *is* converging). Four results, in decision-changing order: (1) **the standing step-1 sweep is a 364-instance job** — the nine already-shipped mappings cover 11.3% of the mass, so the action five harvests have called "highest-yield" and deferred is in fact simply *small*, and was deferred on the assumption it was not; (2) 55 more head mappings reach **40.6%**, leaving 623 names / 1,540 instances — a frequency-ranked map can decapitate this queue but cannot finish it, since the top 20 names carry 28.3% and 249 names are needed for 82%; (3) **every rule that generates a mapping without judgment covers 78 names / 196 instances (6.1%)** — the head is not spelling variants; (4) what the head *is* is the taxonomy gap: `place`(54), `location`(50), `object`(46), `state`(32), `quality`(32), `manner`(30), `degree`(30), `document`(20), `position`(14), `objects`(14) = **322 instances in one conceptual region `VALID_SEMANTIC` has no slot for**. P13's 2026-07-30 update reached the same seven strings from the *reviewer* side; a corpus count and a reviewer-behaviour observation converging on the same ten strings is the strongest available evidence that the gap is in the vocabulary. Filed as a **P20 update** + queue scope 4900 → **2530**. New prioritization datum: **1,121 of the 2,530 carry off-list tags and nothing else** (functionally untagged), against 1,409 that also carry a valid tag — only the first half is user-visible.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — the sole-`general` gloss-keyword suggester, refuted.** Proposed by the observing run after 11 transparent cases (視覚障害 → `health`, 選挙運動 → `politics`, 不敬罪 → `law`), and the oldest recommendation on P13 — Tooling 6 proposes the same instrument, and it has sat unbuilt since 2026-05-23. Built empirically (899 gloss tokens concentrating ≥80% on one tag across the 20,257 specifically-tagged entries) and run against the **3,741** sole-`general` entries: proposals for **554 (14.8%)** at that threshold, **109 (2.9%)** at ≥90%/n≥10, and roughly **one proposal in five is wrong**. The errors share one shape — 眉 → `nature` (its explanation says "ridge"), 保険料 → `transportation` (the gloss illustrates car insurance), 負け惜しみ → `food` — the tag taken from *context the gloss mentions* rather than what the headword *denotes*, which is the P11 defect the tag lane exists to clean up. **A tool that infers a tag from surrounding prose cannot be denotational**, so the instrument is not low-yield but pointed the wrong way. The population resists it structurally too: an entry is sole-`general` *because* no domain was obvious, so the set is enriched for words with no domain signal (万端, 概説, 座標, 型, 連鎖, 特価). Filed as a **P13 update** marking the instrument question **closed**; queue scope 3791 → 3741 with the surviving levers named (the 350 katakana entries, the within-block-inconsistency heuristic, frontier applies — none of which read the gloss).

- **[C] `topics/schema-tag-reliability.md` deepened** — first update since 2026-06-13, with the synthesis the two measurements share: **both instruments were asked to supply judgment that was never encoded in the data they read.** A migration map reads a tag name and can only rewrite it into another name; it cannot invent a category the vocabulary lacks. A gloss suggester reads prose written to *explain* a word and can only correlate; it cannot recover which of the concepts the prose mentions is the one the headword *is*. Where the missing judgment is small and bounded (78 mechanical names, 350 katakana loanwords) automation works; where it is the substance of the task, the honest queue entry is "N entries need a lexicographer", and the useful engineering is the part that **sizes** that number and **routes** the blocked share — which for 322 instances is one curator taxonomy decision, not a script.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — pure-kana furigana wrappers inside inline-link surfaces.** Requested as "a targeted scan" after 00792 首's `⟦{あの|あの}→あの：00915_ano⟧`. Measured: **108 instances across 94 entries**, all a subset of P9's existing 373 `pure-kana` findings (so a targeting hint, not a new item) — but **84 of the 108 are katakana** (`{バイク|ばいく}`, `{ニュース|にゅーす}`), not the hiragana case that prompted it. Three properties make it the slice to sweep first: it is where the defect stops being cosmetic (link surfaces are text a lookup may match against; all 108 resolve today, 5 target `noentry`), the katakana majority inherits P9's unconditional fix rule, and the shape — a katakana word wrapped with a hiragana pseudo-reading — is exactly the belief **Tooling 76** describes in `word_id_lookup.json`, whose `by_reading` holds zero katakana keys. Filed as a **P9 update** + queue note.

**Also filed**: **Tooling 53 resolved as misdiagnosed** — there is no `description` field; `review_accuracy.py:185` specifies `concern`, and a scan of recent reports finds it populated on every issue, so both candidate causes the item lists are answered "neither". Kept rather than deleted, because as written it would send someone to patch a correctly-behaving script, and because its residual point (a bare `"-> general"` suggestion is thin evidence) is real and belongs with **Tooling 75**. **Tooling 46 update** — the 81%/19% split confirmed a sixth time (118 of 146 reviewer `tags` flags were the free detector's off-vocab family), and Measurement 1 now *bounds* the item: the pre-scan should detect exhaustively, suggest for the ~11% the map covers, and hand the rest over as detected-but-undestined rather than promising destinations it cannot supply. **Tooling 24 update** — the throughput case measured directly (**~8 entries/min screening vs ~50/min accuracy**; 512 entries = ~65 min vs ~10 min), precision 1/9 on 26189–26418 with the eight rejects all in documented false-positive families, and the second-order datum that matters: in the run that measured it the *accuracy* pass also fell short (316 of 569), so the screener is now slow **at the expense of** the paying pass. Both true positives this month came from **notes** furigana, the least-swept surface — a note for whatever replaces it. **Tooling 59 update** — the 13 "ambiguous" findings four batches deferred to the curator were **all decidable**, because the surface's own furigana names the reading and the reading selects one candidate (後 あと→09580, 分 ぶん→26216, 船 せん→09875, 方 かた→10665, 館 かん→17308): *lookup ambiguity is not decision ambiguity*, and the filter that proves it is one the detector already implements, so bucketing before filtering is a labelling bug rather than a judgment queue. **Entry Follow-ups** — four new: **26031 格上** (an entry built on a reading that does not exist — 格上 is かくうえ, its gloss and examples are 格上げ under the non-word furigana かくじょうげ, duplicating 07405 and 13122; every repair changes the live URL, so curator-only, and already on `needs_curator.txt`), **04230 打ち明ける** (an etymology note whose 明ける gloss matches no existing entry — 00563 is 開ける, 21288 is 明ける "to dawn"; the note is the defect, not the link), **02918/10077 とき** (near-duplicate entries, and the sole cause of the one residual ambiguous baseform finding), and the **missing hundreds** 二百…九百 (五百 has 12738, its eight siblings do not, so 00780 mixes one live link with eight `noentry` markers — a scope decision to write down, not a task). **The 06000–07999 zero-link band was filed a twelfth and thirteenth time** (06787–06792, 06793–06798); traced continuously now from ~06150 to 06798, with two runs again recommending a block sweep over 067xx–068xx.

**Activity H not run**: `pipeline/metrics-history.jsonl` had **7** new lines since the thirty-first refresh (2026-08-05), below the ≥10 threshold. The next `wiki` run should expect to do it.

**Routed to the curator**: the **taxonomy question** — does `VALID_SEMANTIC` want a spatial/positional tag and a document-type tag? — now sized at 322 instances and identified as the largest single block of the P20 migration queue, blocking both the reviewer and the map; the **364-instance step-1 sweep**, which needs no detector, no budget and no decision, and is now known to be small; the furigana-screener retire-on-cost call, whose throughput argument has grown a second-order cost to the accuracy pass; and the frontier-versus-growth rate, carried forward a fourth time.

**Process note**: ninth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where every measurement **closed** an instrument instead of specifying one. Two of the three (the migration map, the gloss suggester) had been recommended repeatedly — one of them since May — and both failed for the same reason, which is only visible when they are measured on the same day: an instrument can only supply judgment that is already encoded in the data it reads. The rule the last harvest proposed ("when an item has been filed more than twice, the next filing should be a measurement") extends cleanly: **a measurement's most valuable outcome is a closed question, not a sized queue.** A queue that turns out to be 40% reachable is worth knowing; an instrument that turns out to be aimed the wrong way is worth more, because it stops recurring.

## [2026-08-07] maintenance | Harvest of 39 observations; two premises measured, one alarm downgraded to a closed incident

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes; observations backlog high"; 43 unharvested observation lines by the selector's count, 39 tagged lines in four loose sections).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. Branch level with `origin/main`; lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-052t90`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index; `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 39 loose observations** from the 2026-08-06 polish run, the 2026-08-06 priority-lane polish run, the 2026-08-06 `systemic-fix` link-baseform batch 6, and the 2026-08-07 polish, new-entries and accuracy-review runs. Two premises were measured before filing.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — the braced inline-link base forms, where two numbers that looked like a contradiction were both correct.** A polish run reported "220 occurrences repo-wide" against a queue item carrying `scope_estimate: 36` and treated the item as understated. The scan returns **226 instances across 36 entries**, and the ratio is the finding: **6.3 braced base forms per affected entry**, with **33 of the 36 below ID 01000** in two tight runs (00697–00716, the numerals and counters 一 二 人 枚 個 中 時 歳; and 00966–00984) plus three singletons. The honest description is therefore not "a 226-occurrence sweep" but "**36 files, one basic-tier authoring cohort, one regex**" — a single `systemic-fix` batch needing no cursor, no sampling and no priority order. The transformation is provably safe as P24 claims (the `entry_id` resolves the link; the base slot is display-adjacent), with one caution the measurement adds: the diff lands on basic-tier pages, so spot-check rendered output rather than JSON. Filed as a **P24 update** + `scope_measured_at`. The unit ambiguity that caused the false contradiction is itself now **new Tooling 79** — `scope_estimate` has no unit, and this is the *second* time in a week a run has read entries where instances were meant (the stale-`noentry` population has been published as 7,386, 3,809, 2,887, 2,633 and 2,351 in different places, all true of different things).

- **[A/E] Measurement 2 — "GitHub Actions is dispatching nothing", measured, and downgraded from an open emergency to a closed incident with a documented recovery.** A `systemic-fix` run reported that no workflow had run repo-wide since 2026-08-06 15:50 UTC and that three consecutive Routine runs had been unable to reach the §7 CI gate; it proposed adding `workflow_dispatch:` to `validate.yml`. Checked against the workflow-run history, all three parts moved. (1) **The outage was real and is bounded**: `Validate Entries` forms an almost perfectly regular series — one run per Routine run, ~2.5–3.5 h apart, **30 of the last 30 concluding `success`** — with exactly one gap in it, **2026-08-06T15:43Z → 2026-08-07T00:37Z, 8 h 54 m**, about three slots. The two PRs sitting with `total_count: 0` check runs are the strong evidence, because a PR that exists with zero checks cannot be explained by "no PR was opened". (2) **It is over**: three green `Validate Entries` runs since the gap, and nothing in the repository was changed to fix it. (3) **No work was lost, and the thing that saved it is not written down anywhere** — #3130/#3131 never merged as themselves (main goes #3129 → #3132), but their content is on main: `build/check_link_baseform.py` is present and both 的 repairs (00445 開放的, 02627 外交的な) point at `09839_teki`, because the next run fetched the stranded branch and **absorbed its commits into its own** before pushing. That procedure is now documented in `project/content-pipeline.md` as the response to a stranded PR whose checks never appear. The `workflow_dispatch` proposal survives as **new Tooling 80**, reclassified as *preparedness rather than repair* — the reason to build it is not the outage that self-resolved but that commit absorption only works while a *later* run is still scheduled to absorb into.

- **[H] Thirty-second metrics refresh** (17 new lines, 437 → **454 runs**, ~14,188 adjudicated flags) — and the refresh **retires this page's headline `tags` number**. For three refreshes the page has reported a single `tags` apply-among-decided figure and read its movement as reviewer behaviour: 91.5% → 83.0% → **71.0%**. Split by note family, this window's 542 decided `tags` flags are three instruments with nothing in common: **off-vocabulary migration 99.4%** [331/2], **in-list substitution 28.1%** [50/128], **formality/register 10.0%** [3/27]. The off-vocabulary family is ~100% *by construction* — §A's policy makes "not in `VALID_SEMANTIC`" correct by definition — so the blended figure is approximately **the share of the reviewed block's flags that are off-vocabulary**, which is a property of when the block was created and not of the model reading it. The per-block P20 density ran 53% → 41% → 33% → 28% across the same four sweeps, directly behind it. Finding §1 predicted this decline in June; the decomposition is what makes the prediction checkable rather than retrospective, and it also supplies the triage axis Tooling 75 has been asking for — not severity, but "does this flag name a tag outside the closed list?", which a two-line check answers before the flag is read. Everything else moved as expected: **new review-queue floor 11,136** (−882 below the previous floor, second consecutive record drop), escalations resumed after three closed refreshes (`needs_curator.txt` 301 → **319**), furigana screening 1 applied of 24 (**4.2%**), OpenRouter peak day **$0.532** of $5, and the **frontier-versus-growth gap reproduced a fourth time** at +59 entries against +26 frontier IDs (44%; the four-refresh band is now 44–50%).

- **[C] `topics/inline-link-integrity.md` deepened** — first update since 2026-08-02, with Measurement 1 and with the two *wrong-target* classes three runs surfaced this month. `check_link_targets.py` asks "does this ID exist?" and `check_link_baseform.py` asks "is this ID the base form's own entry?"; a link can pass both and still send the reader to a different word. Two shapes, both fully deterministic and both needing only the two entries' own fields: **the homophone that isn't the word** (00897 店員's 店長 → `07537_tenchou` 転調 "modulation"; reading agrees, headword does not) and **the bound suffix linked to its free-standing homophone** (〜的 → `03546_teki` 敵 rather than `09839_teki` 〜的, separable because the suffix entry's headword carries the `〜`). Filed together as **new Tooling 78** — one pass over the link corpus answers both.

- **[A] Project counts refreshed** in `project/overview.md` and `project/vocabulary-tiers.md`: **30,264 entries / 27,461 general / 118,070 examples / 20,753 cross-references**. Cross-references passed **20,000** and are up **+3,685 since 2026-08-01** — by a wide margin the fastest-growing structure in the dictionary, and the direct product of the P42 notes-prose extraction lane, which is worth noting because P42 was filed on 2026-08-05 as a *field-choice* failure whose lexicographic work was already done. The growth rate is what that diagnosis predicted.

**Also filed**: **new Cleanup P43** — the 06800–07100 block measured at **288 of 301 entries (96%) with zero inline links**, filed *against* this wiki's own standing position that zero-link entries are the polish frontier and not a defect. That position is still right about the 23,294 entries as a whole and wrong about this block, for one measured reason: the frontier crawls through it at roughly one-fifth normal speed, because these are one 2026-01-18 grammar-expression batch and a polish run that meets them spends its entire budget writing links from scratch. Filed with a sequencing constraint — run the stale-`noentry` resolution and Tooling 78 *first*, since writing 288 entries of new links against a lookup that answers katakana from `by_headword` only (Tooling 76) is how a fresh cohort of spurious `noentry` markers gets manufactured. **New Cleanup P44** (naked sentence-final です/だ in the 00700–00900 basic block) filed as needs-detector *and* needs-convention, because whether the copula should be linked at all is the same question as the `Xする` label convention. **New Tooling 77** (the reviewer's `tags.formality` sub-family at 10%, measured twice independently this week and now in the ledger; §A already has the right policy — apply only when the entry's own register description contradicts the label — but the reviewer is not told the rule its adjudicator uses), **81** (the §0b sweep can discard `systemic-fix` work; two runs had to leave manual "do not sweep me" comments, and the sweep is most dangerous exactly when the failure it cleans up after is an outage rather than supersession), **82** (`build/propose_inline_links.py` — a polish run prototyped the mechanical half of inline linking, reported it cut per-entry cost "by a large factor", and threw it away), **83** (na-adjective normalization in `check_link_baseform.py`: base 巨大な → entry 巨大 is the same accepted relationship as 参加する → 参加). **Tooling 46 update** — the reviewer turns out to *be* the migration-map generator the 2026-08-06 measurement said could not exist, and the two results are compatible rather than contradictory: a static map must choose a destination per **tag name** (687 in the tail, which is why 55 more head mappings reach only 40.6%), whereas the reviewer chooses one per **entry** and therefore never has to generalise — on 27001–27313 off-vocabulary tags were the entire applicable yield, 34 of 39, each with an unambiguous in-list destination the reviewer supplied. **Extending `TAG_MIGRATION` beyond the ~78 mechanical names is the wrong lever.** **Tooling 24 update** — a fourth consecutive early stop, ~14 entries screened in the time the accuracy pass covered ~150, and its single flag self-annotated "technically not an error"; the precision argument has been settled in the screener's favour for a month, so the case rests entirely on cost, and the cost is now measured twice. **Tooling 73 update** (parse failures quantified at ~4%, which matters because the screener's flag rate is the number Tooling 24 is measured against). **P13 update** — settled: 44 sole-`general` entries corrected in one accuracy-review block, so the lane does the work and the refuted gloss-keyword suggester is not missed. **P35 update** — whole-corpus rescan at **7,386 markers / 2,633 resolving instances / 2,351 distinct multi-character bases**, plus the exclusion that makes it safe (403 instances on 189 single-character bases are mostly bound morphemes — 業, 形, 角 — where the marker is correct). **P42 update** — 8 of 8 twice more, and the reason it keeps resurfacing: the `notes` priority score cannot see it, *because the notes are good*. **Entry Follow-ups** — 01385/02485 気持ち refiled after surviving two months and a `find_merge_candidates.py` pass, 02586/25544 巨大/巨大な added to `entry-pair-consolidation` (7 → 9), and four in-run fixes recorded so a future run does not refile them, including 03654 豊か's 採れる link, which sat deferred through two batches because two wrong candidates were compared against each other and nobody searched for the third (20862 採る).

**Routed to the curator**: **two headword-scope questions**, now written into `project/vocabulary-tiers.md` because both are prior to tiering — are person names in scope (C22806 夏目漱石, harvested from 06801; the dictionary has place names but no biographical headwords and no schema shape for one), and when is a number+counter compound entry-worthy (two consecutive runs decided C22795 三十人 differently, and the rule that would settle it — entry-worthy when the counter's reading changes, or when the combination is a common round figure — would have meant *skipping* it, so writing it down is a real decision rather than a rationalisation). Carried forward: the **taxonomy question** (a spatial/positional tag and a document-type tag, 322 instances, still the largest blocked block of P20); the **364-instance step-1 sweep**, unrun after five harvests and now known to be small; the **furigana-screener retire-on-cost call**, fourth evidenced refusal; and the **frontier-versus-growth rate**, a fifth time and now a stable 44–50% band rather than an observation.

**Process note**: tenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where a measurement was aimed at an **operational alarm** rather than a lexicographic claim. The pattern that produced both of this run's findings is the same and is worth naming: **both reports were correct about what they saw and wrong about what it meant.** "220 occurrences" was a true count read against a field counting something else; "Actions has dispatched nothing" was a true observation of an outage read as an ongoing emergency when it had already ended and the work had already been recovered. Neither error was a mistake in observation, and neither would have been caught by a second anecdote — only by measuring the *surrounding* quantity: the entries-per-instance ratio in the first case, the shape of the run series around the gap in the second. The rule the last two harvests proposed ("when an item has been filed more than twice, the next filing should be a measurement"; "a measurement's most valuable outcome is a closed question") extends once more: **an alarm deserves a measurement before it deserves a fix, because the most common failure of an alarm is not being false but being stale.** The `workflow_dispatch` item is worth building; it was not worth building for the reason it was proposed.

## [2026-08-08] maintenance | Harvest of 20 observations; a seven-week-old bundled backlog item split into one shipment and one retirement

**Session type**: Unified Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 20 unharvested observations, 21 open backlog items, comprehensive frontier 06826).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** and `list_branches` returned **`main` only** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep, zero orphan branches. Branch level with `origin/main`; lock acquired cleanly on `claude/determined-rubin-8ihgsg`.

**Mode**: `wiki`. No entries changed → **§4 self-verification skipped** (OpenRouter untouched, $0 this run); §5 metrics line appended (mode=wiki, changed=0); **no `make build`** (markdown plus one JSON index; `docs/` untouched).

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 20 loose observations** from the two 2026-08-07 polish runs (priority + frontier lanes) and the 2026-08-07 accuracy-review run. Three premises were measured before filing.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — a two-rule backlog item, bundled since June and never sized, splits into a shipment and a retirement.** [Tooling 8](../wiki/ideas/tooling-backlog.md) has carried two proposed enhancements to `check_furigana_format.py` since 2026-06-17, both prompted by one entry (`06147_jiboujiki`) that genuinely contained both shapes: (a) flag `{…}` spans with no `|`, (b) flag fields with unequal brace counts. Measured over the whole corpus, they could hardly be further apart. **Rule (b) is 34 instances / 33 entries**, deterministic, and — unlike almost everything else on the wrapper-anomalies page — **visible to a reader**: `08385`'s rendered page reads "**ぎ} tends to be used for**", a literal brace in running English prose, and `04471` contains `かき{混→かき{混：noentry⟧|ま}ぜ`, a furigana wrapper and a `⟦…⟧` link interleaved into each other so that neither parses. Filed as **new Cleanup P45** + queue item `furigana-unbalanced-braces`, batch-ready at 33 files with no cursor. **Rule (a) is 931 instances / 623 entries and ~100% false**, and is retired. The reason is the finding: the brace is doing a **second, undocumented job** — mention-quoting, the way an English style guide italicises a word under discussion (`The reading {じゅうぶん} means 'enough' while {じゅっぷん/じっぷん} means '10 minutes'`; `Usually read as {だて}, sometimes {たて}`; `- {〜て}たまらない:`). The decomposition is 855 kana mentions in notes prose, 168 grouping wrappers (`{お{正月|しょうがつ}}`), 54 kanji mentions, 22 inside link surfaces, 7 pattern placeholders. **The obvious repair fails too**: "flag only spans containing kanji" looks right until you read the 54, where a reading would frequently be *wrong* to add — 00319 exists precisely to contrast 匂 and 臭, which share the reading にお, so annotating them erases the distinction the note is making. The convention is now written down for the first time in `topics/furigana-wrapper-anomalies.md`, because its absence is not cosmetic: **three sessions across two months have proposed detectors against it**, each correctly reasoning from the only rule that *is* documented.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — a proposed detector category that would have mislabelled its own worst case.** The 2026-08-07 accuracy-review run found `27447` carrying `{弟|おとうと}{さん|くん}` (ruby くん over the kana さん) and proposed splitting the detector's `pure-kana` findings into "kana wrapped with its own reading" (cosmetic) and "kana wrapped with a *different* reading" (a real content error). The second class measures at **5 instances corpus-wide** against 339 cosmetic — and 27447 is not among them, because the observing run fixed it. More usefully, the five are three shapes rather than one: two genuine wrong readings (23394 `{カキ|がき}`, an oyster stranded in its rendaku form) and three **empty reading slots** (`{X|}`), which is the degenerate form P9's 2026-06-20 update left as an open question about detector coverage. And the single most serious instance is not the proposed class at all: **28929 `{に|み}` is a dropped kanji** — 苦悶に満ちた声 lost the 満 out of `{満|み}ちた`, leaving a well-formed-looking wrapper over the particle に. A detector built to the proposed spec would have filed it under "cosmetic kana wrapper". Filed as a fix-on-sight list in **Entry Follow-ups**.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — the stale-`noentry` tilde forms, and why the sweep is blocked rather than ready.** Two polish runs independently hit markers whose base form is bare (`全`, `感`, `代`) while the entry that now exists carries a tilde (`全〜`, `〜感`, `〜代`), and proposed that the lookup try both. Measured: **159 instances across 47 distinct bases** resolve only that way, against 3,536 on the bare headword and 3,686 unresolved. The population has one provenance — it is essentially the **28xxx affix cohort** (〜感 28578, 〜代 28331, 〜書 28467, 〜賃 28343, 〜化 28335, …), a deliberate suffix batch created *after* the entries referencing those morphemes, which is P35's "correct when written, rotted as the dictionary grew" mechanism arriving wholesale instead of one entry at a time. +4.5% on the mechanically-fixable set is small; the sequencing is the point. Applying it writes links whose **label is bare while the target headword carries a tilde**, which is exactly what `check_link_baseform.py` flags — so the affix-tilde relationship must join na-adjective (巨大な → 巨大) and する (参加する → 参加) normalization in [Tooling 83](../wiki/ideas/tooling-backlog.md) *before* the sweep runs, or it manufactures 159 new findings. Queue item `noentry-affix-tilde-forms` filed with `status: blocked` for that reason.

- **[C] `topics/furigana-wrapper-anomalies.md` deepened** with the mention-quote convention, the rule-(a)/rule-(b) split, and the live-site rendering evidence.

**Also filed**: **new Tooling 84** — `review_runner.py`'s `RATE_LIMIT_INTERVAL = 6.0` measured as the binding constraint on accuracy-review coverage. Screening managed **~6.6 entries/min** against `review_accuracy.py`'s **~19/min** — same model, same block, run *concurrently*, no rate-limit errors, $0.31 combined — which is the evidence the interval buys nothing. The consequence is structural, not academic: §A sizes a run at 400–600 entries in both dimensions, and at 6.6/min recent runs have covered their whole range on the accuracy side and **~62% on the furigana side**, a coverage gap created entirely by a constant. **New Tooling 85** — the §A 20%-noise heuristic misreads a tag-contaminated block: 221 of 534 entries flagged (41%) reads as noise, but 234 of 257 issues were tag flags and 156 correctly named a tag genuinely absent from `VALID_SEMANTIC`, which §A's own policy makes correct *by definition*; removing off-vocabulary tag flags first gives gloss 3.4% and translation 0.9%, inside the band the prompt already predicts. That converges independently on [Quality Metrics §14](topics/quality-metrics.md), which retired the blended `tags` precision figure in the same week for the same underlying reason — **two runs reaching it from opposite directions**, and both implying the same cheap triage axis: does this flag name a tag outside the closed list? **New Tooling 86** — Hangul outside a documented loanword etymology as a permanent one-line validator check (27316 had 평상시 in an English note; a whole-corpus scan found only 4 Hangul-bearing entries and the other three are legitimate キムチ/明太子/ナムル etymologies, so scope is 1 and the false-positive set is enumerable at 3). **Tooling 19** was filed a fifth and sixth time this run by two runs that did not know it existed, making it the longest-standing gap on that page between a sized item and its instrument — and the upstream dependency of P43's sequencing constraint. **P42** hit 8-of-8 and 7-of-7 (third and fourth consecutive 100%), with both runs independently restating the diagnosis: `score_note_quality` ranks these entries low *because the notes are good*. **P43** confirmed at the frontier (7 of 7 zero-link, ~4× normal per-entry cost, plus the batched-lookup technique that made seven affordable).

**Activity H not run**: 5 new metrics lines since the thirty-second refresh (454 → 459), below the ≥10 threshold.

**Routed to the curator**: the **proper-noun scope question** (C22806 夏目漱石, C22820 成田), now on its second filing — the dictionary contains no person or place names, so these can never be created and every new-entries run will keep re-picking them until a policy exists in the `find-candidates` skill. The **figure-form headword question** (30493 was created as {百十番|ひゃくとおばん} because an inline link referenced it that way, where the standard orthography is 110番) — the same question as the already-routed number+counter one, and it should be answered once for both. Carried forward: the taxonomy question (322 instances) and the frontier-versus-growth rate, a fifth time.

**Process note**: eleventh consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where the unit of measurement was a **backlog item rather than a claim**. Tooling 8's two rules were proposed in one sentence, from one entry, in June — and they sat unbuilt for seven weeks because the pair looked like one medium job. Sized separately, one is a 33-file afternoon whose defect is legible on the live site and the other should never be written. That is a different failure from the ones the last three harvests named: not an alarm gone stale, not a count read against the wrong unit, but **two items averaged into one estimate**, where the bundle's cost is the sum and its value is not. The rule to carry: **when an item bundles two detection rules, size them separately before scheduling either.** The corollary is what made rule (a) interesting rather than merely wrong — a detector that fires 931 times against a convention is not a bad detector, it is a **missing piece of documentation wearing a detector's clothes**, and the cheap fix is to write the convention down where the next session will read it.

## [2026-08-08] maintenance (Routine v2 `wiki`, run 2) | 23 observations harvested; five premises measured; metrics refresh #33

**Session type**: Scheduled Routine (`wiki` mode — "highest scheduler debt among eligible modes")

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 23 loose observations** from the 2026-08-08 polish run (priority + frontier lanes), the 2026-08-08 new-entries run, and the 2026-08-08 accuracy-review of 28351–28900. Five premises were measured against the whole corpus before filing, and four came back a different size or a different shape than proposed.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — a proposed detector that inverted its own diagnosis.** A polish run found 06835–06841 with fully-linked notes and completely bare examples, read it as "a creation-era batch signature", and proposed a detector for the rest of that block. Measured: **33 entries**, and the observing block is not among them — it was fixed in-run. What remains says the opposite of the diagnosis. **32 of the 33 sit *behind* the polish frontier** (`next: 06842`), in six tight consecutive runs — 06038–06047, 06457–06462, 06631–06638, 06669, 06723–06729 — plus a single outlier at 18725, and their `modified` stamps are polish-run dates (06631: 2026-07-26; 06723: 2026-08-08). These are not entries awaiting the frontier; they are entries the frontier **reached and half-finished**, each carrying 8–11 links in its notes while examples full of linkable vocabulary (06038 閉め出す: 鍵/忘れる/家/猫/外) carry none. The contiguity and the abrupt endings are a **session shape**, not a creation shape: a run that linked notes for a batch and ran out of context before the examples. Filed as **Cleanup P46**, and argued there as a *standing* check rather than a one-off sweep — this is the only class on the page that no cursor will ever revisit, because the cursor has already passed it.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — a rule that is already policy, and a false-positive family that is 54% of it.** Two entries splitting compounds across adjacent links (02274 フランス+語, 02646 筋肉+痛) prompted "a detector for any adjacent link pair whose concatenated surface matches an existing headword". Two things came back. First, **this is not a new idea — it is documented policy**: the `inline-word-links` skill's common-mistakes table has said "link as single compound if entry exists" since it was written, so the observation rediscovered a standard that simply has no instrument. Second, the naive rule is **not** batch-ready: it fires **1,579 times across 1,296 entries**, of which **847 are correct links** where the second element is a short kana grammatical element (に 291, する 204, も 96, の 54, な 45) — 前+に and 少し+ずつ are right, and merely happen to concatenate into a lexicalized entry. Requiring both halves to be ≥2 characters carves out the real class at **443 pairs / 391 entries** (写真+撮影, 身分+証明書, 最高+裁判所). Filed as **Cleanup P47**, with the 204-instance する sub-family split out for a separate decision, since reversing those changes reader-facing text on a scale the compound-noun case does not. Worth noting the shape: **precision split on the length and script of the second element**, the same axis that carved a clean batch out of the stale-`noentry` population on 2026-08-08.
