- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — a count confirmed exactly, over a population a third the reported size, containing a shape nobody saw.** "17 malformed `⟦surface→noentry⟧` links, concentrated in 064xx" measured at exactly **17 instances** — but across **7 entries**, not the implied seventeen. And two of the seventeen, both in **03022**, are `⟦{観光|かんこう}⟧`: **no arrow at all**, so the mechanical repair proposed for the family (re-insert the base form, which equals the de-furigana'd surface) fixes fifteen and would corrupt two. Filed as **Cleanup P48**. The observation's important half held completely: every `noentry` scan in the project anchors on the `→base：` segment these links are missing, so all 17 would have survived the P35 sweep **silently** — a detector's blind spot defined by the exact defect it is meant to find.

- **[A/F] Measurement 4 — a sound premise whose proposed method cannot work.** `domain: business` on 06838 余白 (margins, aesthetics) and 06839 逆転 (sports comebacks) prompted "worth a pass over `domain` tags the way `check_tag_drift.py` handles `semantic`". Measured: **3,593 domain instances across 3,278 entries**, `business` alone **1,162 (32%)** — the premise is right and the scale is large. But the method is unavailable, for a structural reason worth recording once: **every instrument that made the `semantic` cleanup tractable keys on off-vocabulary values, and `domain` has none.** All 3,593 are valid members of `VALID_DOMAIN`; they are merely wrong. `check_tag_drift.py` is blind here by construction, and so is the reviewer's highest-yield flag ("not in the valid tag list", ~97% precision). The only visible route is the reviewer's `tags` dimension, which already judges a semantic tag against the *headword* rather than the example topics, pointed at `domain` — filed as a 100-entry experiment, not a sized item.

- **[A/F] Measurement 5 — a "minor vocabulary addition" that is a taxonomy decision.** `VALID_SEMANTIC` has no category for sound or acoustic phenomena; an accuracy-review met three instances and filed it as a small addition. **896 entries have a gloss naming a sound, voice, noise or acoustic concept**, currently absorbed by catch-alls (`general` 92, `action` 77, `descriptive` 48). The net is loose and 896 is an upper bound, but it is enough to merge this with the spatial/positional and document-type gaps (322 instances) already with the curator: **one taxonomy decision, not three small ones.** Also sized in passing: the proposed `〜的` templated linker — **281 entries, 47 already linking `09839_teki`, 234 not** — recorded as an Informational sizing rather than a queue item, since only the skeleton-carrying subset takes all five links positionally.

- **[H] Quality-metrics refresh #33** (12 new lines, 454 → 466) written as **Finding §15**. Two headlines, and both are cases of a number driven by its population rather than by the process it names. **The frontier-versus-growth gap broke its four-refresh 44–50% band to 76%** — but the frontier column barely moved (19, 29, 26, **29** IDs across four windows); what changed is that new-entry growth fell 59 → 38 because the "seen in entry" candidate lane drained empty. Since that lane refills from comprehensive polish, **the mechanism that closed the gap is the one that will reopen it**, so 44–50% stays the planning number. **§14's mixture-weight reading reproduced with the prediction made first**: pointed at a 43%-contaminated block (236 of 549 entries in 28351–28900), the blended `tags` figure rose 71.0% → **76.2%** while its components held — off-vocabulary migration **97.2%** [495/14], in-list-and-other **5.0%** [7/134], formality **18.2%** [2/9]. Two windows, opposite cohort densities, same decomposition: confirmed rather than proposed. Also: **third consecutive record review-queue floor (10,297, −839)**; escalations light (319 → 323); OpenRouter peak **$0.551** of $5, the eleventh straight refresh at which budget is not the binding constraint. Logged one honest caveat in §15 — this refresh classified the `tags` families by keyword over free-text notes, so its 5.0% is not strictly comparable to last refresh's 28.1%, and a `"fam"` key on `tags` lines in `decisions.jsonl` would remove the classifier for the cost of one field (logged as a `[tooling]` observation).

**Also filed**: **new Cleanup P49** — wrong furigana inside inline-link *surfaces*, **40 pairs**, found *by accident* by `check_stale_noentry.py`'s class R (来春/らいはる, 農作物/のうさくもつ, 墓石/はかいし, 完全試合/かんぜんしあい, 白和え/しろあえ, 部屋干し/へやほし, 言い及ぶ as `{言|い}{及|およ}ぶ`). This is the **third confirmed member of the Tooling 47 family** — after P36 (headword field) and Tooling 47 itself (cross-reference headwords) — where a field outside `examples[].japanese` and `notes` falls through every furigana instrument the project owns, so the item argues for folding link surfaces into the instruments rather than hand-fixing 40 and waiting for the fourth member. **New Tooling 87**: `review_accuracy.py` parallel workers, measured at **~2.4 entries/min serial against ~30/min across four processes** with no rate-limit errors (better than 4× because the serial path is latency-bound, not quota-bound). **New Tooling 88**: `get_next_id.py` should print the target directory — the 500-boundary trap cost a session when 30495–30499 were written into `entries/30500/`. **Updates**: **P11** took its largest single migration yet (236 of 549 entries, 270 instances, 101 distinct invented labels) with **1,902 entries dictionary-wide still carrying baselined off-vocab tags**; **Tooling 84** gained a second and larger bound — running screening and accuracy concurrently on the same model starves the screener to **~1.2 entries/min** against ~19, so §A must run them sequentially or on distinct models, and Tooling 87 must land with a shared throttle rather than on its own; **reviewer formality noise 5 of 5** for a third window with §A's notes-contradiction guard holding perfectly; **P43** at 9 of 9; **P42** reinforced by six priority-lane entries with zero cross-references.

**Routed to the curator**: the **proper-noun scope question**, now on its **third** filing and now with evidence against the policy rather than merely blocked by it — 28138 横浜, 28139 札幌, 28144 オーストラリア, 28145 青森県, 28283 イギリス and 28300 中国 are live entries that all carried off-vocabulary `place-name`/`country` tags, so the corpus contains place names while the standing note says it does not, and C22806/C22820 will be re-picked by every new-entries run until the two are reconciled. The **taxonomy question**, now three gaps rather than two. The **frontier-versus-growth rate**, a sixth time — explicitly *not* closed by this window's 76%.

**Process note**: twelfth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which a measurement **inverted an observation's causal story rather than resizing it**. The bare-examples finding was reported as a creation-era batch signature — a reasonable read from inside a single block — and the corpus said the opposite: every instance but one is behind the frontier, in runs that end abruptly, with polish-run timestamps. That distinction is not academic, because the two diagnoses imply opposite work. A creation-era defect is a one-off sweep that the advancing frontier eventually makes moot; a session-shape defect is permanent unless the *process* changes, since no cursor returns to completed ground. The rule to carry: **when an observation attributes a defect to a batch, check the `modified` stamps before believing it** — the cheapest available test of whether you are looking at what created the entries or at what last touched them.

## [2026-08-09] maintenance (Routine v2 `wiki`) | 24 observations harvested; six premises measured; two detectors retired

**Session type**: Scheduled Routine (`wiki` mode — "highest scheduler debt among eligible modes")

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 24 loose observations** from the 2026-08-09 polish run, the 2026-08-09 accuracy-review of 28901–29294, and the 2026-08-09 systemic-fix run (the 180-pair stale-`noentry` sweep). Six premises were measured against the whole corpus before filing: two were retired outright, two were re-scoped by an order of magnitude, one was cut from 82 to 16, and one confirmed exactly.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — the same detector rediscovered for the sixth and seventh time, and the split that finally made it an item.** Two independent observations this run proposed "a detector for entries with Japanese examples containing no ⟦⟧ at all — a pure absence test, no judgment needed." Measured: **23,404 of 30,316 entries (77%)**. That is the polish frontier restated, and `topics/inline-link-integrity.md` has carried a standing **"do not file a zero-link detector"** since 2026-08-07. What is new is that this pair of filings prompted splitting the scan at the cursor instead of reporting the total — and **55 entries sit *behind* `next: 06845` with zero links**, work the frontier reached and left bare, which no cursor will ever revisit. Filed as **Cleanup P50**, the strict sibling of P46 (half-linked) — P46 is linking half-done, P50 is linking never-started, both behind the frontier. The 55 are near-entirely contiguous blocks: **21 of them are the single-kanji `〜` run 03949–03969**, and unlike P46's abruptly-ending blocks that batch is skipped *completely*, which points at a different cause — a linking pass that asks "is the headword linkable" rather than "are the examples linkable" would skip bound-morpheme headwords (空〜, 元〜, 後〜) as a unit. Their examples are ordinary and full of linkable vocabulary. Generalised on the topic page: **when a coverage detector returns most of the dictionary, the useful query is that detector intersected with a cursor position** — "77% of entries are unlinked" is a restatement of the schedule; "55 entries the schedule already passed are unlinked" is a bug report.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — a backlog size confirmed exactly, and the proposed method measured out of contention.** The accuracy-review proposed extending `check_tag_drift.py`'s `TAG_MIGRATION` with the eight off-vocabulary tag families it met, "so a systemic-fix run can sweep the rest of the dictionary." The size confirmed (**1,848 entries / 2,436 uses**, tracking the 08-08 figure of 1,902 downward), but the shape of the vocabulary defeats the method: the uses spread over **643 distinct invented labels**, **300 of them used exactly once**, 486 used ≤3 times. The current 9 rules cover **7%** of uses; the ten families named cover **8%**; the curve reaches 29% at 25 labels, 43% at 50, 59% at 100, and 75% only at **200 hand-written rules**. This does not retire the mechanical path, it re-scopes it: extend the map with the top ~25 labels because that is nearly free, and plan the ~70% tail as accuracy-review coverage — the reviewer's off-vocabulary flag runs at ~97% apply rate on this exact class and names a destination per instance **without anyone enumerating the vocabulary in advance**, which is the property a 643-label long tail actually requires.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — the fourth detector killed by an undocumented convention, and it would have caused a regression.** Entry 28929's `{苦悶|くもん}{に|み}ちた` (kanji 満 dropped entirely) prompted "a detector for a wrapper whose left side contains no kanji is a cheap, high-precision check" — which follows directly from the project's own stated rule that the pre-pipe segment is kanji only. Measured across all **1,110,639** wrappers: **391 hits, at most 3 of them real — under 1% precision**, and the trigger case is absent because the filing run fixed it. The residue is the interesting part. 65 are harmless identity wrappers, 276 are the katakana class, and **47 are a fourth undocumented brace convention**: numerals are wrapped to supply their spoken reading (`{1990|せんきゅうひゃくきゅうじゅう}`, `{400|よんひゃく}`, `{3|さん}`, `{〇|まる}`, `{々|おの}`) — which is furigana doing exactly its job on a surface that happens not to be kanji. A mechanical sweep on this rule would have **stripped genuine reading information from arabic numerals**: a regression, not a cleanup. **RETIRED**, with the convention written down on `topics/furigana-wrapper-anomalies.md` and documenting the two brace extensions (mention-quotes + numeral readings, 1,131 spans between them) filed as the highest-value fix on that page — it is the only one that prevents work rather than performing it.

- **[A/F] Measurement 4 — the one rule that survived, and the one that needs a decision first.** Inside the retired proposal sits a rule with **no false-positive family**: a wrapper with an empty side. Corpus-wide that is **3 instances** (`00961 {どこ|}`, `23799 {キャンプ|}`, `25376 {うまく|}`), all malformed, none visible to any current instrument — queued as a permanent `check_furigana_format.py` line rather than as a sweep, because the value is the regex, not the three fixes. Separately, the observation's "274 katakana wrappers with hiragana readings" confirmed at **276 / 230 entries / 208 distinct surfaces**, with only 2 identity cases — consistent deliberate behaviour, not sloppiness, sitting between two project rules that disagree (*readings are always hiragana*: satisfied; *furigana annotate kanji*: violated). Queued as **blocked on a curator policy decision**, explicitly not as cleanup: sweeping ahead of the convention is the exact failure this page has now documented four times.

- **[C/F] Measurement 5 — 82 down to 16, and a name for the error.** A polish run fixed 00806 両親 (tagged `formality: formal` + `politeness: honorific`, although the honorific form is ご両親 and 両親 itself is plain) and suspected a family on other kinship terms. The obvious rule — honorific/humble with no お/ご prefix — returns **82 entries and is mostly right to**: 申す, いらっしゃる, いたす, 参る, 召し上がる are suppletive keigo verbs that take no prefix by nature, so prefix-absence is evidence of nothing. Adding "POS is a noun" and "the gloss does not itself describe deferential use" narrows it to **16**, splitting cleanly: 陛下, 閣下, 寸志, 高配, 引き立て are genuine, while **利用者 "user", 尊敬 "respect", 謙遜 "modesty", 配偶者 "spouse"** are the 両親 error repeated. Written up on `topics/register.md` under the name that makes it checkable: **the `politeness` tag conflates *encoding* deference with *denoting* it** — a noun meaning "respect" is not respectful language, any more than the English noun *politeness* is polite — with 利用者 and 配偶者 showing a third variant, contamination by the register of the word's typical habitat. This is the register-axis twin of the meaning-versus-use confusion `schema-tag-reliability.md` documents for semantic tags (財布 tagged `clothing` because wallets live in pockets). Filed as open, 16 entries, explicitly **not** batch-ready.

- **[E] Refreshed the zero-link band table and its two-day delta.** Re-measured with the frontier at 06845 against the 2026-08-07 figures at 06723: the 06000–07999 band shed **118** zero-link entries (1,202 → 1,084) — the lane working exactly as designed — while the 30000+ band grew from 298 entries to **526**, all unlinked. Net: **23,294 → 23,404, up 110 while the frontier advanced 122 IDs.** That is the frontier-versus-growth gap expressed on the link metric for the first time, and it lands on the pessimistic side of the 44–50% band: the linking deficit is not merely failing to shrink, it is growing, and it would still be growing if the frontier ran twice as fast. No change to the standing answer (Tooling 49/82, a read-only link *suggester*, not a detector).

**Also filed**: **new Tooling 89** — an explicit `"fam"` key on `tags` lines in `reviews/decisions.jsonl`. The off-vocab / in-list / formality decomposition is the headline of two consecutive metrics refreshes and is currently **re-derived each time by keyword-matching a ≤10-word free-text note written by a different run**, which is why the in-list figure moved 28.1% → 5.0% between refreshes without the underlying rate necessarily moving; one controlled field under §C's existing lowercase discipline removes the classifier entirely, and no backfill is needed. **New Tooling 90** — `review_accuracy.py --dimensions links`: §4 sends whole *entries* to the reviewer, so on a link-only run every flag returned is pre-existing content and **the change under test is never examined**. The 2026-08-09 systemic-fix run measured both halves: the pass was useful (6 real pre-existing tag/translation errors surfaced) and it was not verification (180 link edits merged with zero independent review). The principle generalises — **§4's unit should be the change, not the entry**. **New Tooling 91** — the **GitHub MCP check-run status is cached and can be ~30 minutes stale**: on PR #3152 `validate` completed `success` 67 seconds after starting while `get_check_runs` reported `in_progress` for half an hour, with `actions_get`'s `updated_at` frozen at the run's start. A Routine polling to the documented ~8-minute cap therefore abandons a **green** PR, which is a direct measured cause of the stranded-PR class §0a exists to clean up; three mitigations proposed, cheapest first (treat a frozen `updated_at` as "no fresh data" rather than "still running"). **Updates**: **Tooling 27** — off-vocabulary tags print only under `--verbose`, collapsing 11,013 warnings to a single integer, which is why the largest tag backlog on the page stayed invisible; the 643-label distribution is precisely what a human cannot see without the tool printing it. **Tooling 17** — in-list substitution flags **0 of 12** applied, twelfth-plus consecutive confirmation, prescription unchanged and still unshipped. **Tooling 84** — a backgrounded `review_runner.py` **survived `pkill -f`** and kept writing result files *after* the run's `git add -A`, stranding 25 artifacts outside the PR; only `kill -9` stopped it, so "process gone" and "output complete" are independent facts and the durable fix is a `--pidfile` or a staging directory promoted on clean exit. **P35** — three independent sightings in one day (05834 しょげる, 01910 勃発する, 04262 擦れる), all in entries the sequential frontier had already passed; current detector state 6,026 instances / 5,348 pairs / 2,689 entries with mechanical A1+A2 at 1,423 pairs, plus the method note that **ID-order sweeps beat detector-order ones** because consecutive entries repeat the same families (180 pairs verified at zero false positives).

**Routed to the curator**: the **katakana-wrapper convention** (276 instances, blocked pending a decision — the instrument must not run first). The **連体詞 category question** raised by 02992 色んな, which has no schema slot and affects こんな/そんな/大きな/あらゆる as a class. The **proper-noun scope contradiction**, now on its **fourth** filing. And the **link-coverage rate**, which this run measured moving the *wrong way* for the first time.

**Not run**: **[H] quality-metrics refresh** — 6 new lines (466 → 472), below the ≥10 threshold. Next `wiki` run should expect it to be due.

**Process note**: thirteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which the measurement's value came from **where a detector was pointed rather than whether it was right**. The zero-link detector was proposed for the sixth and seventh time this run, against a page that already says in bold not to file it — and the correct response turned out not to be a stricter warning but a **filter**: intersected with the polish cursor, the same 23,404-hit scan yields 55 entries that are unambiguously bugs. Six earlier filings all reported the total and were all dismissed. The rule to carry: **a coverage detector that returns most of the corpus has not failed; it has been asked a question about the schedule instead of about the entries.** Intersect it with whatever cursor governs the work before concluding it is useless. The corollary, from measurement 3, cuts the other way and is worth holding at the same time: **a detector derived from the documentation rather than from the corpus should be measured before it is filed**, because the documentation describes the notation's intended core while the corpus has grown deliberate extensions around it that nobody wrote down — four proposals in three months have now died on exactly that gap.

## [2026-08-10] maintenance (Routine v2 `wiki`) | 27 observations harvested; seven premises measured; the queue metric re-read

**Session type**: Scheduled Routine (`wiki` mode — "highest scheduler debt among eligible modes")

**Activities**:

- **[F] Harvested all 27 loose observations** from the 2026-08-09/10 polish, accuracy-review and new-entries runs (17 `[pattern]`, 6 `[tooling]`, 4 `[entry]`). Seven premises were measured against the whole corpus before filing: two confirmed to the entry, one was bounded from "dictionary-wide convention" down to 37, one measured 4.5× larger than filed, one was retired, and one proposed instrument was answered by a re-scan that made it unnecessary.

- **[A/F] Measurement 1 — a genuinely mechanical sweep, and the class behind it.** "29 entries link the calendar-month suffix 月(がつ) at `02230_tsuki` (the *moon*)" measured at **exactly 29**. It is one of the rare items that earns §B's purely-mechanical carve-out: がつ is unambiguously the counter-suffix reading, so the surface cannot denote the entry it names. Filed as **Cleanup P51**. The cause is worth more than the fix — `30418_gatsu` was created *after* those links were written, so every link authored in the interval had nowhere correct to point, and that trap is re-set whenever a suffix, counter, or bound-morpheme entry postdates the homographic free noun it shares a kanji with. Generalised as **Tooling 97**: compare each inline link's surface reading against its target entry's `reading` — a pure string comparison over data the build already loads, which finds the family as a class instead of one filing at a time.

- **[A/F] Measurement 2 — 259 confirmed exactly, and the blind spot confirmed in the source.** `build/find_missing_furigana.py` reads `headword` at line 101 **only to label its output** and never appends it to `fields_to_scan`, so `make check-furigana` reports a bare-kanji headword as clean. Filed as **Cleanup P52** + **Tooling 96** (one line). Two things the filing did not have: severity is **presentational, not data loss** — the reading still renders on its own `entry-reading` line, what is lost is ruby and per-kanji attribution on compounds — and this is an **active regression**, with 126 of the 259 created in 2026-07 and 24 in the first ten days of 2026-08 (~70/month). A sweep shipped without the validator fix would be re-filed within a quarter, which is the whole argument for pairing them.

- **[A/F] Measurement 3 — the proposed instrument answered by the re-scan that would have built it.** Two observations (filed independently, on 06845–06849 and 06850–06855) proposed "a detector that reports contiguous runs of zero-link entries so systemic-fix can target the densest block." Their premise is right — the population **is** creation-batch-shaped. The prescription is not: the 23,420 zero-link entries decompose into **34 runs, three of which cover 96.9%** (18726–30554: 11,810; 09809–18724: 8,847; 07442–09477: 2,036). The detector renders "everything above the frontier" in three rows instead of 23,420. Eighth and ninth rediscovery of the standing "do not file a zero-link detector" instruction — but the cursor split, now applied as routine, brings news worth having: **zero-link entries behind the frontier hold at exactly 55**, identical to 2026-08-09, with **zero** of the 15 IDs crossed since entering the set. **P50 is a fixed, non-growing queue**: the polish lane links what it passes, so clearing it once clears it permanently.

- **[A/F] Measurement 4 — the health metric four refreshes have led with is a third padding.** "`queue.txt` does not converge because it is not deduped against review recency" was filed with an estimate of 776 already-current entries. Measured: **3,513 of 9,932 (35.4%)** carry an accuracy report *newer* than the entry's last modification; 6,187 are genuinely stale; 232 have no report at all. Much of the padding is self-inflicted — the §4 at-birth self-check reviews every new entry and CI then queues those same entries for the review they just had. Filed as **Tooling 94** (one predicate, `reviewed_at >= modified`, at two call sites) and carried into the metrics page as the headline caveat of the 34th refresh. The trend direction survives, since the padding is roughly proportional; the level does not.

- **[A/F] Measurement 5 — re-litigation is structural, and suppression is not enough.** "26 of 26 flagged entries already had a decision" measured corpus-wide at **4,479 of 10,613 open (entry, dimension) pairs (42.2%)**, with **1,081 (10.2%)** already carrying a REJECT. Filed as **Tooling 93**, with the filing's own two options separated by coverage: suppressing prior-REJECT pairs handles 10%, but **both documented oscillations — 29451 塁打 and 29634 ネガティブ, where a 2026-08 reviewer asked to revert corrections deliberately applied in 2026-06 and 2026-07 — were prior APPLYs**, which only the "feed prior decisions into the prompt" option reaches. Ship the filter, then the prompt.

- **[A/F] Measurement 6 — a deferral reversed by its own size.** The compound-verb notes preamble (three lines restating negative / te-form / past, already rendered from the `conjugation` field) was left in place by the polish run "to avoid inconsistency with the hundreds of entries that still carry it." Measured: **37 entries**. It is a creation-batch habit, not a house style, so either decision executes in a single run. Filed as **Cleanup P54**, `needs-decision` — parked on a curator call rather than on cost.

- **[E/F] Measurement 7 — RETIRED: "formality `formal` with no supporting REGISTER note."** Measured at **4,357 of 5,079 `formal` entries (85.8%)**. Third instance of the standing rule that a detector returning most of its population is measuring a documentation habit rather than a defect — most entries simply do not write REGISTER sections. The discriminating rule is the one §A already applies (flag only where the entry's *own notes* contradict the label), which needs no new instrument. Worth noting that the filing run had itself checked the band's `formal` share (14% against the dictionary's 16.7%) and concluded the block was not contaminated: the right check, reaching the right answer, before filing.

- **[H] Quality-metrics 34th refresh** (14 new lines, 466 → 480 — first time over the ≥10 threshold in three windows). Three findings. **(1)** New review-queue floor **9,832**, the first reading under 10,000 and a fourth consecutive record — published alongside Measurement 4's finding that 35% of the queue is already current, so the floor is real and the level is not. **(2)** The frontier-versus-growth gap **reopened to 42%** (+43 entries, +18 frontier IDs) from 76%, exactly as the 33rd refresh predicted and for the predicted reason: supply recovered (38 → 43) while the frontier slowed (29 → 18). The frontier's absolute rate is the stable quantity; the ratio tracks candidate supply, so reading it as a productivity signal produces false alarms in both directions. **(3)** **§14 reproduces a third time, one level deeper.** §14 retired the blended `tags` figure in favour of per-family rates; this window the *in-list* family ran **65.9%** applied against **5.0%** last window — not a reviewer thirteen times better, but the sweep arriving at the never-reviewed 06926–07265 band (147 flags on 135 of 240 entries, 56%). The correct unit of precision is **family × band review history**, not family.

**Also filed. Cleanup**: **P53** (counter entries with an empty `cross_references` array — 39 of 79 after the polish run fixed the basic-tier four; the counters are the project's cleanest closed set and each entry's notes already name its own contrasts). **P11 update**: the contaminated band extends into **06926–07265**, and **none of the wrong tags is off-vocabulary** — 司書 tagged `clothing`, カビ `weather`, 打率 `animal-mammal` are all legal labels on the wrong words, so `validate_tags.py` and the `--check-no-new-unknown` ratchet are blind to the entire class. That is the argument for a dedicated systemic-fix pass over 07266+ rather than letting the accuracy sweep crawl there at ~240 entries/run. **P35 update**: two more stale markers, plus the class the detector *cannot* see — エリア in 04438, naked with no marker at all, which is why **Tooling 98** (a katakana-run detector, tokenizer-free by construction) is the tractable slice of the unlinked-word problem.

**Tooling**: **92 — the §7 CI-wait loop does not wait.** A backgrounded Bash call returns its tool result immediately, so a run following the documented "`sleep 30` with `run_in_background: true`, re-poll when it returns" procedure literally waits **zero seconds** between polls and burns its whole ~16-poll budget in under a minute, against CI that needs 60–90 s to run and often minutes to start. Verified on PR #3156 (check ran 15:32:04→15:33:12; all eight polls fell in 15:32–15:35, every reading accurate). **This supersedes Tooling 91** — the "MCP check-run cache is ~30 minutes stale" report came from a run following the same zero-wait procedure, so 91 is downgraded to unconfirmed pending a sighting with timestamps that rule this out. Also **95** (constrain the reviewer's tag *suggestion* field to `VALID_SEMANTIC` and tell it `general` is a legal fallback — the sole-`general` and formality families are ~27% and ~10% of tag volume at 11.1% and 12.5% apply, while the asymmetric case, a flag that the *entry's existing* tag is off-vocabulary, remains the project's highest-precision signal at 88.5%); **99** (screening and accuracy contend for one rate limit — 28 entries/40 min against ~200 — so run them sequentially, and prefer accuracy when choosing); **100** (`manage_candidates.py add` should run the duplicate check itself, without `--skip-candidates`: 4 of 20 "seen in entry" candidates were variant-orthography duplicates, and fixing the tool beats instructing every prompt that captures candidates).

**Entry Follow-ups**: 01328 内, whose ex13 and ex17 use 内 for a physical interior directly against the entry's own note that 中 is required there; the conflict/dispute cluster (01450 戦争, 04500 対立, 09992 争い) left for one consistent decision after 05758 紛争 was fixed, which is the right instinct and worth generalising — near-synonym clusters should be retagged together or not at all; transitive/intransitive leakage in the 06850s block (06853 焼き付ける fixed with its missing `pair` link, neighbours unchecked); {湿疹|しっしん} and {音符|おんぷ} as the concrete two-entry version of the sole-`general` policy question; the proper-noun scope question, **fifth filing**.

**Routed to the curator**: the compound-verb preamble decision (37 entries, cheap either way); the sole-`general` policy, where 40 rejected proposals per sweep are either 40 reviewer errors or a policy drawn too broadly; the candidate-pool contamination, filed twice this window with a `new-entries` run again unable to reach 20 entries (the ask is a `clean_up_candidates_list.md` pass); and the proper-noun scope contradiction, now re-filed roughly once every two days.

**Process note**: fourteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where a measurement turned on the project's *own* instruments rather than a proposed one. Measurements 4 and 5 both found the same shape: a number the project has been publishing (queue depth) and a ledger the project has been keeping (decisions) are each doing about two-thirds of the job they are assumed to do, for want of one predicate. The rule to carry: **measure the instruments you already trust at the same cadence as the detectors you are asked to build.** A proposed detector gets scrutinised because someone has to write it; an existing metric gets quoted. Four consecutive refreshes led with a queue floor before anyone asked what the queue was counting.

## [2026-08-11] maintenance | Two filed findings fail verification; proper nouns enter scope

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 31 unharvested observation lines, 27 real observations). No entries changed, so §4 self-verification does not apply and `make build` is skipped.

**Activities**: [F] harvest, [A] project sync, [C] deepen `topics/instrument-defects.md`, [E] verify-before-filing. Activity [H] deliberately skipped: `metrics-history.jsonl` is at 487 lines against the 34th refresh's 480, i.e. **7 new lines against the ≥10 threshold** — the next harvest should run it.

**The headline is that two of the 27 observations were wrong, and both were wrong in the same direction: an instrument was blamed for, or credited with, something it did not do.**

- **[E/F] "A contiguous block of 25 entries (07832–07861) with no semantic tags at all" — does not exist.** Re-measured across all 30,365 entry files: **zero** such entries anywhere in 07566–08065. The dictionary-wide population is **79** (19 basic / 4 core / 56 general), down from 105 on 2026-08-04, and its real clusters are 08635–08659 (13), 03948–03969 (12), 02814–02924 (10), 08812–08840 (10). The cause is in the reporting run's *own* `[pattern]` note, filed a paragraph away: its census read `entry["tags"]` where tags live at `entry["metadata"]["tags"]`, and the wrong path returns `{}` silently. The same bug made that run report "0 entries with off-vocabulary tags" for a band that had **208**. One buggy census produced a false negative *and* a false positive in a single run, and both were filed as findings — which is the argument for **Tooling 101**: a census must assert it found the field before it reports a zero, because a zero that cannot distinguish "nothing there" from "looked in the wrong place" enters the backlog as fact.

- **[C/E] "The furigana screener still truncates readings" — the screener was fixed twelve days ago; its *stored output* was not.** Three sweeps have now reported the truncated-reading family as live, most recently as "85 flags across 60 entries, zero true positives, third consecutive sweep at ~0%", with the recommendation to fix the prompt builder before spending further. Opening the instrument (which is what this page exists to prescribe): `trim_context()` is correct, and the pair itself was never windowed — it comes from the regex match. The quoted flag sits in `reviews/screening/07586.json` dated **2026-06-19**, six weeks before the 2026-07-30 fix. Measured across the whole cache: **19,368 of 22,822 stored results (85%) predate the fix**, carrying 1,950 flags; post-fix results flag at **4.9%** against **10.1%** before. So the fix worked and halved the rate, and the halving was in the data the whole time. The cost is ongoing: **every "post-fix precision" figure on record is contaminated**, including the "0 applied of ~158 flags across eight consecutive runs" that the retire-the-screener case rests on, because each of those runs adjudicated a majority of pre-fix flags in a proportion nobody measured. Filed as **instrument-defects case 8** — a genuinely new shape for that page, since in all seven prior cases fixing the instrument ended the defect, whereas here the instrument's output had been *persisted* and outlived the code that produced it. **Tooling 102** is the fix (version-stamp the cache; treat unstamped results as absent) and **Tooling 24**'s retire-or-downsample decision is explicitly marked not-yet-evaluable.

**[A] The 2026-08-11 curator decisions are now written into the project pages.** Proper nouns are **in scope** — place / person / organization / work / event / brand names, collocationally and semantically rich ones prioritized, carrying `proper-noun` plus a type tag as ordinary entries. What made this worth more than a note is that it answers a design question this wiki had been posing as hard: `open-issues.md` argued the schema "doesn't fit" and a lighter encyclopedic format would be needed, and `dictionary-growth.md` listed relaxed validation, a separate schema, and an `entry_type` field as the options. The decision took **none** of them. The objection dissolved once the encyclopedic half was separated out — an entry for 夏目漱石 documents *the name as a word*, and the biography goes in an expository article — so the schema never needed the fields. Recorded as a decision table in `dictionary-growth.md`, as RESOLVED in `open-issues.md`, and as the answer to the person-name question in `vocabulary-tiers.md` (**sixth filing** of that question; the cost of an open scope question is paid once per run that meets it). The `entry-followups.md` thread closes.

**[A] The candidate-quality issue closes with it.** The corpus-harvested bulk (~970 of 984 rows) was purged rather than filtered, discovery moved to per-word vetting (`prompts/newcandidates.md`), and the consequence worth recording separately is a *signal* repair: before the purge `routine_next.py` counted raw rows and reported "candidates plentiful" on a pool with ~13 usable items, which is why a quality-weighted count was requested. Post-purge every row is vetted, so **the raw count is the usable count** and the ask is resolved by construction — but only for as long as bulk harvesting stays retired.

**Also filed. Cleanup**: **P20 vs P11 separated explicitly** — off-vocabulary tags (not in `VALID_SEMANTIC`; mechanically detectable; invisible to CI only because `--check-no-new-unknown` grandfathers the baseline) versus in-vocabulary wrong-category tags (legal labels on the wrong words; only judgment finds them). Same cause — a creating session invents a local taxonomy per topic batch — visible in 07472–07481 kitchen cuts, 07482–07494 office documents, 07495–07501 home/utilities. Re-measured post-migration: **2,065 unknown-semantic flags across 1,603 entries**; total drift 6,720 / 6,220 (sole-general 3,681, semantic-mismatch 957). P11 confirmed continuous 06926→07430+ (57 corrected, second consecutive band at ~30%). Both classes point at the same conclusion from opposite directions: **one dedicated `systemic-fix` sweep, not seven more per-band accuracy reviews.** **P43** took a third confirming cohort — 06865–06875 arrived with zero link coverage in examples *and* notes, plus 5 sole-`general` tags and 5 entries naming a synonym or antonym in notes with an empty `cross_references`: three backlog items, one creation cohort. **Formality-vs-REGISTER** recorded as a *cost* argument (19/19 adjudications reproducible from the entry's own REGISTER sentence) that does **not** overturn the 2026-08-10 finding — the rule was already right; it is deterministic where a REGISTER section exists, and only 555 of 5,068 `formal` entries have one, so it stays a ratchet rather than a sweep.

**Tooling**: **101** (censuses must assert the field was found), **102** (expire stored screening results on screener change; re-read the entry before paying for a deep pass), **103** (`check_stale_noentry.py` `proper_name_risk` column — ⟦朝日⟧ in 朝日新聞 resolving to "morning sun" is a family no reading check can see), **104** (the copula te-form で linked as the particle で; the attributive な after na-adjectives linked inconsistently — plus the `inline-word-links` skill gap on single-kanji morphemes cited in FORMATION sections, recorded not made). Updates to **24**, **77**, **90**, **94**, **95**.

**Entry Follow-ups**: 03515 日光 — add the Nikko sense to the existing entry rather than create a second one; and the prohibitive な (「言うな」), which **cannot be queued at all** because `check_duplicate.py` sees an exact word+reading match against 09497_na, the na-adjective copula — an unrelated morpheme on the same surface. Two members of one class now (日光 is the same collision), which is what makes the homograph override worth building rather than working around.

**Routed to the curator**: the "`general` is too broad" reviewer-flag family — ~25–30% of tag-flag volume every sweep, rejected wholesale by standing policy, **fifth filing**. Escalated as a two-option decision (teach the prompt that `general` is a legal fallback, or rule it unacceptable as a sole tag and hand the whole family to the free `--check sole-general` detector) rather than as a sixth observation, since three separate runs have now written that it needs a decision and not another filing.

**Process note**: fifteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which the verification **overturned** filed findings rather than resizing them. The rule the last harvest proposed — *measure the instruments you already trust at the same cadence as the detectors you are asked to build* — held twice here, and case 8 extends it: **a fixed instrument is not the same as fixed output.** Everything the screener wrote before 2026-07-30 is still sitting in `reviews/screening/`, still being read, still being counted against the current code. The general form is that cached judgment is a claim about a program that may no longer exist, so it needs a version stamp or it should be treated as absent.

## [2026-08-12] maintenance | Nothing new filed: two re-discoveries, three retirements, and a metric retired as a headline

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 26 unharvested observations from the 2026-08-11/12 polish, candidates, new-entries, systemic-fix and accuracy-review runs). No entries changed, so §4 self-verification does not apply and `make build` is skipped.

**Activities**: [F] harvest, [E] verify-before-filing, [A] backlog-queue sync, [H] quality-metrics 35th refresh (496 lines against the 34th refresh's 480 — the ≥10 threshold the last harvest deferred).

**The headline is that this harvest filed no new priority.** Sixteen consecutive harvests have measured premises before filing them; this is the first where the measurement's usual outcome — resize the finding — was replaced by *the finding already exists, open and batch-ready*.

- **[E/F] The braced inline-link base forms are `inline-link-braced-base-form`, found for the fourth time.** Filed as "37 entry files … a clean, mechanical systemic-fix candidate"; measured at **35** (00704 fixed in-run), in two closed 2026-01 creation cohorts — 00697–00716 and 00965–00988 — plus three strays. The queue item has been open since 2026-07-25 and was **promoted to priority 5 on 2026-07-30 precisely because three separate polish runs had already found it independently**, with notes that already recorded the same clustering. Nineteen days and a fourth re-discovery later it has been worked zero times. The number is not the news; a provably-safe, user-visible, 35-entry, one-regex item sitting unworked at priority 5 is a **selector** question, and it is escalated as one rather than re-filed a fifth time.

- **[E/F] The zero-link idiom block is P43's, and the frontier is now inside it.** Filed twice in a day, as "06893 to about 06905" (13 entries) and as "06884–06887 and probably the surrounding range". Measured over 06880–06980: **79 of 101 IDs carry no inline link anywhere**, and the unbroken run begins at **06896** — the current value of `polishing/tasks/comprehensive/progress.txt`. `inline-link-block-06800-07100` was filed on 2026-08-06 on a *prediction* ("the frontier crawls through it at roughly one-fifth normal speed"); the filing run measured the same cost from inside the block ("one entry per 8–10 minutes"). Prediction and observation agree, so the routing decision that item deferred is live with a deadline: hand 06896–07100 to a systemic-fix link pass, or accept that every polish run for roughly six runs spends its whole budget there.

- **[E] Three closures.** (1) **The katakana `word_id_lookup` gap does not exist**: `by_reading['べてらん']` resolves, and `by_headword['ベテラン']` returns a record whose `reading` field is the hiragana the schema wants. What failed was a katakana query against a hiragana-keyed index — the second usage-error-filed-as-defect in two harvests, and the rule worth carrying is that **a filing against a data file should quote the lookup that failed**. This also removes the katakana premise from the P43 filter. (2) The **`style: ["literary"]` template hypothesis** measured **zero** entries in 06800–06999. (3) **Extending `TAG_MIGRATION` is closed, not filed** — see below.

- **[A/F] P20 re-scoped, and the obvious inference declined.** Dictionary-wide: **1,364 entries / 1,635 instances / 486 distinct off-vocabulary labels** (down from 1,603 entries after the 2026-08-12 migration of 189). The concentration hypothesis the filing asked to test is **confirmed but mislocated** — it measured 8066–8565 at 68%, a band straddling the 08000–08499 hot spot (40%) and the clean 08500–08999 block (7%); the real hot spots are **09000–09499 at 48%** and 08000–08499, holding **32% of the whole population** between them, while **23 of 62 blocks are completely clean**. Then the measurement that decides the plan: `TAG_MIGRATION`'s nine rows fully clear **111 of 1,364 entries (8.1%)**. The natural inference — extend the map — has been proposed four times and was **decided against on 2026-08-07** after measurement (a static map picks a destination per *tag name*, 486 of them with 243 singletons; the reviewer picks one per *entry* at 99.4% precision and ~$0.5/1,000). So 8.1% is that decision confirmed from the other side, and the actionable consequence is **routing** — point the reviewer at the two dense blocks — not tooling. Recorded so the fifth proposal meets the decision instead of the temptation.

- **[F] The best news came out of a reported blind spot.** `check_artifacts.py` reports 37 missing-`target_id` instances where a direct scan finds 97; the gap is `ref_is_resolvable()` deliberately suppressing references whose word has no entry, which is correct and which also answers the filing that asked whether the detector covers the 06880–06882 shape (気が利かない, 気が軽い, 気が長い — it sees them and excludes them). Inside that suppressed set: **59 target-less references naming 59 distinct words with no entry, exactly one of which is in `candidate_words.json`** (一昨日, メンテナンス, 内閣改造, ぞっとする, 保冷, くるまる…). Against the 2026-08-11 candidates run's measurement that common-vocabulary lenses now run at **72–100% duplicate rates**, this vein's duplicate rate is **~2% by construction**, and every word in it is already cross-referenced from a live entry, so creating it closes an internal gap. **The dictionary's own dangling references are its highest-yield candidate source and nothing was reading them** (Tooling 108).

- **[H] Quality-metrics 35th refresh** (runs 481–496). Three findings. **(1) The frontier-versus-growth rule the last two refreshes built is falsified — by the frontier.** The gap closed to **90%** (+40 entries, +36 frontier IDs) after 42% and 76%, with supply flat and the frontier doubling, the opposite of the 34th refresh's "the frontier's absolute rate is the stable quantity." The formulation fitting all three windows is that **both terms count how often their mode came up** — five polish runs and two new-entries runs this window — so the ratio reads out the selector's rotation and the mode-mix variance swamps it at 16 runs per window. Either normalise per run of the relevant mode or retire the ratio. **(2) The four-refresh run of record queue floors reverses**, 9,832 → 10,089 → 10,279 live, by exactly the mechanism Tooling 94 identified and which is still unshipped: CI queues every changed entry, so a productive window *raises* the queue. It should stop being quoted as a headline until `reviewed_at >= modified` ships. **(3) A series changed definition**: `candidates` 980 → 170 is the 2026-08-11 purge of ~970 corpus-harvested rows, not attrition — the count is now the *usable* count, which is the quality-weighted signal that had been requested, but pre- and post-purge values are not comparable, and the selector's `candidates_low` threshold of 150 was chosen against the old definition. Precision: `tags` **79.1%**, off-vocabulary migration **87.3%** (still the project's highest-precision signal), in-list **64.0%** — **§14's family × band thesis holding for a second consecutive window** — sole-`general` **4.3%** in its sixth wholesale-rejection window, `furigana` **0.7%**, its worst window and still contaminated by the 85% pre-fix screening cache.

**Also filed. Tooling**: **106** (a `links` dimension for `review_accuracy.py` — fourth request, second measurement at **0 of 13 flags on a link-only run concerning a link**, now with a stated rule to check: *same-lexeme*, which separates the 朝日新聞 family from the 猫に小判 family), **107** (check for existing `reviews/accuracy/{id}.json` before paying for a pass — the 08347–08850 run regenerated reviews that had sat unadjudicated since 2026-06-22; same predicate as Tooling 94, and the reason §A's "400–600 entries per run" is only honest on pre-covered ranges at the measured 1.5–2.6 entries/min), and an update to **100** (the candidate queue is clean at 0 of 170 braced rows, so the ask is prevention, not cleanup). **Entry follow-ups**: 28358 〜ぶり (the manner/style sense belongs in the existing time-interval entry — second instance, after 03515 日光, of a candidate that is real, is not a duplicate entry, and cannot be created because `check_duplicate.py` correctly reports the headword as taken); 06886's derived-form spot-check, deferred to the frontier pass over the same creation cohort; **00823/01525 若い re-filed a second time**, recorded rather than merged silently because polish runs can only ever *file* consolidations, never perform them, and four are now open on that page.

**Routed to the curator**: the era-name ruling (`time-period` + `proper-noun` rather than `event-name`, both already in `VALID_SEMANTIC`; needs one line in the find-candidates skill, which a wiki session cannot edit, and the next candidates run will meet it again at ~80% proper-noun survival), and the priority-5 selector question above.

**Process note**: sixteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first whose dominant outcome was *the finding already exists*. Two of the three largest items this window were open queue entries with better notes than the observations re-reporting them, and the third was a decision of record being re-proposed. The rule that follows is narrow and cheap: **before filing, search `backlog-queue.json` for the defect, not just the wiki prose** — the queue is the deduplicated index and the prose pages are the discussion. The cost of not doing it is visible now: a 35-entry regex has consumed four polish runs' attention and four harvests' filing, which is more than the fix.

## [2026-08-13] maintenance | Two priorities filed by measuring the sighting: 23 homophone links out of 273,656, and no tag band at all

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 26 unharvested observations from the 2026-08-12 wiki/polish/accuracy-review runs and the 2026-08-13 new-entries/polish runs). No entries changed, so §4 self-verification does not apply and `make build` is skipped. OpenRouter untouched, $0 spend.

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/determined-rubin-fk3moo`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest, [F] two dictionary-wide measurements, [C] deepened three topic pages, [E] corrected a dangling forward reference, [A] backlog-queue sync. **[H] skipped** — only 4 metrics lines since the 35th refresh, against the ≥10 threshold.

- **[F] P55 — the wrong-target link class, measured for the first time.** Tooling 78 has sat on two anecdotes since 2026-08-06; a 2026-08-13 new-entries run supplied a third (04231 振り返る linking 顧みる at `13656_kaerimiru`, which is 省みる) and asked for a sweep. Running the comparison over all **273,656 inline links**: 1,491 rows where the base form matches neither the target's headword nor its reading, of which **97% are legitimate conventions** — affix headwords (`〜的`), slash headwords (`速い／早い`), する-verb bases against noun headwords, orthographic variants. The residue that is a real defect is **23 instances across ~22 entries**: 終身→就寝, 用地→幼稚, 詩集→刺繍, 詩的→指摘, 詐称→査証, 天賦→添付, 進水→心酔, 専任→仙人, 書架→初夏, 五時→誤字, 深く→不覚, 科す→貸す, plus single-kanji cases. This is the *tail* of the class the 2026-07-31 systemic-fix batch repaired 87 of, which is why it is small — and the useful engineering result is the filter cascade, since a detector shipped without all four filters buries its own signal at 3% precision. A further 48-instance residue (頃 at 〜ころ, 本/下 at 元) is homograph spelling, not a broken link, and must be filtered out rather than worked.

- **[F] The off-vocabulary tag band does not exist — and the lane clearing it is the fastest on this wiki.** A 2026-08-12 accuracy-review measured 46% density in 08851–09350, matched it to 46% in 08066–08565, and inferred one contiguous batch-creation era worth scoping as a single systemic-fix. Measured across all 62 blocks: affected IDs span **00333–27818**, **24 of 62 blocks are completely clean**, and only five exceed 10% (8000–8499 at 40.2%, 10000–10499 at 29.1%, 20000–20499 at 25.5%, 22000–22499 at 17.2%, 16500–16999 at 12.1%), holding 54% between them. The two 46% blocks are adjacent to one hot spot, which is why they looked like a beginning. Underneath the refutation, the better news: the queue is **1,134 entries / 1,329 instances / 431 names, down from 2,530 / 3,208 / 687 seven days earlier — 55% cleared in a week**. `TAG_MIGRATION`'s coverage fell 11.3% → 9.2% while that happened, which is the expected direction (the sweep clears mapped names first) and closes any fifth proposal to extend the map with a number that worsens as the lane succeeds. The proposed drop rule for `positive`/`negative`/`neutral` is overtaken: all three now measure **zero** dictionary-wide.

- **[F] A second taxonomy gap, and a second curator ruling.** `VALID_SEMANTIC` has no tag for sound or perception — its only entry in that region is `music` — while `perception` (12), `sound` (9) and `sensation` (7) are in use off-list and 音/物音/騒音/足音 sit at sole-`general`, where the P13 detector re-surfaces them on every sweep and **no sweep can ever fix them**. That is the `place`/`location` trap (47 instances) at one-sixth the size, and it makes the standing taxonomy request concrete: two names added to a Python set close ~75 off-list instances and stop the cycling. Escalated in plain language.

- **[C/E] Instrument Defects case 9 — the stale API that is a stopwatch in the caller.** Three Routine runs have reported the GitHub MCP `get_check_runs` endpoint serving stale `in_progress` results, one of them across a recorded 33 minutes. The 2026-08-13 new-entries run traced the mechanism: §7's between-poll wait is a **backgrounded** `sleep 30`, which returns immediately, so an unawaited loop spends its ~16-poll budget in under a minute and reads "still running" as "stale" — and that run confirmed the API answered its own failure correctly. The caller-side explanation is demonstrated rather than inferred and covers all three reports, so the API-staleness diagnosis is demoted to unproven and the loop is escalated for a one-sentence prompt fix (Tooling 109). Fitting for this page: an instrument defect in the Routine's own wrap-up, indistinguishable from a corpus defect from inside the loop.

- **[C] Quality Metrics §18 — two headline metrics suspended.** Granting both of the 35th refresh's own `[pattern]` asks ahead of the next refresh: the **review-queue length** cannot be read in either direction (CI queues every changed entry, so a productive window raises it; 35% of the queue is already-reviewed padding) until Tooling 94 ships — which is itself now blocked behind Tooling 110, since `reviewed_at` is not stamped at write time. The **frontier-versus-growth ratio** is retired in its current form: three refreshes read 76%, 42% and 90% out of it and attributed each move to one side, but both terms count how often their mode came up, so it measures the selector's rotation. The generalisation worth keeping: **a metric whose numerator and denominator are produced by different scheduled modes measures the schedule.**

**Also filed. Tooling**: **109** (escalated, above), **110** (`reviewed_at` stamped at pass start rather than write time — blocks items 94 and 107, both of which consume it), **111** (the reviewer's `tags` dimension should report only tags absent from the supplied vocabulary: its not-in-list flags run 87–99% applied, its in-list substitutions ~5% and are rejected by standing policy), **112** (`check_duplicate.py` blind to three duplicate shapes — kanji/kana variants of one word reported as "homophones", kana-headword entries queried by their kanji spelling reported as absent, and a new *sense* of an existing word arriving as a new headword; four of twenty-four candidates in one batch). Update to **item 20** with its first throughput figure: 39 priority lines read to find 4 workable entries, 31 skipped in one unbroken run. **Cleanup**: P55, P56 (idiom↔body-part cross-references, both directions bare on 6 of 6 frontier entries, scope unmeasured, detector-friendly shape). **Entry follow-ups**: 08989 車席 (probably fabricated; curator), 08985 日日 (wrong orthography for ひにち), 17576 摺り寄る (rare spelling as headword), 06899 顔を出す (top-level gloss covers one of two senses — a convention ruling before an edit), 00743 音. **Queue**: `inline-link-homophone-target` and `crossref-idiom-bodypart` added; `unknown-semantic-tags` re-measured to 1,134.

**Process note**: seventeenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where the measurement *raised* an item's value rather than shrinking it — P55 exists as an actionable 23-instance batch only because the filter cascade separated it from 1,468 false positives. The previous harvest's rule (search the queue before filing) held: `inline-link-block-06800-07100` was re-discovered a fifth time, from inside the block this time, and needed no new item.

## [2026-08-14] maintenance | The report was not blind, the retirement was wrong, and the place tag already existed

**Session type**: Routine v2, `wiki` mode (selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; 34 unharvested observations from the two 2026-08-13 accuracy-review/polish runs, the 2026-08-13 new-entries run, and the 2026-08-14 accuracy-review and polish runs). No entries changed, so §4 self-verification does not apply and `make build` is skipped. OpenRouter untouched, $0 spend.

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/determined-rubin-fpn4jx`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest, [F] four dictionary-wide measurements, [H] thirty-sixth metrics refresh, [C] deepened Instrument Defects with case 10, [A] backlog-queue sync (three new items, one re-measured).

- **[F] P57 — the asymmetry report is not blind; it is unsorted.** Two polish runs, a day apart, each found six-for-six basic/core nouns with an empty `cross_references` array whose obvious neighbours were already named in their own notes, and the second added a hypothesis: the asymmetry report must be under-reporting because it only looks at entries that have at least one reference. Reading `find_asymmetric_references()` refutes that — it iterates *sources* and reports every A→B with no B→A, so a bare target is exactly what it does report. Measuring its output supplies the item the hypothesis was reaching for: of **21,444** references carrying a `target_id`, 6,242 pairs are symmetric and **8,633 are one-way**, and **2,183 of the one-way pairs (1,550 distinct entries: 19 basic, 202 core, 1,329 general) point at an entry with no references of either kind**. That sub-class needs no judgment — another entry has already decided the relationship is worth recording and the target has no reference list to weigh it against — while the remaining 6,450 are editorial choices that must not be swept. Most-pointed-at bare entries: 01433 正月 (10 inbound), 01464 注意, 02504 秘密, 04117 温泉 (8 each). The tool ask is correspondingly small: a `--bare-targets` filter and grouping by target (Tooling 113), so 正月 appears once rather than ten times among 8,633 lines.

- **[F] The place/location taxonomy gap closes with a tag that already exists.** Yesterday's harvest escalated to the curator that `VALID_SEMANTIC` has no place tag and no sound tag, leaving ~75 off-list instances with nowhere to go. The 2026-08-13 accuracy-review answered the first half from inside the corpus — the street and district words belong with the 54 entries that already use `geography` — and this harvest sized it: **`geography` is on 617 entries**, and the off-list place family is **~40 instances** (`place` 23, `location` 13, `places` 2, `location-area` 1, `regional` 1), all of which map to it, with `transportation` for road infrastructure. That is a `TAG_MIGRATION` row, not a curator ruling. **The sound/perception half stands** (`perception` 11, `sound` 9, `sensation` 7, `taste` 2 — 29 instances, 音/物音/騒音/足音 still stuck at sole-`general`), and 04095 足跡 added a third gap: nothing in-list covers a physical trace or mark. The escalation is now smaller and sharper than when it was raised. Off-vocabulary queue re-measured at **998 entries / 1,161 instances / 394 names**, from 1,134 / 1,329 / 431 eight days ago.

- **[F] Two register measurements, one item and one non-item.** The 2026-08-14 polish run reported register tags drifting toward "marked" on ordinary vocabulary (03552 徹夜 tagged `slang` because the *notes* mention オール; 04095 足跡 tagged `formal` + `literary`, of footprints in snow) and the 2026-08-13 run proposed sweeping `style: literary` co-occurring with `formality: informal`. The contradiction sweep is **3 entries** corpus-wide (02792 けち, 06000 郷愁, 07400 巡り合わせ) — not an item. The markedness reading is: **231 basic/core-tier nouns carry a marked register** (173 `formal`, 26 `literary`, 19 literary+formal, 8 `slang`, 5 `archaic`), which is a review queue worth working precisely because the tier restriction makes each call decidable in a glance — the same predicate over the whole dictionary returns 5,079 and was retired as a detector on 2026-08-10 for that reason.

- **[F/C] Instrument Defects case 10 — a retirement that does not reproduce.** Checking the `literary` premise against its own history turned up something worse than the premise: the 2026-08-12 retirement of the `style: ["literary"]` template hypothesis states "measured across 06800–06999: zero entries", and **five entries in that range carry exactly that value, three of them unmodified since before the retirement was written**. The verdict survives re-measurement (443 entries dictionary-wide, a normal style label), but the number does not, and this is the first case on that page of an instrument making the corpus look *cleaner* than it is — the more dangerous direction, because a false positive costs one adjudication while a false retirement closes a question and takes the evidence with it. New rule, the mirror of the one that page adopted two days ago for filings: **a filing that retires an item should quote the command it ran.**

- **[H] Thirty-sixth metrics refresh** (runs 497–509, 866 adjudicated flags). **The off-vocabulary tag family — 97.7% applied [386/9] and the project's highest-precision signal for four windows — produced its first large escalation block**, one aggregated line of 74 flags reading "off-vocab tags with no safe in-list destination". Nothing about the reviewer changed; the sweep reached words whose correct category is not in the vocabulary at all. That makes the family's apply rate a **leading indicator of taxonomy coverage** rather than of reviewer quality, and it predicts its own decline as the mappable names clear. **A counting defect surfaced while reconciling the same event**: the ledger records 75 flag decisions this window while `needs_curator.txt` grew by 6, because 74 of them are one aggregated line — so "escalations flat", five refreshes' phrasing, is right about the file and wrong about the ledger, and the two units must never be compared across a window. `furigana` at 3.9% [2/49] now has a measured cause rather than a shrug: 46 of 48 flags in the 2026-08-14 range predate the `trim_context` fix. §14's family split holds a fourth window (headline `tags` 61.7%, in-list 11.2%, sole-`general` 16.1%, formality/register 6.5%).

**Also filed. Tooling**: **113** (`--bare-targets` for the asymmetry report), **114** (the screening cache stores 1,002 `flagged` of 10,888 screened under a single file-level timestamp, so the requested `--rescreen-before DATE` cannot be implemented until entries are stamped individually — the same defect as item 110 in the other review instrument, and the residue costs ~$0.01/entry in deep review), **115** (`--notes-only` link checks — the notes-bare direction has never been sized), **116** (a rendaku warning in `manage_candidates.py add`: あしてまとい reached a candidate row, and a wrong reading there becomes a permanent wrong `romaji` in an entry ID), **117** (two furigana detectors: ruby spans crossing a word boundary, and single-kanji spans carrying their in-compound reading). Updates to **111** (the reviewer assigns `error` to 5 and `warn` to 40 off-vocabulary flags, for a property decidable by set membership — a run following §A's effort-scaling rule misses 89% of the highest-precision class) and **84** (screening at ~9 entries/min against accuracy review's ~37/min on the same account, at one-eighth the cost — the cheap instrument is latency-bound). **Entry follow-ups**: 01420/02368 幸せ (fifth open consolidation), 03605 武器 (核武器 is not a word; needs a replacement example, not a patch), 03459 図書 (図書カード glossed as "library card"), 02206 草 (entry-level `slang` for one documented sense — a convention ruling), 04095 足跡. **Queue**: `crossref-bare-target`, `tag-baseball-leisure`, `tag-register-marked-basic-core` added; `unknown-semantic-tags` re-measured to 998.

**Re-discoveries needing no new item**: `inline-link-block-06800-07100` for the sixth, seventh and eighth time (06910–06913, 06915–06918, 06919–06925, all zero-link); `inline-link-split-compound` (03795 我々 linking 私 + たち with 28351_watashitachi live); `inline-link-stale-noentry` and `stale-calendar-month-links` three more times — that last one now with the strongest scheduling argument it has had, a fourth consecutive band (03167–03756, 181 pairs) where the external self-check raised zero objections to a context-checked link substitution. It is already the lowest-numbered open, batch-ready item in the queue, so the next `systemic-fix` run will take it without anything being changed.

**Process note**: eighteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which the measurement caught *this wiki* rather than a script or a corpus. Three of the four measurements this session changed what got filed — the asymmetry report was accused of a blindness it does not have, the place-tag escalation needed a lookup rather than a ruling, and a sweep proposed against 443 entries turned out to be three. The retirement error is the one to carry forward: the discipline this page has been building (measure before filing) is only half of it, because a measurement that is never re-run becomes a claim, and claims written into a wiki are what the next session reasons from.

## [2026-08-15] maintenance | Sixty-six days of frontier data replace a two-day delta; two mechanical priorities; two detectors retired

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 19 unharvested observations, 35 open backlog items, candidates 157, frontier 06936).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/determined-rubin-5xx9z5`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 19 observations, [F] seven dictionary-wide measurements, [C] deepened Inline Link Integrity with the 66-day rate series, [D] two new cleanup priorities, [A] backlog-queue sync (four new items, two re-measured, one retired).

- **[C/F] The zero-link band is the cursor, and the cursor is losing — measured over 66 days rather than two.** Two polish runs filed 06926–06929 and 06930–06935 as a creation batch that skipped inline linking, "worth a targeted sweep" — the eighth and ninth filing of that non-item. The frontier sat at **06936** when they filed: they were inside its own bucket describing the cliff edge (06900–06999 is 59% zero-link; 07000–07499 is 98%; everything from 10000 up is 100.0%). What made this pair worth more than a ninth correction is that the page refuting them was itself resting on thin evidence — a **two-day** delta (23,294 → 23,404 while the frontier moved 122 IDs), which cannot distinguish a trend from a busy Tuesday. `pipeline/metrics-history.jsonl` has been recording `comprehensive_next` and `entries_total` on every run since 2026-06-10, so the argument now rests on **516 runs over 66 days**: the frontier advances **14.3 IDs/day** against **24.1 entries/day** of growth, net **+9.8/day**, and **the gap has not closed in any 7-, 14-, 30- or 66-day window** (7d +6.7, 14d +11.0, 30d +11.4). The link metric agrees independently: zero-link **23,444** against **23,508 entries ahead of the frontier** — the two populations are the same set to within 64 entries. The practical correction is that the standing "roughly two years to reach ID 30,000" estimate treats a moving target as fixed; at measured rates **the frontier does not converge on the end of the dictionary at all**. Escalated to the curator as a strategy call (not a bug): either linking is a feature of the polished prefix by decision, or one of Tooling 49 (the link suggester, which attacks the ~90% of a linking slot that is lookup rather than judgment), linking at creation time, or slower growth has to change the 3:5 ratio.

- **[D] P59 — 32 entries print a potential form that is not a Japanese word.** A new-entries run hit `愛できる`-shaped output while creating 30647 処する, fixed that one by hand, and filed the class with a detection rule. The rule holds and the scope is exact: of **4,592** `suru`-type conjugation tables, **33** have a single-character stem and **32** carry the impossible 〜できる potential (愛する, 発する, 接する, 察する, 面する …), where the class takes 〜せる. Provably safe to rewrite, because 〜できる is not a possible form of it — but **`add_conjugations.py` must be fixed first** (Tooling 122), or the next `--force` run reintroduces all 32. That is the standing P16 lesson: a data cleanup whose generator is unfixed is a scheduled regression.

- **[D] P60 — 275 katakana strings are wearing furigana, and the site renders it.** A systemic-fix run found `{ラベル|らべる}` in 03995 宛名, noted correctly that every furigana instrument asks "does this kanji have a reading?" and none asks "does this reading belong here?", and left the scope unmeasured. Measured: **275 instances / 229 entries**, spanning 00138–30640 rather than one batch (notes 135, examples 129, explanations 11). It is not cosmetic — `docs/entries/00000/00138_kaisetsu.html` emits `<ruby>ニュース<rt>にゅーす</rt></ruby>`, so the live site prints information-free ruby that reads as a mistake to the learner ruby exists for. **271 of the 275 readings are the exact kana transliteration of the base** and 3 more repeat the katakana unchanged, so 274 are lossless to strip. **The 275th is the find**: 23394 二枚貝 carries `{カキ|がき}` — oyster read がき — a genuine reading error that no furigana instrument could ever have caught, because they all key on kanji.

- **[F] Both formality detectors retired, and they were one sighting filed from two ends.** The polish run proposed flagging `formality: formal` where the entry's own REGISTER line says "neutral"; the systemic-fix run proposed flagging it where the notes attribute formality to a neighbouring word. The first returns **8** entries across all 5,067 formal-tagged ones, and **six of the eight are correct as tagged** — they read "Neutral **to** formal", or describe a *connotation* as neutral. The second returns **419**, dominated by the correct case (00122 寺院 "more formal than お寺", 00147 価格, 00252 苦情, 00264 給与 — entries where the formal word *is* the headword), and its three named witnesses (03922 削る, 04077 火傷, 04222 取り除く) **already read `neutral`**, having been fixed by the run that filed them. Separating "this word is formal" from "that other word is the formal one" is the whole semantic task. Per case 10's rule, both commands are quoted in the backlog.

- **[F] Two filings corrected by counting.** (a) "POS-term entries drift to `education` — a small closed set worth a one-shot sweep" is a **five-way convention gap**: 26 grammar-terminology headwords carry `grammatical` (8), `language` (8), `education` (6), `general` (4), `communication` (3). The filing run's own evidence refutes its framing — it fixed 名詞 and 音読み in the same pass **to different tags**, and 訓読み/音読み and 他動詞/自動詞 split the same way. There is no convention to sweep toward; escalated for a ruling, after which the sweep is 26 entries. (b) "11 `validate_tags.py` errors: five 〜ずる verbs plus a proper-noun entry" is 5 + **3** proper nouns (08853, 08907, 08908) + the 3 irregular verbs ある/来る/する, where `verb_class` holds the precise value and the POS tag the coarse one — arguably the validator's defect, not the entries'.

- **[F] One "dedicated backlog item" request answered by an item open for two weeks.** An accuracy-review run asked for a systemic-fix item listing every sole-`general` entry; `tag-sole-general` has been open and `batch_ready` since 2026-08-01. Re-measured at **3,642** (3,151 general, 442 core, 49 basic), down from 3,681 — hand-clearing runs at ~40/day against a class of 3,600.

**Also filed. Tooling**: **118** — `review_accuracy.py --dimensions tags` fails in both directions over the same 411 entries: 80 tag flags, every one sampled a confabulation (the model invents a finer-grained tag the entry lacks, then "suggests" the tag already present), while **0 of the 22 entries genuinely carrying off-vocabulary tags were flagged**. Precision ≈ 0, recall = 0, on the one class a five-line set-difference against `VALID_SEMANTIC` finds free; fix is two sentences of prompt, and until then the dimension should not be run. **119** (screening truncates the reading inside its own report — rendering `{犯罪|はんざい}` as "only はん provided" — then flags the truncation it invented; 43/43 false positives, and a clerical failure rather than a Japanese one, so cheaper to fix than any family in the calibration report). **120** (a `conjugation.type`-vs-POS check: three lines, two hits, one of them **20837 さする**, a godan verb carrying a サ変 table end to end). **121** (katakana-base furigana detector, feeding P60). **122** (the `add_conjugations.py` generator fix, blocking P59). Updates to **84**: measured throughput is ~4.5 entries/min for accuracy review and **~5× slower** for screening, and **running the two concurrently collapsed screening to near-zero** — so ranges should be sized to 250–300 entries, not routine2's 400–600. Plus a prompt-wording recommendation ("casual" is not a `formality` enum value; the schema wants `informal`) and a note that `review_accuracy.py` writes an empty `description` — read `concern`. **Entry follow-ups**: 20837 さする, 23394 二枚貝, 10166 寿司屋, 10951 ショート, 04946/04951, and the January-2026 core-noun note-shape batch (uniform score 75, restructuring lifts to 94–97). **Queue**: `conjugation-suru-single-kanji-potential`, `furigana-katakana-base`, `validate-tags-standing-errors`, `tag-grammar-terminology-convention` added; `tag-sole-general` re-measured; `tag-formality-contradicts-register-note` retired.

**[H] Metrics trend not run**: 516 lines against `quality-metrics.md`'s last refresh at 509 — 7 new, below the ≥10 threshold. Due next wiki run.

**Process note**: nineteenth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first where the discipline was turned on the *strongest* claim on a page rather than the weakest. The zero-link argument was already correct and already agreed with; re-measuring it anyway upgraded a two-day anecdote into a 66-day series and changed the conclusion in a way that matters — from "the frontier will take about two years" to "at current rates the frontier does not finish", which is a different question for the curator. That is the natural extension of case 10: a measurement that is never re-run becomes a claim, and the claims most likely to go unchecked are the ones everybody already believes. A near-miss the same session argues the point from the other side — a katakana measurement returned **0 instances**, apparently retiring the item, and the zero was an artifact of an earlier `cd` leaving the shell outside the repository root. Re-run from the correct directory it returned 275. Absolute paths in measurement commands, and treat a clean zero as a claim needing its own check.

## [2026-08-15] maintenance | The self-check reverses from insurance to the best instrument on the page; a recurring non-item finally explains itself

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 8 unharvested observations, 36 open backlog items, candidates 160, frontier 06947).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/determined-rubin-vsi5y6`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 12 observations, [F] five dictionary-wide measurements, [H] metrics refresh 37 (runs 510–521), [D] one new cleanup priority, [A] backlog-queue sync (one item added, one re-measured), [E] three curator escalations.

- **[H] The §4 self-check is the highest-precision instrument on the page — 75.7% applied [28/9] — one window after being proposed for trimming.** Refresh 36 closed by noting three consecutive clean self-checks and suggesting that "the birth-time check is now functioning as insurance rather than detection… if it holds for another five runs the budget question is worth asking explicitly." It did not hold for one. The reversal is a population effect, not an instrument effect, and it is §14's mixture lesson in a new place: sorting the 33 self-check ledger lines by the run that produced them splits them cleanly — **`new-entries` self-checks ran 1/0** (insurance, exactly as read) while **`polish` and `systemic-fix` self-checks ran 27/9**. The §4 rule is "send exactly the entries this run changed", so when the run is a *polishing* run those entries are old and often never reviewed, and the check is a first review rather than a birth check. **This inverts refresh 36's budget argument**: the self-check already buys never-reviewed-entry coverage, at ~4.5× the accuracy sweep's rate on the same dimensions, by inheriting the polish lane's targeting for free. What it caught is not marginal either — an entire 泊まる ("stay the night") sense with five example sentences was living inside **basic-tier** 00555 止まる ("stop"), moved out to 10445 by the run whose self-check found it.
