- **[H] The same tag family is rejected wholesale by one source and applied eight times by the other, and both are right.** "The sole tag `general` is too broad" was bulk-rejected by the accuracy sweep (one aggregated line of **74**) and applied by the self-check on 04267 eyebrow, 04369 petal, 04379 ginkgo, 04407 bamboo grass, 04419 dementia, 04991, 04994, 04999. No inconsistency: the sweep meets the flag as a bulk proposal against a 3,641-entry population with no destination attached; the self-check meets it with the entry open and the right in-list tag obvious from the notes. **The family's applicability is a property of the adjudicator's context, not of the flag** — the strongest argument yet that `sole-general` belongs to the polish lane as a side task and should never be run as a sweep. Headline `tags` at **16.9%** [39/192] is a mixture weight for the fifth consecutive window, and this time the noise is fully enumerated: four named families cover 181 of 192 rejects, and the remainder runs **78.0%**. `furigana` posts its first exactly-zero window [0/48], now with a named clerical cause (43 are Tooling 119's self-inflicted truncation) rather than "contaminated, direction unknown".

- **[F] The zero-link "creation cohort" is the polish frontier seen through the creation-date axis — and that finally explains eight rediscoveries.** A polish run filed the January-18/19 batch as systematically unlinked, concluding that "inline linking appears to have been added to the entry-creation flow after that batch". The cliff is real and sharp — 2026-01-17: 1.0% zero-link, 2026-01-18: 35.1%, 2026-01-19: **97.5%**, everything after: ~100% — and it is not a process change. Scanned by ID band, the same cliff falls between **06000–06999 (7.5%)** and **07000–07999 (98.9%)**, i.e. at `next: 06947`. Entries were created in ID order, so creation date proxies entry ID, and the frontier is a step function in ID: **any** field correlated with ID returns a perfect-looking cluster. (The hypothesis is separately refuted by policy — CLAUDE.md has always forbidden linking at creation time, so it cannot have been removed from the flow.) The value here is the mechanism: the recurrence has been read as sessions failing to consult a standing note, but a session that sees five unlinked entries and checks one shared field gets a convincing cluster from any axis it picks. Filed as **Tooling 124** — print the frontier where sessions already look ("06975 is above the comprehensive frontier; zero links is expected here") rather than in a document they must recall. General form of the Tooling 118 lesson: when a question has a cheap deterministic answer, put the answer in the tool.

- **[D] P62 — 2,496 entries lose 10 note-quality points to a bullet character.** `vocabulary-notes` mandates `- `; `score_note_quality.py` credits only `- `; 2,496 entries / 18,089 lines use `・` instead. The number that makes it an item rather than a curiosity: **130 of the top 500** entries on `polishing/priority/notes.txt` (26%) carry the shape, so a quarter of the worst-scoring queue is mis-ranked for a typographic defect. Batch-safe as `^・` → `- ` on the notes field only (a line-initial `・` cannot be the katakana middle dot), and unlike P59/P60 there is no generator to fix first. Added to the queue as `notes-nakaguro-bullets`; `make priorities` must be re-run after the sweep.

- **[F] The `verify_furigana.py` filing was corrected, and the corrected defect is worse than the one filed.** The claim was "it does not check the `headword` field". It does — proved on 01310 召し上がる, where it reports `Missing furigana for: 召, 上`. What it does is **exit 0 with a clean-looking summary when its argument matches nothing**: `verify_furigana.py 06940` prints one warning, then "Entries checked: 0 / with issues: 0 / OK: 0". The trap is that **the failing form is the documented one** — CLAUDE.md's command table says `verify_furigana.py <id>`, which reads as the five-digit number, not `NNNNN_romaji`. Filed as **Tooling 123** (two-line fix: exit non-zero on zero entries checked; resolve a bare ID by glob) and escalated. The `validate.py` half of the filing stands and explains P52: schema validation does not gate on furigana, so 258 bare-kanji headwords are reported by the global scan and gated by nothing.

- **[F] Three claims shrank under measurement, one grew.** P50 re-measured at **54** (from 55) — not converging, but its shape is new: six *contiguous* ID runs (03949–03969 alone is 21 consecutive entries) plus seven singletons, so it should be worked run-by-run. The `word_id_lookup` slash-variant gap is **3 headwords of 28,546 keys**, not "a whole class" — real, three-line fix, de-scoped, and the third headword-index hypothesis in three weeks to shrink this way. Note-header kanji without furigana: **8 surviving entries**, all already reported by `find_missing_furigana.py`, so an undrained queue rather than a detector gap. The one that grew is Tooling 84 below.

**Also filed. Tooling**: **84 update** — two runs a day apart measured `review_accuracy.py` throughput and disagreed **14×** (~4.5 vs ~62 entries/min). Reconciled: the slow figure was measured while screening ran concurrently, and the same window separately filed the finding that concurrency collapses both passes. So **4.5/min is a contention artifact**, routine2 §A's 400–600 range target is correct after all, and the recommendation it displaced ("size to 250–300") was drawn from the wrong number. Uncontended: accuracy ~62/min, screening ~8.5/min — it is *screening* that needs the smaller range, which is the observation's own proposal that the selector hand out two ranges rather than one. **111/118 update**: 74 of one run's 108 tag flags are a single family, "`general` is too broad", raised at `error` severity against a backlog item the project's own tooling grades `info`; with the 8 formality flags contradicted by the entries' own REGISTER notes, four sentences of prompt would remove **82 of 108** without losing an applied correction. **Recurrences**: the empty `description` field and "casual"-as-a-`formality`-value were both filed by the midday harvest and re-observed within nine hours. **Entry follow-ups**: the ていうか/というか/つーか cluster (06920, 16105) for stacked contractions, and 06946's deliberate `noentry` on 三月末. **Escalated to the curator**: the missing `formality` row in `prompts/newentries.md`, the `verify_furigana.py` silent no-op, and a note-header convention question for the vocabulary-notes skill.

**Process note**: twentieth consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which the *reversal of a previous refresh's own recommendation* was the headline. Both of this session's largest findings have the same shape — a series that looks like one thing is two populations averaged together (self-check by triggering mode; `tags` by family), and a cluster that looks like a cause is an artifact of the axis it was viewed on (creation date proxying entry ID). The practical rule that falls out, and the one worth carrying forward: **before filing a cluster, check whether the grouping field correlates with entry ID** — the frontier makes every ID-correlated field produce a convincing false cohort. The corresponding rule for this page is that a metric proposed for retirement should be split before it is cut; refresh 36 came within one window of recommending the removal of the project's highest-yield review instrument.


## [2026-08-16] maintenance | Two filings propose rewriting the dictionary to satisfy a broken checker; a hot spot moves 3,000 IDs

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 30 unharvested observations, 37 open backlog items, candidates 164, frontier 06967).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/determined-rubin-svl079`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 30 observations, [F] eight dictionary-wide measurements, [D] two new cleanup priorities, [A] backlog-queue sync (two items added, two re-measured), [E] two curator escalations.

- **[F/E] A checker that is 55% wrong asked the dictionary to change instead, and it nearly got its way.** A polish run reported that `check_consistency.py --issue no-collocations` false-positives on entries documenting collocations under a different heading, and proposed *"standardizing the heading to `COMMON COLLOCATIONS:` across the dictionary… worth a mechanical sweep since the transformation is heading-only."* Both halves measured. The false-positive rate is real and **larger than reported — 3,717 of 6,759 flags (55.0%)**, so the majority of the check's output is wrong on content. But the proposed sweep would have destroyed real structure: the two dominant families are `COMMON PATTERNS:` (2,140) and `COMMON EXPRESSIONS:` (1,320), which are grammatical frames and set phrases — not collocations — and `PARTICLE PATTERNS:` (196) and `SIMILAR`/`RELATED EXPRESSIONS:` (788) less so still. Renaming 3,460 entries' sections into a category they do not belong to, to make a checker stop complaining, inverts the dependency. Filed as **Tooling 125** (one line in the checker) plus **P63** for the genuinely mechanical residue — the near-synonyms of headings the dictionary already standardises on, **730 entries**: `COLLOCATIONS:` 330, `PATTERN:` 154, `PATTERNS:` 115, `USAGE PATTERNS:` 92, `KEY PATTERNS:` 39. The sequencing is explicit in the item, because it is the whole lesson: **fix the checker first, then rename the 730 — never the other way round.**

- **[F] The off-vocabulary tag hot spot is 3,000 IDs from where its filing run put it.** An accuracy-review run over 11501–12000 reported that "the 11750–11950 band is where the dictionary's off-vocabulary semantic tags cluster… almost all of them sit above ID 11750. The entry-creation cohort in that band evidently invented tag names freely." Measured against `VALID_SEMANTIC` across all 30,484 entries: **934 entries** carry at least one off-list tag, the largest concentration is **08000–08999 with 201 — 22% of the whole population in one band** — and **348 of the 934 sit below ID 11000**. The 11000-band is not in the top six. This is the third ID-correlated false cohort in three weeks and it has exactly the shape the 2026-08-15 harvest named: a run generalises a *band* from the *sample it happens to be holding*. The correction is operational rather than cosmetic, because the run's recommendation was right and only its target was wrong — a `systemic-fix` pass on `check_tag_drift.py --check unknown-semantic` is the fastest route through this population, and it should start at **08000**, which is 201 entries of the highest-precision flag family the project has.

- **[D/F] A defect class no furigana instrument can see, sized and given a working detector.** 04651 {関節痛|かんせつつう} carries `{痛|いたみ}` where the correct form is `{痛|いた}み`; the filing run asked whether `check_furigana_format.py` sees the shape. **It does not** — 765 findings, 04651 not among them. The structural reason is the mirror of P60's: every furigana check asks *"does this kanji have a reading?"* and here it does, the reading having simply absorbed the okurigana that belongs outside the braces. A rule that works: for a single-kanji base K with reading R, flag R when a proper prefix of R is a common reading of K elsewhere (R ≤3 occurrences, prefix ≥10). **Measured: 90 distinct pairs / 123 instances**, with an unusually strong head — `{切|きり}`×1 against `{切|き}`×3,631, `{入|いれ}`×1 against `{入|い}`×2,785, `{痛|いたみ}`×1 against `{痛|いた}`×810. Filed as **P64** and **Tooling 130**, explicitly *not* mechanical: the same output contains `{止|とど}`, which is a genuine reading (とどまる).

- **[F] Three premises did not survive measurement, and one of the three is this session's most useful negative.** (a) *"Several older entries open their notes with a bare section header and never state what the word means in a sentence"* — 7,810 entries (**26% of the dictionary**) open with a bare header, `USAGE:` alone accounting for 4,560; but **all 7,810 have a filled `definitions` field**, which is where meanings live, so the defect half of the report is empty and what remains is a style question at a quarter of the corpus. Escalated as a convention ruling rather than filed as a detector. (b) The katakana-ruby class re-reported as *"50 entry files"* is **271 instances / 225 files** — the 50 came from a shell character-range regex, and the correct figure matches P60's standing 275/229. That is the *second consecutive harvest* in which a katakana measurement was wrong because of how it was run rather than what it found. (c) The transitivity `PAIR:`-paragraph template said to be shared by *"all five verb entries polished from the priority lane this run (00563, 00605, 00735, 00841, 00869, 00895)"* — six IDs behind the word "five" — is carried by **24 entries dictionary-wide**, and **none of the six is among them**.

- **[F/E] The most-reported operational defect in the project turns out to be two defects.** Tooling 109 attributes every "`get_check_runs` is stale" report to the Routine's own poll loop: it waits with a *backgrounded* `sleep 30` that returns instantly, so the ~16-poll budget burns in under a minute and forty seconds of wall clock is misread as a stuck API. Two more runs reported the symptom on 2026-08-16, and **one of them refutes that explanation for itself**: PR #3216 read `in_progress` **across 14 polls over more than 40 minutes**, and its `completed_at` when it finally resolved was 12:37:29 — about **70 seconds after `started_at`**. Forty minutes is not a sub-minute budget burn, and a completion timestamp that far in the past is a genuinely stale cached response. So 109's fix is necessary and not sufficient, five runs have now hit this, and its cost is precisely the stranded-PR class §0a exists to clean up after. Both filing runs independently proposed the same cheap remedy — cross-check with `get_check_run` (which needs only the id the stale call itself returns) before concluding "pending". Escalated with the new evidence.

- **[F] Two "new systemic-fix items" were items already open.** The polish run's proposal to extract cross-references from notes prose (witnesses 02009, 02132, 05685) is `crossref-missing-from-notes-prose`, open and `batch_ready`; re-measured at **1,828** entries with an empty `cross_references` array and ≥3 distinct inline-link targets in their notes. All three witnesses had been repaired by the run that filed them, so none appears in the population. The zero-link block at 06947+ was re-filed for the **tenth** time. And `check_semantic_clusters.py`'s pair rule really is single-field — but the blind field is `cross_references`, the opposite of the one the run guessed, and the population is **17 entries** (13 invisible pairs, 4 false `missing_reciprocal`), so it was confirmed and de-scoped in the same breath.

**[H] Metrics trend not run**: 529 lines against `quality-metrics.md`'s last refresh at 521 runs — 8 new, below the ≥10 threshold. Due next wiki run, as it was last run.

**Process note**: twenty-first consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first in which *two independent runs on the same day* proposed changing the dictionary to accommodate a tool that was wrong. The collocation-heading sweep would have merged three real note sections; the tag sweep would have been pointed at a band holding a fraction of its target. Neither run did anything careless — each generalised correctly from what it could see, which was one range of entries and one tool's output. What the two cases share is the shape the previous harvest named for clusters, now visible for instruments: **a tool's output is a sample too**, and its false-positive rate is a property to measure before treating its complaints as a description of the corpus. The rule this page has been building — measure before filing — gains a clause: when a filing proposes changing the corpus to satisfy a checker, measure the checker first.

## [2026-08-21] maintenance | Harvest of 18 observations; Quality Metrics refresh 38; P50 measured growing for the first time

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 22 unharvested observation lines, 39 open backlog items, candidates 163, frontier 06985, cross-model cursor 12713).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/lucid-wozniak-lc1tzo`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 18 observations from five runs, [F] seven dictionary-wide measurements, [H] quality-metrics refresh 38 (runs 522–536), [E] recompute of a stale all-time table, [A] backlog-queue sync (one item added, two sized/re-measured), [E] two curator escalations.

- **[F] P50 is growing, and its members were visited rather than skipped.** Two polish runs (2026-08-17, 2026-08-20) proposed building a zero-link coverage detector — the sixth and seventh re-discovery of a class the wiki settled with an explicit *do not file* ruling, since the unfiltered population is **23,460 of 30,504 entries (77%)** and is the frontier position, not a defect. The actionable residue is P50, and re-measuring it produced the first genuinely new result in the series: **55 → 54 → 57**, i.e. it **grew by three while the frontier advanced 38 IDs**, and all three new members (06958 アジェンダ, 06959 ストレージ, 06960 デバイス) sit inside the band the lane just crossed. Because the comprehensive cursor stands at 06985, the lane has by definition already walked past all 57 — so these are entries it *visited and left unlinked*, and five of them still carry their 2026-01-19 creation-cleanup `modified` date, meaning the visit changed nothing at all. Spot-checks confirm they are ordinarily linkable (03949 空〜's examples hold 予約/空港/迎え/行く; 06011 呼吸器's hold 病気/入院/喫煙/負担). The 2026-08-15 diagnosis — "a lane that skipped a directory's worth of work" — is replaced by **"a lane that visited and silently dropped the linking checklist item."** That changes the remedy from scheduling a backfill to understanding why the item is dropped on whole contiguous blocks.

- **[F/E] Seven re-discoveries of one ruling is a routing failure, not seven insights.** This harvest checked *why* the zero-link class keeps coming back and found the answer immediately: **neither `prompts/comprehensive_polish.md` nor `.claude/skills/inline-word-links/SKILL.md` mentions the ruling, P50, or `inline-link-integrity.md` at all.** The knowledge exists; the sessions that need it never see it. Filed as **Tooling 133** (an "already settled — do not re-file" block in the polishing prompt, same remedy as Tooling 124), with the furigana truncated-reading family and the in-list-narrowness tag family as the obvious next two entries.

- **[H] The de-mixed `tags` number is stable across two windows, and `furigana` records a second exactly-zero window.** Headline accuracy `tags` reads **41.9%** [93/129] against refresh 37's 16.9%, but §14's correction applies for a sixth time: five named noise families account for 106 of the 129 rejects, and the **residual runs 80.2%** [93/23] against last window's 78.0% — two windows, different bands, the same answer within two points, so the de-mixed figure is now *stable* rather than merely reproducible. The same correction rescues refresh 37's self-check headline: raw `self-check` `tags` **31.8%** looks like a collapse from 75.7%, but 33 of its 45 rejects are two aggregated noise lines and **stripped it is 63.6%** — ordinary movement. Meanwhile `furigana` is **0 applied of 92** [**0 of 140 across two windows**], both aggregated families named as defects in the *reporting* rather than the dictionary, and corroborated out-of-band by the 2026-08-16 run's hand-verification of the same family. The recommendation loses its hedge: retire the furigana pass over already-polished ranges.

- **[H] The review queue rose a third time, and the largest source is the project itself.** 10,567 → 10,755. The window wrote **482 entries**, of which **256 (53%) came from two `systemic-fix` runs**, and CI re-queues everything any run touches — so a high-throughput systemic-fix run is structurally the biggest queue-*growth* event the Routine produces. That is not an argument against the mode; it is a reason the queue number cannot be read as a quality signal in any window containing one, on top of the padding Tooling 94 would remove. Standing recommendation restated: keep the queue out of refresh headlines until that predicate ships.

- **[E] A stale table reversed a standing claim.** The page's all-time "Flag precision by source" table had not been recomputed in several refreshes and understated every denominator by 3–4× (self-check 348 against an actual 1,282; accuracy 4,675 against 13,593). Recomputed, it shows the **accuracy sweep is the highest-rate content source all-time — 62.9% apply-among-decided against the self-check's 40.1%** — reversing the ordering the surrounding prose asserted. The old claim was true when written: the sweep's denominator was then dominated by the unknown-semantic escalation backlog, which has since drained.

- **[F] Four premises did not survive measurement.** (a) *"Several movement verbs in this range share 06970's over-broad `business` domain"* — **four entries dictionary-wide** carry `movement` + `business`, and the other three (来店, お見えになる, 出張する) are legitimately business-domain; 06970 is the only member. (b) *"Does `check_stale_noentry.py` weight notes as heavily as examples?"* — it scans `notes`/`usage_notes` at line 135, *before* examples at 139; the three sightings were outside the swept band, and all three had already been fixed by their own filing run. (c) *"Medical compounds harvested from a parent entry's SIMILAR WORDS list"* as a candidate-vetting class — **1 of 163** queued words matches (脂漏 itself), so there is no class to write a rule for. (d) *"CLAUDE.md and comprehensive_polish.md both describe `word_id_lookup.json` as a flat map"* — half-refuted; `comprehensive_polish.md:82` already names both indexes, so the fix is one word in CLAUDE.md.

- **[A] Two items were already queued and simply unsized.** The 2026-08-20 run's conjugation-bullets pattern is P31 / `notes-duplicate-conjugation-block`, re-filed a fourth time and carrying `scope_estimate: null` since it was created — **now measured at 46 entries**, far smaller than the "whole creation batch" earlier reports implied, and therefore one bounded batch once Tooling 132 adds the detector class. The ください base-form split is genuinely new: **1,323 kana vs 237 kanji instances**, both resolving, filed as P66 / `inline-link-kudasai-baseform-split` at `needs-decision` because standardising without a skill-level ruling just lets the split regenerate.

- **[E] Two escalations for the curator.** A **location/space semantic tag** is missing from `VALID_SEMANTIC` — 00829 場所's sole-`general` is the correct answer for lack of an alternative, and the off-list tally independently shows `place` at 23 instances. And the **ください base form** needs a house ruling; the recommendation is the kana form, on 84.8% of existing usage plus the convention that the auxiliary ください is written in kana.

**Process note**: twenty-second consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the first to turn that discipline on **the wiki's own output**. Four of the five refutations were routine. The fifth was not: a page-level table that had quietly gone stale was still being cited in prose that drew the wrong conclusion from it, for several refreshes. Measuring before filing protects the backlog from bad inputs; it does nothing for a number that was right when written and decayed afterwards. The clause to add is that **a refresh should recompute the tables it reasons from, not only append to them** — the maintenance instructions at the foot of the page already say "recompute precision-by-dimension and by-source", and this is the first harvest to notice that recent refreshes had stopped doing the second half.

## [2026-08-23] maintenance | The tag hot spot has a name, both P50 backfills are refuted, and the 74-hour gap was a decision

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 12 unharvested observation lines, 39 open backlog items, candidates 151, frontier 07005, cross-model cursor 12913).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/intelligent-curie-6lqm3k`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 12 observations from three runs, [F] eight dictionary-wide measurements, [A] backlog-queue sync (two items added, four re-measured), [E] a cadence resolution on the metrics page, [E] two curator escalations. **No [H] refresh**: only 5 new `metrics-history.jsonl` lines since refresh 38, against the ≥10 threshold.

- **[F] P20's largest hot spot has a cause, found by changing axis.** The filing run proposed sweeping "the whole 2026-02-22 creation cohort" after six off-list tags turned up in 12743–12764. That cohort is **399 entries with 2 off-list survivors** — 2.0% even counting back the six the run fixed, against a **3.0% dictionary-wide rate**. Refuted, and the fourth ID-correlated false cohort in five weeks. But re-measuring the whole 920-entry population **by creation date** — an axis this item had never tried, after three refuted ID-band cohorts — produces the first cohort claim that survives: the **2026-01-25 run created 330 entries of which 191 (57.9%) carry off-list tags**, and **all 191 sit inside 08000–08999**, which is exactly the 201-entry hot spot refresh 38 located without being able to explain. So the hot spot is not a range that happens to be dirty; it is one authored batch with one ad-hoc vocabulary (`gardening` ×10, `loanword` ×10, `competition` ×8, `services` ×7). The operational handle does not change — ID-band concentration is still tighter overall, top 3 bands holding 50% against the top 5 creation days — but a sweep over 08000–08999 can now lean on the fact that the same handful of invented names recur throughout it. **Creation date is the diagnostic axis; ID band stays the operational one.**

- **[F] P50: both proposed backfills are refuted, and the item is smaller than either.** The 2026-08-21 polish run proposed keying a backfill on creation batch rather than the frontier. All 57 members *were* created in one eight-day window (2026-01-11→18), which looks like confirmation until the control runs over every sub-frontier entry: the six preceding creation days are **perfectly clean across 2,057 entries**, but inside the window the worst day is **3.8%** (17 of 445) against a 0.8% baseline. So creation-batch keying means reading 445 entries to find 17, while **03900–03999 holds 21 of the 57 in one hundred IDs (21%)** — block keying is **5× more efficient per entry read**. The real conclusion is that **neither keying is needed**: 57 entries is one bounded batch, already enumerated on the item as six contiguous runs plus seven singletons. Twenty-third consecutive harvest organised around measuring a premise before filing it, and the second in which the *control* rather than the measurement did the work.

- **[F] A new priority from two observations that did not know they were about the same thing.** A polish run found 00534 誰 carrying the off-list tag `interrogative` and guessed "likely a small family worth migrating together"; an accuracy-review run found 01484 なぜ tagged `pos: ["noun"]` beside its adverb synonyms and guessed "worth checking the whole interrogative family". Both guesses are right, and the family is worse than either supposed: **12 entries, all basic tier, carrying six semantic labels (`interrogative` ×2 off-list, `grammatical` ×4, `direction` ×2, `descriptive` ×2, `general` ×1, nothing at all ×2) and four POS labels.** Filed as **P67 at `needs-decision`**, not `open`: `grammatical` is the plurality and the only label fitting all twelve, but migrating without a ruling just re-scatters a closed paradigm. Escalated to the curator as a one-word decision.

- **[E] The 74-hour gap was a schedule change, and the series can tell.** Refresh 38 logged the gap as a `[pattern]` observation it could see but not explain. The timestamps on both sides settle it: **~3.0 h for twenty consecutive intervals** to 2026-08-17T00:34, then the gap, then **~12.0 h for four consecutive intervals** to 2026-08-23T00:30, this run included. An outage does not end in a new stable period. The consequence for the page is more permanent than refresh 38 feared — **throughput is structurally ~4× lower** (≈8 runs/day → 2), so every per-window rate has a discontinuity at 2026-08-17 and **per-run rates should be preferred in refresh headlines from here on**. Recorded as an interim note rather than a refresh.

- **[F] Two proposed detectors measured and refused.** (a) A `formality: formal` detector for everyday concrete nouns, prompted by 06994 ゴミ箱: as specified it flags **214 entries**, overwhelmingly Sino-Japanese words that are legitimately register-marked (果実, 昼食, 衣服, お手洗い, ご主人) — the predicate cannot separate "everyday object" from "formal word for an everyday object", which is what the tag is *for*. The high-precision cut inside it is **4 katakana entries**, filed as a follow-up instead; one of them, 03855 タイトル, turns out to carry `communication`/`food`/`leisure`/`tool`, a textbook example-topic contamination. (b) Whether the sole-`general` check "filters" 06985–06994: it does not — the queue holds 3,591 entries starting at 00005, and those ten are absent because **the observing run fixed all ten the same day**. A detector queue read after the fix cannot show what the fix removed.

- **[A] Four re-discoveries, and one measurement that was already on the page.** Duplicate conjugation blocks in verb notes (fifth filing — P31 at 46 entries plus P54 at 37); candidate-queue okurigana twins (third sighting — the fix has been in Tooling 41/43 since 2026-07-30); the zero-link block at 06995–07003, which is *above* the frontier and therefore the standing structural fact rather than a defect; and the note scorer's `・` blindness, which Tooling 20's structured-note credit has named explicitly since 2026-07-02. On the last of these the harvest's own contribution nearly repeated an existing measurement — P62 already recorded "130 of the top 500 (26%)" on 2026-08-15, and 24-of-the-top-100 is the same fact at a different depth. What was genuinely missing is the **base rate: 8.1% dictionary-wide**, which turns a bare percentage into a **3× enrichment** — and also bounds it, since 76 of the top 100 are not `・` entries.

- **[A] The 気持ち merge stops being a judgment call.** 01385 / 02485 has been on the consolidation list since 2026-06-10 and was re-filed a third time this week. Counting inbound references decides it: **01385 has 187, 02485 has 19**, so keep 01385 (also basic tier) and fold in 02485's third sense, "intention, sentiment". One wrinkle worth carrying into the merge — 01385's headword is `{気持ち|きもち}`, which swallows the okurigana into the ruby, so a P64 defect is sitting on the entry that 187 links point at, and the two fixes belong in one edit.

**Process note**: twenty-third consecutive measure-before-filing harvest, and the one where **changing the axis** did the work rather than changing the threshold. P20's ID-band cohort claims have been refuted three times; the same population measured by creation date resolved on the first attempt and then *explained* the ID-band hot spot rather than replacing it. The clause worth keeping is that a refuted cohort claim is evidence the grouping variable is wrong, not that there is no grouping — three refutations in a row were treated as "there is no cohort here" when they meant "you are asking by the wrong key."

## [2026-08-27] maintenance | Harvest 29 observations, refresh 39 — the furigana zero gets a cause, and the screening cache turns out to be missing half its own results

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 29 unharvested observation lines, 40 open backlog items, candidates 146, frontier 07023, cross-model cursor 13900).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/intelligent-curie-e79b5e`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 29 observations from three runs, [H] metrics refresh 39 (12 new lines since refresh 38, against the ≥10 threshold), [E] a wiki-wide link and anchor lint, [A] backlog-queue sync.

- **[F] The furigana screener's skip-cache is a strict subset of its own results, short by 12,016.** `reviews/screening/` holds **22,991** per-entry result files; `screening_status.json` records **10,975** entries; the difference is one-directional (0 in the index without a result), which is the signature of a lost end-of-run write — and `save_screening_status` is called exactly once, *after* the screening loop, while results are written inside it. Every timeout, crash or budget stop therefore discards the index for everything the pass completed. Two consequences: reported coverage has been low by 52% (~36% against ~75% on disk), and the next pass pays to re-screen work it already did. Filed as **Tooling 137**. The same discovery **retires Tooling 114's blocker**: that item concluded `--rescreen-before DATE` "cannot be implemented as stored" because the index carries one timestamp for the whole file — but **every one of the 22,991 result files carries `screened_at`**, so the feature falls out of the rebuild for free, and the pre/post-`trim_context` comparison can finally be computed whole-disk: **8.6% (1,947/22,519) before the fix against 5.7% (27/472) after** — real, and **1.5×** rather than the 3× a 171-entry sample suggested.

- **[F] The filing run's diagnosis was inverted, and the code says why.** A run reported that `--pass screening --range 13413 13899` "does not process entries in ID order… it iterates in directory-glob order", and named 13413–13549 and 13699–13899 as never screened. `resolve_entry_ids` builds the range with `for i in range(start, end+1)` — strictly ascending. What happened is the skip branch: 13413–13549 was already indexed (137 of 137) and was skipped **without printing a line**, so the run's first visible work was 13550. On disk it is the *opposite* of what the run recorded — 13413–13549 is the one complete block, and the two blocks the run believed it screened are the ones the lost index write dropped. Filed as **Tooling 138** (print skips), with the glob-order claim refuted.

- **[H] Refresh 39: `furigana` records a third consecutive zero — 0 of 81, 221 flags across three windows — and it now has a mechanism.** 69 of 1,974 flagged results are parse failures recorded as findings; 516 of 1,974 flagged concerns contain their own acquittal ("is correct", "will not flag"), the truncation family Tooling 119 describes. The instrument is substantially reporting on itself, which converts refresh 38's hedged retirement recommendation into a plain one. §14's de-mixing also reproduces a **seventh** time at its widest separation yet: headline `tags` **28.3%** splits into off-vocabulary migration **100%** [42/0] and individually-adjudicated flags **91.7%** [22/2] — a **97.0%** residual — against three policy-settled families at **1.2%** [2/165]. What is new is completeness: **165 of the 167 rejects are eight aggregated family lines**, so there is no tail left to discover and a runner-side filter would remove ~70% of `tags` volume and nothing else.

- **[F] Two proposed detectors measured, one refuted and one re-scoped.** (a) `existence` as a catch-all for action verbs: **112 entries lead with it**, but reading the 92 verbs dissolves the family — 死ぬ, 滅亡, 現存, 生息, 実在 and most of the rest are genuinely about existing; the crisp state-change cut ("to become/turn/grow") is **6 entries**. Refuted as a batch item. (b) sole-`education` as a catch-all: **461 entries**, and the gloss-keyword filter the run proposed cuts to 178 with poor precision (it keeps 課題, 復習, 黒板, 単位, 算数). The precise family is **the names of disciplines** — ~20 entries where 科学/医学/数学/哲学/心理学 are tagged by where a learner meets the word rather than by what it denotes, which is the same failure mode as the run's own 漢語/漢詩/紀行 migrations. Filed as **P68**.

- **[F] The "mid-07000 zero-link cohort" is P50, for the eighth time — but the re-measurement is worth having.** The proposed dictionary-wide detector returns **23,463 of 30,544 entries (77%)**, which is the standing *do not file a zero-link detector* ruling in Inline Link Integrity. Below the frontier the population is **57 — unchanged** at a frontier 38 IDs further on, so the 2026-08-21 "it is diverging" reading does not hold this window. New information: the residue has a **shape** — 3949–3969 is 21 consecutive bound-prefix entries and 6006–6014 the 9-entry anatomy cluster, so 57 entries is three sittings, not 57 decisions.

- **[E] Wiki link lint: 10 genuinely broken anchors, all repaired; 0 broken file targets.** The first pass reported 48, of which 38 were the linter's own fault — GitHub does not collapse runs of whitespace when slugging a heading, so every anchor containing a dropped `&`, `—` or `・` looked broken and was not. The real ten were rename drift (`#priority-37` and `#priority-11` written as bare prefixes; P62's anchor pointing at a heading that had been rewritten; a `23404` count baked into an anchor that now reads `23444`) plus two truncated tooling-item anchors and one this session created. **The lesson is about the tool, not the wiki**: an anchor linter with a slightly wrong slug function produces a 79% false-positive rate and would have had this session "fixing" 38 working links.

- **[A] One filing caught as a duplicate before it landed.** The zero-link finding was written up as a new priority P68 and a new queue item, then the link lint surfaced `topics/inline-link-integrity.md`'s reference to P50 — the same item, same 57 entries. Folded into P50 and the existing `zero-link-behind-frontier` queue row, and the later filings renumbered. The lint paid for itself in the same session.

**Process note**: twenty-fourth consecutive measure-before-filing harvest, and the first in which **reading the code beat re-measuring the data twice over** — the screening-cache gap, the skip-silence, the parse-failure flags and the refuted glob-order claim all came from four greps of `review_runner.py`, and each of them corrects a number some previous refresh reported in good faith. Worth carrying forward: when an observation describes what a *tool* did, the cheapest verification is the tool's source, not another run of it.

## [2026-08-31] maintenance | The notes priority list is ranking a heading name, and the loanword link family has a tell after all

**Session type**: Routine (`wiki` mode — selector: "wiki: highest scheduler debt among eligible modes"; signals: 35 unharvested observation lines, 41 open backlog items, candidates 152, frontier 07059, cross-model cursor 14639).

**Pre-flight**: §0a `list_pull_requests` returned **0 open PRs** — nothing to rescue, nothing to sweep. `list_branches` returned `main` only — zero orphan branches. Lock acquired on `claude/intelligent-curie-8sa17g`.

**Activities**: [F] harvest of 35 observations from five runs, [F] eleven dictionary-wide measurements, [C] a new family section on `topics/inline-link-integrity.md`, [C] a taxonomy section on `topics/schema-tag-reliability.md`, [A] backlog-queue sync (six items added, three rescoped, one marked duplicate), [E] three curator escalations. **No [H] refresh**: 7 new `metrics-history.jsonl` lines since refresh 39, against the ≥10 threshold.

- **[F] The worst-notes ranking is mostly measuring a heading name.** A polish run reported from seven priority-lane entries that low note scores "are very often caused by a missing *required section header*, not thin content." Dictionary-wide the claim holds far past what it could see: **5,748 of the 9,067 entries with a required-section template (63%) carry 250+ characters of notes and are missing a required section, while exactly 2 entries are short and missing one.** The cause is one line — `find_sections` matches `common patterns` on `[common pattern, patterns?:]`, and **2,499 of the 2,965 する-verb cases (84%) head the material `COMMON COLLOCATIONS`**, which the same function happily matches to the *optional* `collocations`. Required sections are 30 of 100 points and する-verbs declare exactly one, so those entries are capped at 70 for a synonym. That is enough to dominate the ranking: **76 of the worst-scoring 100 in `polishing/priority/notes.txt` are this class, against an 18.8% base rate — 4× enrichment.** Filed as Tooling 140 at `needs-decision`, because the one-line fix silently reshuffles thousands of entries in a list the polish lane plans from.

- **[F] Two runs found the same link family from opposite ends, and the tell is in neither of the places they looked.** One asked for a hard-coded denylist after ロック → `08116` "rock (music)" fired a **fifth** time in a lock context; the other found コーラスパート → `03106` "part-time work" and concluded that because the target makes no self-declaration, **no notes-scanning rule can see it** — correctly ruling out Tooling 134's refinement. Both are one family, *the target entry silently covers a different sense*, and its tell is in the base form: the word is a katakana loanword. Of `check_stale_noentry.py`'s **123 mechanical pairs, 22 have an entirely katakana base**, and reading all 22 gives **8 sense mismatches** — ロック ×4, パート, バー (scroll bar → drinking establishment), コマ (a comet's coma → "frame, panel"), フライ (a baseball fly → "deep-fried food") — against 14 correct. One predicate holds every instance both runs found plus three neither spotted, with no denylist and no notes scan. Filed as Tooling 143 and written up as Shape 3 on Inline Link Integrity. It works because of a fact about the dictionary rather than the checker: **same-spelling-different-sense is the normal case for loanwords and the exceptional case for everything else.**

- **[F] The "legacy January template" is the dictionary's majority style, and recent batches use it more.** Three observations across two polish runs inferred that the 2026-01 cohort's ALL-CAPS `FORMATION:` / `COLLOCATIONS:` headers "predate the house style" and that the frontier lane should budget for full notes rewrites. Measured: **21,073 of 30,564 entries (69%) use ALL-CAPS note headers**, and the rate runs *higher* on later creation days — 89.2% on 2026-03-28 and 89.0% on 2026-02-22 against 59.0% on 2026-01-18. `score_note_quality.py` awards its formatting bonus only to that style (Tooling 20), so converting them costs the entry points. The zero-inline-links half of the same observation stands, and so does the `・`-bullet half (P62); the template half does not, and the lane should not budget on it.

- **[A] A bounded batch confirmed, a scope corrected, and a duplicate pair caught.** Shop nouns tagged sole-`building` measured at **22** (the run guessed fifteen) out of 115 shop-shaped headwords — filed as P70, and unlike sole-`general` it has a definite destination tag, so it is a real `systemic-fix` item. `artifact-missing-target-id` still carried `scope_estimate: 12` from its filing against `check_artifacts.py`'s current **44**. And the katakana-in-braces population turns out to be filed **twice**: `furigana-katakana-base` (open, mechanical) and `katakana-hiragana-reading-wrappers` (blocked, "policy decision") describe the same **273 instances in 227 entries** — 270 of them katakana wrapped with a hiragana reading. A bounded fix with a blocked twin stays unfixed; merged, with the actual policy question (is any of the 273 a deliberate reading gloss?) carried onto the surviving item.

- **[F] Three premises refuted, two by two lines of code each.** (a) The product classifiers 製/系/用 "have no entries and recur constantly" — they have entries, written 〜製 (02001), 〜系 (28466), 〜用 (09842); what recurs is `check_duplicate.py`'s inability to match a bare suffix against its tilde form, measured at **99 tilde-form headwords and 42 product-classifier `noentry` markers** and filed as a fourth blind shape on Tooling 112. (b) A copied "many colour words are na-adjectives" claim — **two entries dictionary-wide** pair colour with na-adjective in one sentence, カラフル and 華やか, and both are correct; 00959 was a one-off, already fixed. (c) The deep furigana pass being "slow" in some diffuse way — `DEEP_MODELS` carries `gemini-2.5-pro` (`review_runner.py:52`) while screening uses flash, and `RATE_LIMIT_INTERVAL = 6.0` per model accounts for ~12 seconds of the reported 15 minutes per entry. Filed as Tooling 141: the operational problem is not that it is slow but that **a Routine which starts it has no way to stop it.**

- **[E] Three escalations.** The notes-scoring alias (one line versus a 2,499-entry rename, and the priority list is unreliable until it lands); a house-style conflict where `newentries.md` bans register parentheticals in glosses while **247 entries** carry them; and a one-word ruling on what `existence` denotes — **129 entries, 102 of them verbs**, generating reviewer flags that always reject because the offered replacements are worse.

**Process note**: twenty-fifth consecutive measure-before-filing harvest, and the second running in which reading the source settled a question faster than re-measuring the data. What is new is a case where **the observing run's own negative result was the useful part**: the コーラスパート run concluded that no rule could detect its family, and it was right about every place it looked — the target's notes, the readings, the spelling. The tell was in the base form's *script*, which is a property of neither entry but of how Japanese borrows. Worth carrying forward: when a run reports "there is no detectable signal here", the claim is usually true of the fields it examined, and the cheapest next move is to ask which field it never thought to look at.
